0.2.518
- POV 6.09.01 broke two repairs. Both are fixed, and the other 56 were
  checked rather than assumed.

  POV jumped 6.08.15 -> 6.09.01 (a minor release, ~45 files). Every patcher
  this add-on has for POV was run against a real 6.09.01 tree and a real
  6.08.15 tree, a FRESH copy per patcher, and the verdicts compared. Exactly
  two differ.

  1) THE INTERNAL-SCRAPER SHIM, and this one is subtler than it looks. POV
  changed two things in the same loop:

      -   for loader, module_name, is_pkg in __import__('pkgutil').iter_modules([source_path]):
      +   for loader, module_name, is_pkg in pkgutil.iter_modules([source_path]):
      ...
      -   append(('internal', loader.find_spec(module_name).loader.load_module(module_name).source, module_name))
      +   try: module_source = importlib.import_module('.' + module_name, package='debrids').source

  The first is cosmetic (pkgutil moved to a top-level import) and is what made
  the anchor stop matching. THE SECOND IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: loading now
  resolves through POV's own `debrids` package instead of through the folder a
  module was discovered in, so a scraper anywhere else is FOUND by the scan and
  then fails to import. Repairing only the scan line would have moved the
  failure two lines down and left the symptom identical -- the add-on's
  internal sources missing, exactly as before.

  So the repair now covers scan and load together: POV's own import is tried
  first and unchanged, and only when it raises does the module load from the
  file it was actually discovered in. POV's own scrapers take the first branch
  and behave exactly as upstream wrote them.

  2) THE DEBRID UNBOUND-NAME GUARD. POV renamed
  `indexers/real_debrid_api.py` -> `realdebrid_api.py`, and the guard reported
  `realdebrid=no_file` -- silently not applying, on the client that carries the
  very crash it exists to stop. It already knew how to follow a FOLDER move
  (debrids/ <-> indexers/); it now follows file renames too, first existing
  name wins, so one build serves both POV versions.

  NOT A REGRESSION, though it reads like one: `pov_alldebrid_status_fix` is
  `unmatched`, and that is correct. It repaired a `result['magnets'][0]`
  subscript that POV itself fixed in 6.08.15. The bug is gone, so the repair
  has nothing to do. Left alone deliberately rather than re-anchored onto code
  that is already right.

  UMBRELLA was checked the same way. It has moved 6.7.85 -> 6.7.86, and all
  seven Umbrella repairs apply cleanly on both -- zero regressions. That also
  retires a long-standing "never verified against current Umbrella" note: they
  are now measured against the shipped version and the newest upstream.

  tools/test_pov_internal_scraper_loads.py is new and does not trust a return
  value. It pulls the patched block out of the real sources.py, runs it, and
  requires a scraper in the legacy folder to arrive at POV's own `append` as a
  LOADED object -- which is the difference between the two POV versions and
  the thing `ensure_patched() == 'patched'` cannot see. Two mutants, both
  caught.

0.2.517
- The AI translation add-on moves to Gemini 3.8 Flash. Anyone already on 3.5,
  3.6 or 3.7 Flash is moved across once, automatically.

  Google published 3.8 Flash as the current regular-Flash model ("our most
  intelligent Flash model") and reclassified 3.7 as previous-generation. It sits
  on identical rate limits in every table Google publishes -- same tokens per
  minute, same tokens per day, same free requests per day -- so this is a
  drop-in swap exactly like the 3.5/3.6 -> 3.7 move before it, with no change
  to quota use or pacing.

  3.7 still answers, so nothing here was urgent. What is not fine is leaving a
  stored model id the picker no longer lists: the settings screen then shows a
  blank where the model should be. Hence the one-shot migration.

  IT RUNS ON BOTH CHANNELS. The build edition and the standalone (repo-channel)
  add-on do NOT run the same service file, and changelog text is shared between
  them -- so a promise like the one at the top of this entry can be true for one
  and a lie for the other. That is not hypothetical: the 3.7 migration in
  0.2.494 reached the build and never reached the standalone, and survived three
  releases before a user found it by reading the zip. tools/test_channels_agree
  .py now checks this claim against both services, and the 3.8 migration was
  written into both at the same time.

  Each bump gets its OWN marker (`_gemini_model_bump_v3`), never a reused one.
  The previous marker is already set on every device that took the previous
  bump, so reusing it would skip the migration for precisely the users who need
  it. The retired set covers 3.6 and 3.5 Flash too, so the migration is correct
  on its own rather than correct only because of the order two functions happen
  to run in.

  The free-tier daily counter keeps its row for 3.7 even though the picker has
  dropped it. A stored id outlives a dropdown -- a device that has not booted
  since the update, or one restored from a backup, still asks that table for a
  number, and a missing row would silently hand it the Flash-Lite figure of 500
  a day against a real free cap of 20.

  tools/test_gemini_model_table.py is new and holds four tables to one list of
  models: the picker, the daily-cap table, the requests-per-minute pacing and
  what the migrations write. It was written before the change and immediately
  caught two defects in it -- a migration still pointing at 3.7, which the
  picker no longer offers, and a marker count that only looked right.

0.2.516
- temperature and Top-P are no longer sent to the models that stopped
  accepting them.

  Google's Gemini API docs, verbatim: "temperature, top_p, and top_k are
  deprecated and ignored. In future model generations, supplying these
  parameters returns an HTTP 400 error. Remove these parameters from all
  requests."

  Ignored today, a hard 400 tomorrow -- and a 400 on a translation chunk is
  not a worse translation, it is no translation: the request raises and the
  chunk is lost. But the parameters are NOT simply removed, because on Gemini
  2.5 and 3.1 they still do real work, and the build's 1.0 / 0.95 defaults
  were measured on exactly those models. So the rule is per model: 3.5 and up
  send neither, 2.5 and 3.1 keep both.

  ONE PLACE DECIDES. gemini.sampling_params_supported() reads the generation
  out of the model id and both request builders consult it, so a caller may
  keep passing temperature= and top_p= without knowing the rule and a caller
  added later cannot forget it. That matters more than it sounds: the audio
  call SubSync makes for speech intervals lives in subsync.py with a hardcoded
  temperature=0.0, nowhere near the translation code, and is precisely the
  site an if-per-call-site would have missed. It is covered now by
  construction. (top_k is in Google's list too; this add-on has never sent it
  and has no setting for it, so there was nothing to gate.)

  A model id with no readable generation -- an alias like
  'gemini-flash-latest' -- also sends neither. That direction is chosen: such
  an alias resolves to whatever is newest, which is where the parameters are
  going away; omitting them is a valid request on every model that ever took
  them; and guessing the other way costs a 400.

  THE TWO SLIDERS ARE GONE FROM THE SETTINGS SCREEN on a model that ignores
  them -- a control that changes nothing is worse than no control. They come
  back on 2.5 and 3.1 with the user's own numbers intact, because the settings
  are still stored and still read; only the request changes. The default model
  is 3.5 Flash Lite, so out of the box neither slider shows.

  The visibility list in settings.xml is a WHITELIST of the models that still
  honour the parameters, not a blacklist of the ones that do not: add a newer
  model to the picker and forget the list, and the slider correctly stays
  hidden. tools/test_gemini_sampling_params.py fails if the list and the code
  ever disagree, builds real payloads through a stubbed requests to prove what
  each model's request actually carries, and catches eight mutants of the rule.

0.2.515
- "חיבור שירותים" opened an empty screen, and then POV's own English menu.

  The tile did not go to POV's services screen. It opened a POV "shortcut
  folder" -- a row in POV's navigator.db holding exactly one item,
  {'mode': 'myservices'} -- and the row was gone. The log says it plainly:

      0.03s route=0.03s mode=navigator.build_shortcut_folder_list
                        &name=[B]חיבור שירותים[/B]
      Control 50 in window 10025 has been asked to focus, but it can't
      Unable to find plugin / GetDirectory - Error getting plugin://

  0.03 seconds is POV returning an empty directory (get_shortcut_folder_contents
  finds no row -> `except: return []`). Kodi then walks UP out of the empty
  folder, and the parent of a plugin path is the plugin ROOT -- POV's stock
  English menu. That root menu is the screenshot people send, which is why the
  report reads as "POV opens instead of the services screen"; one Back further
  is the bare `plugin://` in those two errors.

  Not the reader. Seconds earlier in the same log, every OTHER shortcut folder
  loaded normally -- both FENtastic personal areas, both networks rows -- so
  pov_navigator_read_patcher is doing its job. It is this one row, and it is
  the only custom row in the shipped navigator.db that no reseeder of ours
  restores: the networks and genre rows have one, the personal areas are
  rewritten on every startup. So it is the only one that stays gone.

  THE FOLDER WAS NEVER NEEDED. It holds a single item, so the indirection
  bought one keypress and one thing to lose. Arctic Fuse 3 has always called
  mode=myservices directly and has never had this report -- and myservices is
  a DIALOG, not a directory, so ActivateWindow was the wrong verb for it from
  the start. The tile now calls it directly on every skin. No database row in
  the path, no window left empty, nothing to walk up out of. Name, icon and
  position stay exactly as each user has them.

  The per-skin favourites SEEDS are repaired too, not just the user's own file.
  The wizard copies a seed OVER favourites.xml on every skin switch, so a fix
  that lives only in the user's copy comes back broken the first time somebody
  changes skin. The same pass also carries the older "send log" action fix into
  the seeds, which had the same hole.

  tools/test_connect_services_tile.py replays it: the old tile is rewritten and
  only that one element changes, a shortcut folder the USER added themselves is
  left alone, both seeds are repaired, a torn seed is never written back, and
  five mutants of the repair are each caught.

0.2.514
- The health report now covers the Connect Services window too, which was the
  most visible thing it did not cover.

  Three patchers BUILD their marker from an integer constant -- '# ..._v{0}'
  .format(INJECT_VERSION) -- so the live name exists nowhere in the source and
  the highest literal in the file is a RETIRED one. 0.2.513 refused to guess
  and reported those as unmeasurable. One of the three is the patcher that
  builds Connect Services.

  The name is not unknowable: the family comes from the retired literals and
  the number from the constant. Verified rather than assumed -- running the
  patcher against a real POV 6.08.14 writes exactly the name this
  reconstruction produces.

  Rebuilt is not proven, so such a marker starts UNVERIFIED. If the host is
  seen carrying it, the guess was right and it is alarmable from then on like
  any other. If it is never found it stays unverified and never raises: "the
  repair is gone" and "I guessed the name wrong" look identical from outside,
  and only one of them is worth an alarm.

  45 of 51 POV markers now verified present on a real 6.08.14, up from 44 with
  Connect Services outside the count entirely. The remaining six are each
  explained and none is an alarm.

0.2.513
- A repair that stops applying now says so, on the next boot, by name.

  Every repair this add-on makes to another add-on is a text edit gated on a
  versioned marker. When the other add-on refactors, the anchor stops matching
  and the repair stops applying. That is expected and it is handled -- the
  patchers refuse rather than guess. What was NOT handled is that it happened
  in complete silence.

  The mechanism, exactly: the startup pass runs `step()` and DISCARDS the
  return value, and all 123 step functions return None anyway. A no-longer-
  matching anchor is not an exception, so it never reaches the WARNING branch
  either. POV 6.08.14 broke five repairs this way and the first anyone knew was
  a user reporting a symptom days later. One of the five had been dead even
  longer, from a duplicate `def` in our own file.

  So a new last step asks the host add-ons what they actually contain, rather
  than trusting what anything returned -- a patcher that reported success and
  whose write silently failed would still have read green. Marker present, the
  repair is applied; absent, it is not.

  THE HARD PART IS SILENCE, NOT DETECTION. Most devices do not have most hosts,
  and a repair for a skin nobody installed is absent forever; reporting that
  every boot trains everyone to ignore the report. So absence is only news
  against history: a marker seen applied before and missing now is LAPSED and
  is named at WARNING. Never once seen applied is quiet. A host that was
  patched and has since been UNINSTALLED is quiet too.

  Two findings from running it against the real tree rather than reasoning
  about it. A patcher keeps its own superseded markers so it can strip its
  previous work, and looking for those reported eleven false absences from one
  module -- so each marker family is collapsed to the one live version. And
  three patchers BUILD their live marker from an integer constant, so it exists
  nowhere in the source and the highest literal is a retired one; those are
  reported as unmeasurable and named, rather than as permanently broken.

  The full table is written to addon_data/.../patcher_health.txt every boot.
  The WARNING lines are always logged, so any log a user uploads now carries
  the diagnosis instead of just the symptom. The on-screen popup appears only
  where the log level is DEBUG -- a viewer cannot act on this, and telling them
  the build is broken would be alarming and useless.

  Measured, not assumed: 0.15s per boot, reading 1.1 MB of host text, against a
  pass that already runs for tens of seconds. It covers 68 patchers across 10
  host add-ons, and it never raises into the boot -- every failure path returns
  less information instead.

0.2.512
- Internal sources are found again. POV 6.08.14 did not restructure the folder
  it loads internal scrapers from, it RENAMED it, with a byte-identical file
  list, and moved its own pointer with it:

      6.08.13   resources/lib/scrapers/   kodi_utils.scrapers_path
      6.08.14   resources/lib/debrids/    kodi_utils.internal_path

  POV scans exactly ONE folder, so after 6.08.14 any scraper a third-party
  add-on had installed there was never looked at -- "it shows the external
  sources only" in the field. Worse, the installer writes its module as the
  FIRST of several edits, so the missing folder raised ENOENT and aborted
  everything after it, including the settings edit that registers the name.
  That is why the feature vanished outright instead of degrading.

  Two edits, and both are needed. The old folder is re-created with its
  __init__.py, so the installer's write succeeds. And the one line in
  modules/sources.py that reads the folder is taught to scan BOTH --
  pkgutil.iter_modules already takes a list of directories. POV's own folder
  goes FIRST, so a stale copy of one of POV's own modules left behind in the
  legacy folder can never shadow it; iter_modules dedupes by module name and
  the first directory wins. Verified by running POV's own patched line through
  pkgutil against both real trees, not by reading the text.

  An earlier attempt mirrored files between the folders instead. It only works
  if our pass happens to run after the other add-on's service that boot, and
  that add-on re-checks on its own timer, so a device could sit for an hour
  with the file written and never copied. Teaching POV to scan both removes the
  race: whenever the file appears, the next scrape sees it. It also stops us
  writing inside somebody else's add-on directory.

- And a third edit, which is what makes the other two safe rather than
  dangerous. Once a module POV does not know about is loaded, its rows flow
  into POV's sorter, and modules/sources.py does this for every row of every
  result set:

      def get_provider_rank(self, account_type):
          return self.source.provider_sort_ranks[account_type] or 11

  A bare subscript. A provider name that is not in that dict raises KeyError
  out of sort_results, which process() -> get_sources() -> source_select()
  calls unguarded, so the WHOLE list dies -- POV's own sources included.
  Reproduced against POV's own function: `rd_cloud` ranks 2, an unregistered
  name raises. The fix is one token, `.get(account_type)`, and it changes
  nothing for a name that is registered, because POV already treats a falsy
  rank as 11 and .get returns None for a miss. It also protects POV from every
  future provider it does not know, not just this one.

  Both edits refuse rather than guess: two copies of either line, a shape that
  does not match, or a result that would not compile leaves POV byte-for-byte
  as it was, and says so in the log.

0.2.511
- AllDebrid plays again. POV 6.08.14 shipped a one-line regression that broke
  playback for every AllDebrid user: indexers/alldebrid_api.py torrent_info()
  went from `result['magnets']` to `result['magnets'][0]`. Queried with an id
  that endpoint returns a single object, so indexing it with [0] raises
  KeyError(0) -- which is exactly what two field logs show, dozens of times
  each, once per source tried:

      >> resolve_external_sources exception <<: 0
         {'debrid': 'alldebrid', 'cache_provider': 'Unchecked alldebrid', ...}

  It fails inside parse_magnet_pack, before any file is chosen, so POV moves to
  the next source and fails the same way. To a user that is "it goes through
  every source and plays nothing", and on a title whose sources are all
  external it looks like "no results" instead.

  The identical line appears twice in that file and only one is wrong --
  create_transfer() calls an endpoint that really does return a list and
  carried [0] in 6.08.13 too. The patch matches the whole torrent_info body so
  it cannot land on the other one, and it does not simply revert: it uses what
  the API returned and indexes only if it is a list, so it is right under
  either response shape. Verified by executing POV's own method before and
  after against a single object, a list, and an empty list.

- Our own account check was importing a module POV moved. `debrids.alldebrid_api`
  became `indexers.alldebrid_api` in 6.08.14, and the log said so plainly --
  "AllDebrid status lookup failed: No module named 'debrids.alldebrid_api'" --
  on every account read, on both devices. The file-path patchers were taught
  this rename in 0.2.510 and this runtime import was missed. It now tries the
  new package first and falls back.

0.2.510
- POV 6.08.14 compatibility. POV restructured itself and several of our repairs
  stopped applying -- silently, with no error, reporting "no file" as if that
  were healthy. The defects they prevent did NOT go away; they moved with the
  files.

  What moved: the debrid API clients went from resources/lib/debrids/ to
  resources/lib/indexers/, and resources/lib/scrapers/ was renamed to
  resources/lib/debrids/. Four repairs were looking in the old place and one
  more had its anchor shifted by a refactor.

  Re-pointed, and the folder is now decided by POV's own import line rather
  than by which file happens to exist -- both folders ship in 6.08.14, so
  "first one found" would rewrite a dead copy and report success while the file
  POV actually imports went untouched. Verified on a deliberately mixed tree.

  Two of the five guard the PLAY path specifically: one prevents POV's debrid
  error handlers from crashing in a way that deletes the real error, the other
  keeps TorBox links legal. Both had lapsed.

- Every repair we ship was run against both POV versions, file by file. Seven
  behaved differently on 6.08.14; five are fixed here, one is a difference by
  design, one was an artefact of the test harness. After all repairs are
  applied to a real 6.08.14 tree, POV still compiles with zero errors, and no
  repair injects a reference to anything 6.08.14 removed.

- A third-party add-on that installs a scraper into POV was writing to the
  folder that got renamed and failing with "no such file or directory". The
  folder is re-created so its installer can complete. Whether that alone is
  enough is not established -- POV gates which scrapers it loads by a fixed
  list, so the rest depends on that add-on's own author. Said plainly here
  rather than claimed as fixed.

0.2.509
- Measurement only. Nothing about how the build behaves changes in this
  version; one instrument gets more honest.

- The timing line now reads `1.50s route=0.30s exit=1.20s` instead of one
  number. v1 and v2 timed `with self: return routing(sys)`, and POV's
  Router.__exit__ sits inside that `with` -- it calls get_property(), which is
  a Kodi GUI call that takes the graphics lock. So a call that finished its POV
  work in a second and then waited on a busy GUI thread was reported as a slow
  POV call.

  That is not academic: it sent a diagnosis into a ditch. A field log was read
  as database lock contention between concurrent POV calls, a patch to POV's
  cache layer was written, tested and nearly shipped. An independent
  re-analysis then showed every slow call had already blown past its own
  baseline by 1.25-4.11s WHILE RUNNING ALONE, and that six of eight finished
  within 1-56 ms of a rival call's end -- the signature of waiting on a
  handoff, not on a database. The patch was deleted unreleased. A second review
  found it would also have raised inside POV's own cache constructor, turning a
  slow category into one that does not load at all.

- Recorded, not acted on: three of our modules justify themselves with "POV
  ships reuselanguageinvoker=true, so concurrent invocations share ONE Python
  interpreter." Measured on a device with reuse on: 11 concurrent pairs, none
  sharing an invoker, and all 8 second-arrivals started cold. Kodi declines
  reuse for a concurrent caller and builds a fresh sub-interpreter. The crash
  those modules address is real; the mechanism written above it is not what
  happens. Nothing was loosened on the strength of that -- the headers now
  carry the correction so the next person re-derives it.

- The upgrade harness could not see a patcher whose version marker was spelled
  in a shape its regex did not know, so the module was absent from the report
  rather than listed as unproven. It now requires any runnable patcher with a
  versioned constant to be pinned.

0.2.508
- The poster view's logo comes back on Estuary, and it was never FENtastic's
  bug. 0.2.507's write-up credited a clearlogo error in a field log to
  fentastic_clearlogo_var_patcher; a fact-check caught that the module only
  ever touches skin.fentastic and the device was running Estuary. Scanning the
  shipped build instead of assuming: skin.estuary/xml/Variables.xml has the
  same two unclosed brackets in the same ClearArtLogo variable -- and unlike
  FENtastic's copy, whose only consumer is commented out, Estuary's is live at
  View_51_Poster.xml:256. Both conditions unparseable means both are false,
  the variable resolves to nothing, and that texture has been drawing nothing
  in the default poster view on every Estuary device. The patcher now carries
  the skin per site and repairs all four; a device has one of the two skins, so
  the other reporting no_skin is the healthy answer. Exactly two lines and two
  bytes change in the file, and the test proves it by diff.

- The fast-navigation switch needs ONE restart, not two, and the release that
  shipped it said two. The first field log with it on shows POV noticing the
  change, offering a profile reload, and applying it in that same session --
  one dialog, once. Corrected in the help text (both languages), the README
  and the line service.py logs.

- What the switch actually bought, now measured rather than predicted. The
  mods= counter added in 0.2.507 reports len(sys.modules) either side of each
  call, and it settles it: the same route ran 7.22s at mods=81->355 and 1.08s
  at mods=355->355, and a local menu is 0.01s reused. Like-for-like on
  tmdb_tv_networks the floor went 1.72s -> 1.09s. That is about 0.6s a press,
  NOT the whole 1.75s that release predicted -- the rest is TMDb and Trakt
  network, which no interpreter setting can touch. Recorded in the guard's
  header so the next person reads the measurement, not the forecast.

v0.2.507
- POV's menus have felt heavy since 14 August, and this release says why and
  hands the choice over. The cause is ours: pov_language_invoker_guard turns
  POV's reuse-language-invoker flag off, because concurrent POV invocations
  sharing one Python interpreter is a native crash that closes the whole
  application. That fix is real and it is staying on by default. What nobody
  had was its price.
  Now measured, from a reporter's device on 0.2.506 running Estuary, using the
  per-navigation timing line 0.2.506 itself shipped. Sixteen navigations, and
  five unrelated route families -- tmdb_tv_networks, tmdb_movies_popular,
  trakt_tv_trending, tmdb_tv_premieres and tmdb_movies_latest_releases -- all
  landing between 1.72s and 1.89s. That is a spread of 0.17s across different
  modules talking to different companies' servers; tmdb_movies_popular and
  trakt_tv_trending both took 1.77s, to the hundredth. Repeat visits do not move it: FOX 2.46s then 1.78s,
  Amazon 2.24s then 1.73s, so roughly 0.6s of a first visit is cacheable
  network and the floor beneath it is not.
  A route-independent, cache-immune floor is a fixed per-invocation cost, and
  POV pays it somewhere unusually visible: every route defers its weight into
  the call itself. Counted over top-level imports, POV's entry.py loads 4 of
  its own modules and 10 stdlib ones before the call begins -- and reaching
  menus.tvshows then adds twenty more of its own, 210KB, and twenty-seven more
  stdlib packages, requests and concurrent.futures and unicodedata among them.
  A reused interpreter pays that once per session. A fresh one pays it per
  press.
- So there is now a switch: "POV: fast navigation", in the add-on's settings
  under Advanced, at Expert level. Off out of the box, which is exactly what
  every device has been doing since August.
  Turning it on gives POV its reused interpreter back -- the first press after
  each Kodi start still pays the imports, everything after it does not -- and
  accepts the crash with it. It takes two Kodi restarts to activate: one for
  the add-on to write POV's flag, one for Kodi to read it.
- The idea this release deliberately did NOT ship, recorded so it is not tried
  again: the crash was reported on Arctic Fuse 3, so keeping the guard on only
  for that skin and giving everyone else their speed looks obvious. The same
  log kills it. That device is on Estuary, with one person pressing buttons,
  and it still produced two overlapping POV invocations -- one route stuck at
  7.31s while the next press started underneath it. Concurrency is not a
  property of a skin; it is a property of a call being slow enough for the
  next one to land inside it, which turning reuse off makes more likely, not
  less.
- The timing line itself gained a field, so the next log settles this instead
  of arguing about it. It now reads `1.75s mods=64->431 ?action=...`: the two
  numbers are how many Python modules were loaded before and after the call.
  The first says whether the interpreter arrived cold or warm at a glance, and
  the difference is what that route had to load for itself. On the reading
  above it should be hundreds today and zero with the switch on -- same route,
  same work, different starting point. If it is not, the diagnosis is wrong and
  the log will say so.
- The test that pinned the old behaviour was replaced rather than deleted. It
  used to assert that the guard could never re-enable the flag; it now asserts
  the property that still protects people, which is that OFF is the
  destination of every failure -- no kodi_utils, a settings store that throws,
  a setting nobody has ever touched -- and that the only route to ON is an
  explicit true. Unlike the regex it replaces, it is executed.

v0.2.506
- Umbrella and its scraper module now arrive on every device instead of
  waiting behind a menu entry nobody had to find. Half the build already
  assumed they were there: the home screen carries Umbrella tiles, the search
  wiring has an Umbrella branch, the account manager pushes debrid accounts
  into it, and a dozen small repairs exist for no other reason than to make it
  speak Hebrew. On a device without it every one of those quietly did nothing,
  and the screen looked identical either way -- which is exactly why it should
  not have been left to chance. It installs once, in the background, and its
  own repositories come with it, so from that moment the developers publish
  its updates rather than us. A tile you remove stays removed.
- An update no longer throws away settings you changed that evening. The
  wizard closed Kodi by killing it, which skips the save Kodi does on a normal
  shutdown -- correct after a full build install, where a save would overwrite
  the guisettings.xml that was just extracted, and wrong after a quick update,
  where nothing of the kind was written. Audio passthrough turning itself back
  off is what made it visible, because that is a setting people set once and
  notice immediately, but every setting was affected. The quick update now
  asks Kodi to close properly and falls back to the kill only if that does not
  take; the build install and the skin switch keep the kill they need.
- Pressing a tile in the second and a half after an update no longer drops you
  into an empty video screen. To make a fix take effect the same evening the
  build switches the video add-on off and on again, and it waits for the home
  screen to settle first -- but if the screen never settled it used to go
  ahead anyway. A log showed exactly what that costs: three minutes of
  browsing without a pause, the switch fired at the deadline, and 1.2 seconds
  later a press on the Disney+ tile landed in the bare Videos list because
  Kodi could not resolve the add-on it points at. Three minutes of continuous
  activity means somebody is watching the screen, which makes it the worst
  possible moment. It now gives up instead, records that the switch is still
  owed, and does it at the next start.
- Sources no longer arrive late, or come back empty when they exist. Two
  reports on the same evening -- "the results take ages again" and "no sources
  for a title that definitely has them" -- and two logs that say the same
  thing in different words. To make a fix take effect the same evening the
  build switches the video add-on off and on again, and it waits for the home
  screen to be idle first. But every window that add-on puts up while it works
  -- the search progress, the list of sources -- FLOATS over the home screen.
  Start a title from a home-screen row and the home screen is still there
  underneath, nothing is playing, no row is loading, and somebody reading a
  list of sources is not pressing buttons. Every test for "idle" said yes
  while the user was mid-search with a dialog in front of their face. One log
  shows the switch landing on an open source list and then pulling the focus
  back to the home screen; the other shows it killing a search seven seconds
  in -- and that is the search reported as "no results". A dialog on screen is
  now never a moment to do this, checked again in the last second before it
  happens.
- Add-ons that stopped updating themselves update again. Kodi keeps a table of
  add-ons excluded from automatic updates, and it writes rows into it by
  itself: an add-on whose ORIGIN repository stops answering has no version to
  be compared against, so it is marked "not the latest" and excluded for good.
  This build has such a repository -- a field log catches it returning 404 --
  and five of the add-ons registered to it are ones the build ships. The
  excluded add-on still appears in the manual "available updates" list, which
  is why updating by hand worked and made the fault look random. The build now
  clears those automatic exclusions for the add-ons it ships, never touches
  one somebody set by hand or a version somebody installed from a zip on
  purpose, and names every one it finds in the log. It also checks the
  "install updates automatically" setting itself, puts it back once if it has
  drifted, and says so at every start if it has not.
- A debrid service that refuses your account now says why, instead of turning
  into "no sources". AllDebrid, TorBox and Premiumize all answer normally --
  HTTP 200 -- and put the refusal in the body, and the video add-on only
  records a problem when the status code is bad, so the reason the provider
  spelled out in words was dropped one line after it arrived.
- And Premiumize had a second, worse version of it, which explains a report
  that read as nonsense: "the count of sources found goes up while it
  searches, and then it says no results and never shows the list -- only on
  Premiumize". The add-on's Premiumize cache check reads one field of the
  reply without checking it is there. A refusal has no such field, so that
  line fails, and the caller catches every error and returns an empty list of
  cached sources. Every source found is then marked uncached and filtered
  away: the counters really did climb, and the list really was empty. It was
  never visible because the reason was destroyed two steps before anyone could
  read it. It now says so, on screen and in the log -- and the refusal itself
  is the account's, not something this build can fix for you.
- And a third cause of it, which is the one that was doing most of the damage.
  Once the scrapers have found their torrents, POV asks the debrid service
  which of them it already holds -- and it builds the list it shows you
  entirely out of the answers that came back in time. One debrid configured
  means one answer, so a debrid that is a second late leaves nothing to build
  from and the whole search is discarded unread: three hundred sources found,
  "no results" on screen. Worse, a check that FAILED -- timed out, refused,
  answered nonsense -- is recorded as a definite "none of these are cached",
  and the default setting then deletes every one of those from the list. Both
  roads end at an empty screen after the counters climbed.
  Why Premiumize and not TorBox on the same build: one number, "Scraper/Debrid
  Timeout", is both how long POV waits for the answer and how long the
  Premiumize request itself is allowed to take, so a slow request always
  finishes at or after the moment POV stopped waiting -- and raising the
  number raises both halves equally, which is why raising it in the last
  update helped some people and not others. TorBox sends all of its torrents in
  one compact request; Premiumize sends each one as a separate field, hundreds
  of them on a popular title. And it works on the second try because POV
  remembers the answers, so a title you just searched skips the request
  entirely.
  Now: a debrid that does not answer no longer erases anything. Its sources
  are kept and marked "not checked" -- which is exactly what POV already does
  for Real-Debrid and AllDebrid every time -- so you get the list, with the
  unverified ones at the bottom. A debrid that DOES answer is treated exactly
  as before, down to the byte; an honest "nothing cached here" is still
  believed and still hidden.
- Every category POV opens now records how long it took. A report of a spinner
  on each press came with a log that contains no POV timing at all, so the
  only evidence was the gaps between unrelated errors -- reading time and
  loading time added together, and impossible to separate. This does not make
  anything faster and must not be mistaken for having done so: it puts a
  number and a route on each press, so the next report of a slow build can be
  answered instead of guessed at.
- The logo in the player's on-screen display now draws. Under it, a pair of
  images: the title's own logo, and the studio logo when the title has none.
  Each is shown by a condition missing one closing bracket, so Kodi cannot
  parse either, treats both as false, and draws NEITHER -- on every device, on
  every title, since the day it was written. One of the two is meant to be
  showing at all times. Also repaired, and honestly worth less: the same
  mistake in the skin's poster-view logo variable, which is the line the log
  actually complains about. That one changes nothing on screen, because the
  block that would draw it is commented out in the skin -- somebody clearly
  tried it, saw nothing (for this exact reason), and gave up. Un-commenting it
  is a visible change nobody asked for, so it is left alone. Nineteen other
  conditions in the same skin have the same defect and are deliberately not
  touched: most are compound, where the bracket could close in two places that
  mean different things, and repairing one blind would make something appear,
  or something happen, that no user of this build has ever seen.
- And a line of red that a device without Umbrella collected every sixty
  seconds, forever: asking Kodi whether an add-on is installed by trying to
  open it makes Kodi write "Unknown addon id" at error level before it
  answers, and the background job that keeps Umbrella's accounts in step with
  POV's asked exactly that way, once a minute, for as long as Kodi was up.

v0.2.505
- Right after an update, the POV home tiles could come back empty and the
  error log could fill with red -- and it was this build doing it. When a fix
  lands, the video add-on is holding the old code in memory, so we switch it
  off and on again to make it pick the fix up the same evening. For the second
  and a half it is off, Kodi does not know it. On a cold start the home screen
  is still loading its rows in exactly those seconds, so the rows asked an
  add-on that was not there, failed, and -- because it is the switching OFF
  that triggers the rebuild -- nothing rebuilt them afterwards. They stayed
  empty for the session. Clearing the cache appeared to fix it; so did moving
  to the 64-bit build. Neither fixed anything: both just shifted the timing.
  Now the switch waits for the home screen to be genuinely settled, and if the
  rows still come back empty it redraws them once. None of the waiting happens
  where anyone can feel it, and startup is in fact a little faster than before.
- The error screen also filled with "Unknown addon id" for Umbrella and its
  scraper module on devices that simply do not have them. Asking "is this
  installed?" the obvious way makes Kodi write that line before it answers, so
  a device without them collected a handful of red on every start for a
  question whose answer was "no, and that is fine" -- and the one line that
  mattered scrolled past unnoticed. It reads the disk now and says nothing.
- A debrid service that refuses your account now says so, on screen, instead of
  failing every source in silence. AllDebrid reports that kind of refusal
  inside a normal reply rather than as an error, so three separate layers threw
  it away and the log ended up with no reason at all. If the key is not valid,
  the access is blocked, the account is suspended or it is not premium, you get
  told which, in Hebrew. Nothing is said for a timeout or a bad connection --
  only for an answer the service actually gave.
- Subtitle sync learns from you again. While one of our subtitles is playing,
  the amount you shift it by hand is meant to be the strongest possible
  evidence about whether it is in sync -- it is the only thing that resolves
  files no automatic method can. That measurement was reading zero every time,
  and the watch never started at all, because of three missing lines that each
  failed quietly into a handler that swallowed them. It has never once
  reported. It does now.

v0.2.504
- POV's source list appears the moment the sources are ready again. Someone
  reported it taking five to ten seconds to draw after the search had clearly
  finished, where it used to be about one, and the log timed it: two seconds
  with three Hebrew subtitles available for the title, four with six, ten with
  seventeen. The Hebrew-match badge on each row was comparing every source
  against every available subtitle name, and re-deriving both names from
  scratch every single time -- so seventeen names were taken apart seventy
  times each before one row could be drawn. Each name is now read once, the
  built-in-Hebrew check costs nothing at all, and no comparison runs that
  cannot change the answer: about twenty-five times less work, with every
  badge coming out identical. Umbrella's source list gets the same.
- And the reason the POV source screen slowed down out of nowhere, with
  nothing having changed: the subtitle lists that badge walks were never
  trimmed, so they grew a little with every title watched until the delay
  became visible. They are capped now, far above anything a real title has, so
  it cannot creep back. Three leftover diagnostics that wrote whole lists into
  the log on every play and every source screen are gone with it.

v0.2.503
- Kan 11 items in Idan Plus play again. Nothing from that channel would start:
  YouTube answered "This video is unavailable" for every one of them, five
  different player clients in a row, because the id it was being asked for was
  the literal word "watch". Idan Plus reads the video id out of the address
  path, and in the ordinary youtube.com/watch?v= form the id is not in the
  path -- it is in the part that gets cut off. The add-on builds that address
  itself, from a bare id Kan hands it, and then fails to read back what it just
  wrote. So this was never a Kan change and never a regression: those items
  have never played. One line now reads the id from the right place, and only
  where what was extracted cannot be an id at all, so every link the add-on
  already opened correctly opens exactly as before. If Idan Plus fixes this on
  its side, the fix simply stops applying and changes nothing.

v0.2.502
- AllDebrid sources that refused to play now say why. A report of "no results"
  turned out not to be a no-results condition at all: 70 sources were found,
  and all 38 of the AllDebrid ones failed to start with the same internal
  error. POV's own error handler was crashing on a variable it never set, and
  that crash was replacing the message AllDebrid had actually sent -- so the
  reason, whatever it was, never reached the log. The handler no longer
  crashes, and for AllDebrid and Real-Debrid the provider's real message now
  comes through, which is what makes the problem diagnosable instead of just
  visible. This does not by itself make a refused account work; it makes the
  refusal readable.

v0.2.501
- Umbrella's "Shows in Progress" and "Episodes in Progress" fill themselves in
  again, with no manual refresh in either one. Update 599 fixed the shows list
  by clearing the sync cursor, but it cleared two more keys alongside it, and
  those two were not cursors -- they are the signal Umbrella reads to decide
  whether there is new watched activity worth syncing. Zeroing them meant
  "nothing new, serve the cache" forever, so the episodes list, which had
  always been right, started needing a refresh too. Only the fetch cursor is
  cleared now, and the devices that took the previous release are repaired on
  their next start.
- And the root cause underneath both: Umbrella's MDBList sync wrote "synced up
  to now" even when the fetch had failed. The cursor only ever moves forward,
  so anything that fell inside a failed request was skipped permanently. The
  cursor now advances only when the fetch really returned a page of results --
  a failed request, an unreadable response, or an error envelope all leave it
  where it was, so the same window is asked for again next time.

v0.2.500
- The update itself no longer rewrites the whole build to change a handful of
  files. Every quick update was laying down all 1,969 files, 1,330 of them
  belonging to the skin that was on screen at that moment -- and on some
  devices Kodi force-closed partway through, over and over, before the update
  could finish. It now writes only the files that actually differ from what is
  already on the box: five, on a typical update, instead of 1,969. A device
  that is several updates behind still receives everything it is missing --
  the comparison is against that device's own files, so the further behind it
  is, the more it gets.
- POV's "Reuse Language Invoker SETTING/XML mismatch" message no longer
  appears. POV updates itself and its update restores a setting we deliberately
  turn off, so POV noticed the disagreement and asked to reload the profile.
  We were already repairing it -- nine seconds too late, every time. The repair
  now runs first, before POV looks. Pressing OK on that message was always
  harmless.

v0.2.499
- The player bar now hides itself on every skin that can do it, not only on
  FENtastic. The setting that closes it after a few idle seconds was being
  turned on by a check that named one skin and returned for all the others, so
  anyone on Nox had the bar stay up until they pressed Back. It is detected
  from the skin's own files now, so a skin that has the feature gets it, and if
  you turn it back off yourself it stays off.
- Umbrella's "continue watching" for SERIES no longer gets stuck several
  episodes behind while the EPISODES list is correct. The two read different
  things: episodes come from MDBList live, series are rebuilt from a table on
  the device. That table was losing rows -- Umbrella moves its sync marker
  forward to the current time even when a request failed or came back empty,
  and the marker never moves back, so whatever was in that window was skipped
  for good. The sync now reads thirty days further back so a missed window is
  picked up instead of lost, and a table that has already lost rows is refilled
  once, by itself -- the same thing Umbrella's own "Force MDBList Sync" button
  does, without needing to know the button exists.

v0.2.498
- If you installed the AI subtitles add-on on its own, from the repository,
  rather than as part of the build: the automatic move to Gemini 3.7 Flash
  announced in 0.2.494 never reached you. The announcement was shared between
  both, the change itself was not. Anyone still on 3.5 or 3.6 Flash is moved
  across once, now, and any other model you chose yourself is left as it is.
  Nothing to do; it happens at the next start.

v0.2.497
- Pressing Like or Unlike on a list you found by searching used to say
  "Success" and then re-open the search keyboard. It now simply redraws the
  list you were looking at, with the entry flipped to the other one. The same
  applied to Unlike, and to both alike.
- MDBList list search now opens a screen of its own instead of the keyboard
  directly, listing the searches you have run before. Pressing Back from a set
  of results returns to that screen rather than all the way out to the home
  screen, so looking up another list no longer means starting over. A search
  you have run before is one click away, and can be removed from the list or
  the whole history cleared. The results themselves also carry a "new search"
  row at the top, so a second search never needs Back at all.
- Cancelling the search keyboard leaves you where you were. It used to take
  you to an empty listing with nothing in it, which then had to be backed out
  of.

v0.2.496
- MDBList lists now carry the same long-press menu that Trakt lists already
  had, including Like and Unlike. The entry shown is the one that applies: a
  list you have already liked offers Unlike, one you have not offers Like. The
  check reads the list data the add-on has already fetched, so it costs no
  extra network request; when that data is not there to read, both entries are
  offered rather than the wrong one guessed. Lists that belong to you, and
  lists MDBList itself only mirrors from elsewhere, are left alone -- neither
  can be liked, so neither is offered a button that cannot work.
- A new row in the subtitle list, "עברית מסונכרנת למובנה", takes a Hebrew subtitle and
  re-times it onto the timeline of the film's own built-in subtitle track. It
  appears only when there is both a Hebrew subtitle to re-time and a built-in
  track to time it against, and it is never chosen for you -- it delivers a
  different translation from the one inside the file, so it is offered as a
  choice rather than applied automatically.
- A raw subtitle number and timecode could appear inside the text of a
  subtitle, mid-film. Two entries had been welded into one when the translation
  lost the blank line between them, and everything after the weld was read as
  text. They are separated again, and a cue is only ever split where real text
  follows.
- The "piratebay" source provider is now off. Anyone who turns it back on keeps
  it on, through this and any later update.
- Around forty of the repairs this add-on applies to the other add-ons could
  stop silently on a file they failed to read, leaving the repair unapplied
  with nothing said about it. They now report it. The tool that audits them was
  itself unable to fail, and now can.

v0.2.495
- Films and episodes play again. The video add-on updated itself this morning
  and began asking the file host to put the file's name inside the download
  link; the name arrives with spaces in it, which makes the link invalid, and
  Kodi refused it without ever contacting the file. Every release name has
  spaces, so nothing played at all. The link is now made valid before playback
  instead of the request being taken away, so the add-on keeps the feature it
  added and a future version of it needs no further repair.
- The same add-on update moved its favourites code, which quietly stopped the
  repair that makes an added title appear straight away instead of only after
  navigating away and back. It works again, and it is now matched in a way
  that a further change of that kind cannot break.

v0.2.494
- Kodi no longer force-closes when several rows on the home screen reload at
  once -- most often on the way back from a submenu to the home screen. The
  video add-on was set to run every one of those loads inside a single shared
  Python interpreter, and loading them together corrupted it. Each load now
  gets its own, which closes the whole family of crashes rather than the one
  way of provoking it that was reported. Takes effect from the next start of
  Kodi after this update, and rows may take slightly longer to draw.
- The AI translation add-on moves to Gemini 3.7 Flash. Anyone already on 3.5
  or 3.6 Flash is moved across once, automatically; any other model you chose
  yourself is left exactly as it is.

v0.2.493
- The "10 latest updates" tile is now only offered once the wizard installed on
  this device is new enough to open it. Until then nothing is written and
  nothing is shown, so the tile never appears as something that cannot be
  clicked; it arrives on its own the moment the wizard catches up.
- The main video add-on is now also covered at the earlier of its two moments
  -- the one during import, before its own code runs -- so the seconds in which
  Kodi still calls a just-re-enabled add-on unknown can no longer take its
  background service at either point.

v0.2.492
- The ten most recent update notes are now readable from a tile on the home
  screen. Removing the tile is respected: it does not come back at the next
  update or the next start. Switching skin rewrites the home tiles from the
  skin's own defaults -- if that removes the tile without you asking it is
  restored, and if you removed it yourself it stays removed.
- The main video add-on no longer loses its background service to the seconds
  in which Kodi still calls a just-re-enabled add-on unknown, so Trakt sync
  keeps running instead of stopping for the rest of the session.
- Two of the bundled add-ons stop offering a newer version at every start that
  their own settings screen had no way to accept. Switch the check back on and
  it stays on.
- An add-on you switched off yourself is no longer switched back on. The build
  only repairs one it disabled itself and could not restore.
- Arctic Fuse 3 no longer force-closes Kodi when a submenu with no content of
  its own is opened.
- You are not asked to reinstall the whole application for a package that
  carries nothing new for an install that already exists.

v0.2.491
- A quick update no longer freezes Kodi. The build refreshes the skin after an
  update, and it also restarts POV so the update takes effect straight away;
  when the two happened at the same moment the home screen was rebuilt while
  POV could not load, every POV row on it failed and Kodi had to be closed.
  Each refresh now waits for POV to be ready first, on every skin, and if it
  is not ready in time the refresh is left for the next start instead of being
  forced through.

v0.2.490
- Umbrella now looks up a series by its original name before searching for
  sources, so a show with a Hebrew name gets its own sources instead of
  whatever happened to share the same season and episode number.

v0.2.489
- Fixes POV search, and the new/popular rows being empty or missing, after
  POV updates itself: the new version writes its caches in a different order
  than the tables it inherited, so everything landed in the wrong column.
  The tables are rebuilt once, keeping every entry that is still readable.
- POV favourites saved before that update are back - the new version keeps
  them somewhere else and left them behind.
- Fixes a fresh install that could not start at all.
- The watched tick no longer appears on films and series you never watched
  in the album-icons view.
- An add-on that an interrupted update left switched off is recovered more
  reliably, and an update is never told it was applied when it was not.

v0.2.486
- MDBList now shows up in "My Movies" and "My Series" after you connect it in
  Account Manager, which is how it is connected now.
- The two MDBList tiles sit next to their own kind on the home screen - films
  with films, series with series - instead of side by side at the end.

v0.2.485
- The tick that marks something as watched now appears on the poster too. It
  was showing in every other view and missing only in the one the build opens
  by default, in POV and in Umbrella alike, for films and for seasons.
- An update no longer closes Kodi. It is applied in place, so there is nothing
  to reopen afterwards. This takes effect from the next update onwards, since
  this one is still installed by the previous version.

v0.2.484
- Trakt no longer has to be reconnected by hand either. When the server stops
  accepting the saved authorisation, POV now renews it and retries, the same
  way MDBList already does, instead of going quiet for half an hour.

v0.2.483
- MDBList no longer has to be reconnected by hand. When the server stops
  accepting the saved authorisation, POV now renews it and retries instead of
  going quiet for half an hour, and Umbrella picks the renewed one up instead
  of asking you to authorise it again in a screen this build does not use.
- Umbrella's watched marks and resume points come back if something has reset
  them. If you connect the service again it is restored automatically; if it
  was reset without you connecting anything, you are asked once, and an answer
  of no is never asked again.
- The two MDBList tiles on the home screen come back for anyone who lost them.

v0.2.482
- Trakt is now connected once, for both POV and Umbrella. Connecting it in POV
  used to leave Umbrella with nothing, which is why Umbrella showed every
  episode as unwatched while POV showed the ticks.
- Umbrella now actually reads watched state and resume points from whichever
  service you connected. Connecting one never made Umbrella use it, so the
  ticks stayed missing even when everything said "connected". MDBList is
  preferred when both are connected, whichever you connected first. A source
  you have chosen yourself is never changed.
- The season list now opens in a view that shows each season's own poster.
  POV always supplied them; in some skins the screen was a plain list with no
  poster in it at all. If you pick a view yourself afterwards, it stays.
- Season names are in Hebrew.
- Our own settings screen no longer logs an error every time it opens.

v0.2.481
- Fixed the "POV error" when opening a search screen. POV changed the layout
  of its cache between versions but kept the old one on devices that had it,
  so anything it saved afterwards was written into the wrong place. The cache
  is repaired in place and your saved searches are kept.
- MDBList is now connected once, for both POV and Umbrella. Umbrella used to
  need a separate authorisation of its own; it no longer does.

v0.2.480
- Connecting MDBList now offers Umbrella's own one-time authorisation.
  Umbrella cannot accept the key from Account Manager - it needs its own
  authorisation - which is why it used to show as connected while staying
  empty.

v0.2.479
- The Hebrew search fix in 0.2.478 never actually reached your device. It
  looked for a line ending that the copy of Umbrella this build installs
  does not use, so it reported that it could not apply and changed nothing.
  Searching in Hebrew now works.
- "Playback failed" no longer appears when you back out of the source list
  without picking anything. It was Kodi's own message, raised on a timer
  after 20 seconds of browsing rather than by any real failure.

v0.2.478
- Searching Umbrella in Hebrew now finds what you are looking for. Typing a
  title in Hebrew returned "no results" while the same title in English
  worked; the search request was never actually being sent. Movie search, TV
  search and person search are all fixed, and so are titles with an
  apostrophe in them.
- Repaired the unified Discover data source for POV 6.08, which had stopped
  being applied after a POV update.

v0.2.477
- The Hebrew keyboard comes back by itself. If the Hebrew layout ever
  disappears from Kodi's keyboard-layout list - so the on-screen keyboard can
  only type English, everywhere - it is now restored automatically on the next
  start. Your chosen layout, and any other layout you added, are left exactly
  as they are.

v0.2.475
- Umbrella (optional): the source list now shows, under each source and before
  you pick it, how well an available Hebrew subtitle matches that exact
  release - the same badge POV already has, and the two share their data, so a
  title checked in one shows its badge straight away in the other.
- MDBList: connecting or disconnecting it can no longer disturb any other POV
  setting. Kodi rewrites a whole add-on's settings file around every single
  write, and this was the last place still doing that the old way.
- Both source lists are now protected against a failure in that badge. It
  could previously have hidden every source in the list, which would have
  looked like the sources themselves had stopped working.

v0.2.474
- "Connect Services" now connects your debrid accounts everywhere at once.
  Real-Debrid, Premiumize, AllDebrid, TorBox, OffCloud and Easynews go
  through Account Manager, so one connect fills them into every add-on you
  have installed instead of into POV alone. Same screen, same single click,
  and it is in Hebrew now. Each row says whether it is connected everywhere,
  connected to POV only, or not connected. Trakt, TMDb lists and EasyDebrid
  still connect to POV directly.
- Account Manager now installs by itself, for everyone, including anyone who
  already has the build. Nothing you have connected changes.
- MDBList keeps its QR pairing from a phone, and now passes the key on to
  your other add-ons too.
- New: you can choose whether the search button searches with POV or with
  Umbrella. The new tile "מנוע החיפוש" on the home screen switches between
  them, on all four skins, and the change appears right away. Only shown if
  Umbrella is installed.
- New home tile for Umbrella itself, for anyone who installed it.

v0.2.473
- Umbrella (optional): the rest of the menus are now in Hebrew too - the main
  menu, the Discover lists and the personal lists. A few rows stay in English
  because their names are fixed in the add-on and cannot be translated.

v0.2.472
- Umbrella (optional): its menus are now in Hebrew, and it asks for titles,
  plot summaries and artwork in Hebrew too. Anything with no Hebrew
  translation still shows in English, exactly as before. If you already chose

v0.2.471
- New optional add-on in the wizard menu: Account Manager. Connect your
  debrid accounts once in one place and it fills them into every add-on you
  have installed, POV and Umbrella included - and into anything you install
  later, by itself. Nothing installs or changes unless you pick it.

v0.2.470
- Umbrella (optional): three more search providers are switched on in the
  search engine it uses, a source that fails to play now moves on to the next
  one instead of stopping, and a search that gets stuck gives up sooner.
  Anything you changed yourself is left exactly as you set it.

v0.2.469
- Umbrella (optional): two extra search providers are now switched on in the
  search engine it uses, so a search reaches sources it previously missed.
  Anyone who prefers otherwise can turn them off and the choice is kept.

v0.2.468
- Three long-standing POV fixes started silently missing their target after
  POV's latest rewrite and now apply again: sources coming back empty for
  some setups, a Trakt cache crash, and lists that would not clear.
- Umbrella (optional): the lists no longer come up empty when its language
  is set to Hebrew, CocoScrapers is set up automatically on install, and
  subtitle matching now works for sources whose link hides the file name.

v0.2.467
- Umbrella (the optional pilot addon) showed blank labels throughout its
  settings on a Hebrew-language Kodi: it ships its texts only in the legacy
  language layout. The texts are now mirrored into the modern layout Kodi
  looks for, self-healing after every Umbrella update.

v0.2.466
- The "last watched" gold badge on the source you picked last time is back:
  it was being written to a field the current player version no longer
  displays, so it silently vanished. It now marks the field that is
  actually shown, with the old field kept as a fallback.

v0.2.465
- Lines the AI silently skipped (usually strong language) are no longer left
  in English as a group: each one is now isolated and translated on its own,
  through every fallback engine, before English is ever accepted -- and only
  ever for a single line, never a whole stretch.

v0.2.464
- Stopping an episode part-way no longer leaves the screen on a spinner (or
  a bare ".." listing) while the episode list rebuilds: the list now stays
  live and refreshes once, after the progress sync finishes.

v0.2.463
- Masculine forms used where a woman is being addressed were the biggest
  quality complaint, and two separate things caused them. The add-on could
  end up translating with no gender information available to it at all, in
  which case it fell back to masculine everywhere; and even when it had that
  information it sometimes did not apply it. Both are fixed, and the finished
  translation is now checked line by line before it is saved. On a full film
  this took gender agreement to 100% -- no wrong lines at all, against 24 in
  the same file before.
- MoranSubs opened with an empty list and no explanation for anyone whose
  Kodi subtitle languages did not name Hebrew. Opening a Hebrew subtitle
  add-on IS the request for Hebrew, so the list is no longer withheld over a
  setting. The automatic on-play translation still follows the setting, as it
  must, but now records why it skipped instead of going silent. Nothing in
  Kodi's own settings is changed on your behalf.
- Speaker names stopped appearing inside the Hebrew ("- איאן: לא, לא."). A
  name is removed only where the English line it came from really did start
  with one, so a line that genuinely uses a colon is never touched. There
  were 60 of them in one file a viewer sent in.
- The stray "-" cues are gone. A sound-effect line behind a dialogue dash
  lost its bracketed part and shipped the dash on its own -- 95 of those in
  that same file, 14 cues that were nothing else at all.
- Letters from another alphabet in the middle of a Hebrew word are now
  repaired instead of deleted. The word itself was right; only some of its
  letters were typed in the wrong script, and removing them left half a word
  ("לעצמי" arrived as "לעצ"). They are spelled back into Hebrew now.
- Vowel points that turned up here and there are removed. Hebrew subtitles
  are written without them.
- A line the AI refuses to translate is now passed to a different
  translation engine before it is given up on and left in English.
- Subtitles already saved on your device, and anything already shared to the
  community pool, get the wrong-alphabet and vowel-point repairs as they are
  read -- so they improve without being translated again.

v0.2.462
- Fresh installs came up with empty Movies, TV shows and Idan+ screens. The
  five FENtastic widget files those screens are built from were being skipped
  during installation: the wizard skips them on purpose so a quick update
  never overwrites a home layout you customised, but the check it used also
  matched a brand new install, where there was no layout to keep and the files
  simply never arrived. The skin then failed every widget include and drew
  nothing. The check now keeps a file you actually have and writes one you are
  missing, and the add-on also puts any missing widget file back at startup, so
  a device that already lost them repairs itself.
- The seek bar Kodi refused to load on every single start is fixed. The shipped
  file was missing one closing tag, so Kodi rejected the whole window and fell
  back to the plain one.
- "חיבור שירותים", "חיפוש לפי שנה", "לפי רשתות" and both personal areas opened
  onto nothing. Every folder POV ships is stored in one spelling and read in
  another, so POV read them all as empty and never said so. POV now reads them
  the way they are written. Nothing in the database is touched, so anything you
  have set up stays exactly as it is.
- The same mismatch also made POV quietly replace the build's menus with its
  own defaults. That stops too.

v0.2.461
- Kodi's own Hebrew strings now self-heal, not just the skin's. A strings.po
  that stops part-way through leaves Kodi falling back to English for the ids
  it cannot find and showing nothing at all for ids missing everywhere, so one
  damaged file looked like three faults: "Favourites" and "Exit" suddenly in
  English, two power-menu entries with no caption, and a FENtastic home that
  reads as empty because on that skin the labels ARE the content. A pristine
  copy of Kodi's Hebrew file now ships inside this add-on and is restored when
  the live one is damaged. Kodi reads those strings once at startup, so that
  repair shows up on the NEXT start; the skin's own files take effect at once,
  because the skin is reloaded for them.
- Damage detection no longer trusts file size alone -- a write killed in the
  last third passed that check and still lost every label after the cut. It now
  also looks for far fewer entries than the shipped copy, or a final entry that
  never finished. A cut landing exactly between two entries is deliberately
  left alone, and so is a translation that merely differs from ours.
- 43 settings Kodi dropped on every startup are fixed. They used a control type
  that does not exist for add-on settings, so Kodi logged an error and threw
  each one away -- and they are the markers that record which one-time
  migrations have already run.
- Restored two repairs POV 6.08 had quietly outgrown, retired a diagnostic that
  answered its question years of releases ago, and the MDBList pairing QR no
  longer calls itself Gemini.

v0.2.460
- Fixes "no results" on the first attempt at a movie or episode, with sources
  appearing on the second. POV 6.08 spends one setting, the scraper/debrid
  timeout, on two phases that run back to back: finding torrents, then asking
  the debrid which of them it already holds. After each phase it keeps only the
  threads that finished, and the final source list is built exclusively from the
  debrid phase -- so with a single debrid configured, one slow answer discards
  every source that was found. The torrents are not lost: each provider caches
  its own results as it finishes, which is why the next attempt is instant and
  works.
- POV shipped 6.08.01 with this setting's default raised from 10 to 20 and
  renamed from "Scraper Timeout" to "Scraper/Debrid Timeout (secs)". Our build
  pinned 10 in userdata, and a stored value beats a schema default forever --
  an add-on update replaces the add-on folder and never touches userdata, so
  the new default could not reach anyone. It is now raised to POV's own 20,
  only upwards: anyone who set it higher keeps their number, and anyone who
  lowers it afterwards keeps that too.
- The write also drops POV's cached settings snapshot, without which the value
  would be correct on disk while POV's running process kept using the old one,
  and it is verified by reading it back, so a write Kodi silently swallows is
  retried instead of being recorded as done.

v0.2.459
- Two text defects in AI translations, and the repair now runs where the text is
  MADE rather than only on a later pass over it. A cue sometimes came back
  holding the source-language lines above the Hebrew ones; only the Hebrew is
  kept now. A single letter sometimes drew as a square, which is the player
  saying it has no glyph for that codepoint -- Hebrew presentation forms
  (U+FB1D-FB4F) are folded to the ordinary letters the shipped fonts do have,
  and invisible zero-width and bidi controls are dropped.
- Both repairs previously ran only on the RE-repair paths, so the FIRST watch of
  a new translation still showed the defect, cached it, and contributed it to the
  community pool -- where it could never be fixed afterwards, because pool entries
  are written once and only repaired on read. They now run in the fresh-
  translation path before the file is cached or contributed, in the live preview
  shown during translation, and on both delivery repairs.
- The echo strip only ever touches OUR OWN output, only removes leading lines
  with no Hebrew in them, and never empties a cue or orphans a styling tag. A cue
  the model left untranslated has no Hebrew in it and is exempt in full; a line
  with Hebrew in it, like "נולדתי בChina", is never a candidate.

v0.2.458
- POV now says WHY a debrid source refused to play. Its one message,
  "selected_files failed", covered two opposite failures: the debrid returned no
  files at all (the source is advertised as cached by an external scraper but
  the provider does not actually hold it), or it returned files and POV's own
  season/episode filter rejected every one. From outside those are
  indistinguishable, so "I clicked a source, it ran through twenty and played
  nothing" could not be diagnosed without guessing. The message now carries the
  evidence -- "(0 file(s) from torbox)" or "(14 file(s) from torbox, filtered
  out: ...)". Behaviour is untouched; only the log grows.
- Restored a guard POV had silently outgrown. It protects resolve_external_
  sources' error handler from crashing on its own unbound variables, which would
  abort the whole "try the next source" loop and end at "no results" with
  sources still on screen. It matched one exact signature line, POV turned that
  function into a method, and it has been quietly skipping ever since.

v0.2.457
- A fresh install that lands on an EMPTY FENtastic home screen now repairs
  itself. The build ships a 36-tile favourites.xml, something between the zip
  and the first boot was losing it, and the workaround people found -- switch
  the skin to NOX and back -- says what was missing: that path makes the wizard
  copy a favourites seed over the file. We were holding the same canonical file
  all along and refusing to use it, because the repair was disabled outright
  after an older version of it resurrected tiles users had deliberately deleted.
  That refusal still stands for any home with tiles on it; it no longer applies
  to one with almost nothing, which cannot be a preference. Tiles you added
  yourself are kept, and a missing or truncated file is covered too.
- The MDBList "My Movies / My Series" tiles get one way back. Insert-once was
  unable to tell a deletion from a wipe -- a skin switch, a favourites reseed, a
  home-widget edit -- so installs that lost them the second way were refused
  forever. They are restored once for anyone who still has MDBList connected,
  and the credit is not spent on anyone who has not connected yet. After that
  the normal rule resumes: delete them and they stay deleted.

v0.2.456
- Embedded subtitle extraction stops punishing one congestion event six times.
  The pace controller backed off per 429 RESPONSE rather than per congestion
  EPOCH: a single cluster fetch retries up to five times, and every retry that
  answered 429 widened the gap again, so one unlucky cluster could multiply it
  by 1.5^5 on its own. A field log shows the whole failure in thirty-three
  seconds -- first refusal after 68 requests, sixth refusal 27 requests later,
  and the pace at its 2.00s ceiling for the rest of the run. It was overshoot,
  not a slow provider: the same log then records 25 consecutive CLEAN requests
  at that ceiling. Now, after widening, the controller refuses to widen again
  until the new pace has actually been measured, so repeat refusals from the
  same event are honoured and retried without compounding.
- Recovery could not keep up with the back-off either. 0.9x per 25 clean
  requests is one 10% step per fifty seconds at a 2.00s pace, and that run
  managed exactly one such step in its lifetime. Now 0.85x per 12 -- still far
  more cautious than the 1.5x back-off, so it errs slow rather than fast, but
  no longer permanently.
- And it no longer relearns the same lesson every episode. Every extraction
  started at the same optimistic pace, spent the provider's burst allowance
  discovering it was too fast, and paid the climb again. The pace a run settles
  on is now remembered per provider and the next run starts there, 25% quicker
  so it keeps probing and one bad night fades over a few runs. It can never
  start faster than a fresh install would, only slower.
  Measured against the real debrid link from the field log: a 2.79GB BluRay
  REMUX that had reached 143 of 364 cues in four minutes now returns all 364 in
  just over two, with the subtitle complete and no errors.
- The MDBList "My Movies / My Series" home tiles appear as soon as MDBList is
  connected, instead of needing two restarts. Kodi reads favourites.xml exactly
  once, when the profile loads, and answers everything afterwards from memory --
  so the tiles our startup repair appended were on disk but not on screen, and
  the restart that wrote them could never be the one that revealed them. They
  are now also handed to the running Kodi through its own API, and connecting
  MDBList runs the repair there and then. Tiles you deleted stay deleted.

v0.2.455
- The three-connection extraction from the last version is switched back off,
  and the harm it did is undone. It was wrong, and the reasoning was wrong in a
  way worth writing down: keeping the same gap between requests is not the same
  as keeping the same request RATE. Serially each line cost the gap AND a round
  trip to the provider; with three connections the round trip stops counting and
  only the gap remains, so we asked about three times as often. That IS the
  speedup -- waiting less means asking more -- and it is also three times the
  load, which is exactly what a debrid account limits. TorBox refused within
  thirty seconds, and by the time the add-on had backed off to one connection
  the automatic slow-down had already widened the gap tenfold, where it stays
  until hundreds of clean requests walk it back. An episode that used to take
  four minutes was left crawling for nineteen.
- So: back to one connection, and a slow-down caused by our own burst is now
  undone when the burst stops, instead of being carried for the rest of the
  extraction. Anyone who saw an extraction crawling after the last update gets
  the old speed back. The faster path is kept and still tested, switched off,
  until there is a provider it can be shown to help on -- measured against that
  provider rather than against a stand-in.

v0.2.454
- Extracting a built-in subtitle is about twice as fast. It was never limited by
  bandwidth -- it was waiting: one connection, one request at a time, so every
  line cost the gap we deliberately leave between requests AND a round trip to
  the debrid server on top of it, and the link sat idle for most of the four
  minutes an episode took. It now uses three connections while the provider is
  behaving, and -- this is the part that matters -- they all take their turn from
  ONE shared schedule, so the server is still asked at exactly the same rate as
  before. Only our own waiting disappears. Measured at the real settings against
  a typical debrid round trip: an episode goes from about three minutes to about
  one and a half, for exactly the same number of requests.
- It stands down at the first sign of trouble. The moment the provider pushes
  back even once, or the file turns out to be one whose structure has to be
  re-learned as it goes, everything hands back to the single connection and
  finishes the way it always did. That is deliberate: pushback is judged by how
  many refusals pile up against progress, and three connections pile them up
  three times faster -- which would make the add-on give up on exactly the
  awkward-but-usable provider it was taught to patiently crawl through.
- And a fault while fetching can no longer end as a quiet success. If anything
  unexpected goes wrong on one part of the file, that part is now re-read the
  slow, thorough way instead of vanishing from a subtitle that still reports
  itself complete -- which would also have thrown away the saved progress that
  exists to recover it next time.

v0.2.453
- Ktuvit subtitles that arrive unzipped now reach the community pool, and their
  timing can be checked again. The engine converts a downloaded subtitle to
  UTF-8 only when it has just unpacked it from an archive; Ktuvit sometimes
  hands over the .srt on its own, and those files were used exactly as they
