  arrived -- in the older Hebrew encoding. Everything that reads a subtitle
  afterwards reads it as UTF-8, so every Hebrew letter turned into a
  replacement character: the pool's own quality check then saw a subtitle with
  no Hebrew in it and refused to upload it (which is why it never appeared in
  the channel), and the timing check saw one line of dialogue instead of
  hundreds and gave up. A downloaded subtitle is now converted whichever way it
  arrived, including one already sitting in the local cache from before. A file
  that is already UTF-8 is not touched at all, so nothing already shared is
  re-uploaded.
- ...and the conversion can no longer damage a file it was not meant to touch.
  "Not valid UTF-8" is not the same as "the old Hebrew encoding": it also
  describes a perfectly good file with one damaged byte, and the old encoding
  accepts almost any bytes. Reading such a file the wrong way turned every
  curly quote and dash in it into gibberish -- a glitch nobody would notice
  made into a ruined subtitle. The conversion is now accepted only when it
  actually produces Hebrew, in the engine's own converter as well as the new
  one, so an English subtitle with a single bad byte keeps the single bad byte.

v0.2.452
- Hardening on yesterday's TorBox repair, from a review of it. The repair now
  refuses to install its copy of POV's file unless that copy compiles: POV
  imports the TorBox client the moment anything debrid-related loads, so a file
  that will not parse would take down every debrid path rather than just the
  one line being fixed. It also no longer accepts the mere mention of the
  missing property as proof a device is healthy -- only an actual assignment
  counts -- and it clears up the stray file our old packages planted even on
  devices where POV had already replaced the client on its own.
- The log stops repeating a warning it cannot act on. A patch that adds TorBox
  30-day usage to POV's account screen has been aiming at a file POV deleted;
  it only appeared to still apply because our own package kept putting an old
  copy of that file back. It now recognises that POV moved on and says nothing
  further, so the log keeps meaning something when the next problem arrives.

v0.2.451
- Picking a source plays it again. Since 6.07.92 POV reads a property on its
  TorBox client one line AFTER the source has already resolved and the playable
  link is in hand -- and on this build that property was missing, so the link
  was thrown away and POV moved on to the next source, and the next, until the
  list ran out and it said "no results". The property was missing because of
  us: the build's quick-update package had been carrying a copy of POV's own
  TorBox file from June, and Kodi lays a quick update straight over the add-ons
  folder, so every update quietly put that older copy back over POV's. It did
  no harm for weeks, until POV started needing something only its own newer
  copy has. The package no longer carries any POV file at all, and this version
  puts POV's own file back where an earlier package overwrote it. Nothing is
  touched on a device that was not affected.

v0.2.450
- "No results" on every movie and every episode is fixed. POV's own scraper
  list starts with a line that is a takeover rather than a filter: if the
  AIOStreams provider is switched on, AIOStreams becomes the ONLY scraper POV
  will ask, and every other one -- external, the debrid cloud scrapers,
  easynews -- is dropped before it is ever consulted. The AIOStreams scraper in
  turn returns an empty list immediately, without a single network request,
  when its username and password are blank. The two together are exactly what
  users saw: POV's progress window flashing open and shut in a twentieth of a
  second, then "no results", on every title, forever. POV removed AIOStreams in
  6.04 and brought it back in 6.07, and Kodi keeps a setting's stored value even
  while an add-on stops declaring it -- so a switch left on years ago got its
  meaning back with the update, without anyone touching it. That takeover now
  also requires the credentials to be present, and a switched-on AIOStreams with
  no credentials is switched back off. Both only ever act when the credentials
  are empty, so anyone actually using AIOStreams is left exactly as they were.
- Added a switch that stops this add-on touching POV at all. It rewrites about
  twenty of POV's own source files on every startup, and POV updates itself
  whenever its author publishes, so any of those rewrites can stop being right
  at a moment nobody chooses. Turning the switch on makes every one of them
  return before doing anything; reinstall POV from its repository and it stays
  as its author shipped it. It exists so "is it the add-on?" can be answered by
  trying it instead of by guesswork.

v0.2.449
- Sources are found again. POV updated itself and every title began answering
  "no results", which is what POV shows when its result list comes back empty.
  This add-on corrects rows POV labels SD whose own release name says 1080p --
  and, having corrected one, it also re-applied the user's resolution filter and
  removed the row if the corrected quality was not in it. That read a POV
  setting by name and split its value on commas, both of which are POV's to
  change; a value in any other shape produces a filter that matches nothing and
  removes every row it touches. It now corrects the label and never removes a
  row: POV has already applied the filter itself, so a row that reached the list
  is one POV chose to show.
- Kodi no longer updates, closes itself, and then does it again on every launch.
  The update recorded that it had run through a store that silently discards
  errors, at the moment the package it had just unpacked replaced the wizard's
  own files, and then force-closed Kodi in a way that deliberately skips saving.
  Lose that write and the next launch finds the same update waiting. The record
  now goes to a second place as well, is read back to confirm it survived, and
  Kodi is not closed unless it did -- the update is installed either way and
  simply takes effect the next time Kodi starts on its own. Repeated attempts
  are also counted and capped, so nothing can retry without end.
- Pressing play during a subtitle extraction no longer risks closing the film.
  Resuming after a pause used to cancel the extraction; not cancelling it left
  the player's first read of the film competing with it for the same debrid
  connection, which could be refused and end playback. Neither was necessary:
  the extraction now stands aside for a few seconds so the player can get ahead,
  then carries on from where it was.

v0.2.448
- Pressing play no longer cancels an extraction in progress. The rule was that
  resuming the film mattered if the provider had refused even one request -- and
  one refusal in fifty-seven is ordinary busy-CDN noise, not a provider in
  trouble. Worse, the pause being resumed is usually one the add-on itself
  caused, because opening the subtitle list pauses playback: every subtitle
  picked by hand therefore began paused, and pressing play to carry on watching
  was being read as a signal about the provider. A field log shows an extraction
  killed at 51 lines of 321 the instant play was pressed, and again on the next
  attempt, so that file never got past 18 per cent. Cancelling now requires the
  provider to have actually throttled the extraction down, and to have done so
  recently rather than at some point earlier in the run. The separate guard that
  watches for the picture freezing is unchanged.
- Extraction says that it is working, from the first line it reads. It used to
  wait for the first whole twenty per cent, which on a distant provider is
  minutes, and the run above never reached twenty per cent at all -- so the only
  thing that user ever saw was the message saying it had stopped. It now reports
  as soon as the first line comes out, then every ten per cent, and never goes
  more than three quarters of a minute without saying something while lines are
  still arriving. On a fast provider it is still only a handful of messages.

v0.2.447
- An extraction in progress is no longer cancelled by pausing the film and
  resuming it. Handing the connection back to the player is only worth doing
  when the provider is actually pushing back on the extra requests; when it is
  not, the extraction simply carries on and the player is unaffected. Before
  this, a single pause part-way through threw away minutes of work.
- Extraction now shows how far along it is. It reports its progress as it goes,
  including over a full-screen picture, where previously nothing at all appeared
  for several minutes. An extraction that is cut short says how much of the file
  it managed to read; that much is kept, and choosing the subtitle again picks up
  from there instead of starting over.
- A provider that starts refusing requests part-way through no longer ends the
  extraction. It is now crawled through at whatever rate the provider will
  tolerate, and the rate is raised again once the refusals stop. On a provider
  that refused two out of every three requests, this is the difference between
  no subtitle at all and a complete one.
- A subtitle can no longer come out with only part of its lines because a
  provider quietly returned a short answer. A capped or cut-off response carries
  no error of any kind, and when the bytes happened to run out on an internal
  boundary even reading them back looked normal. The amount of data returned is
  now checked against the amount owed, both for the file's index and for the
  subtitle data itself, and anything short defers the extraction rather than
  handing over a subtitle that is missing lines without saying so. A file whose
  provider merely reports its size a little wrong still finishes, because that
  case is settled by asking the provider how long the file actually is, rather
  than by guessing from an answer that did not arrive.
- Lines the AI leaves out of its reply are asked for again instead of being left
  in the original language. A reply that came back missing a hundred lines used
  to leave a hundred lines untranslated in the middle of the film; the missing
  ones are now re-requested on their own, in smaller pieces, and only what still
  does not come back stays as it was.

v0.2.446
- Reading a video's embedded subtitle track over a debrid link now costs about
  half the network requests it used to. Every subtitle line was paying for two
  separate range requests: one at the start of its cluster, purely to work out
  where the line sits and what time it begins, and one for the line itself.
  Neither of those two facts has to be re-read for every line -- the first is a
  property of how the file was muxed, the second is already recorded in the
  file's own index. Both are checked against the old method before they are
  relied on, and anything that does not check out falls back to it, so a file
  can only be read faster, never less accurately.
- A short buffering hiccup no longer cancels an extraction in progress. Kodi
  reports a buffering stall and a seek exactly the way it reports a pause, and a
  single momentary one was enough to cancel -- which is why extraction could run
  for minutes and finish nothing while the movie was simply playing. A pause now
  has to be held for a few seconds to count. Pausing deliberately and then
  resuming still hands the connection straight back to the player, as before.
- An interrupted extraction keeps what it collected, and the next attempt
  continues from there. On a provider that limits requests aggressively an
  extraction can only ever be cut short, so it previously never finished at all,
  no matter how many times the file was played. A partial result is still never
  used as a subtitle; this only decides what survives an interruption.
- When a provider pushes back, extraction slows down as before -- and now speeds
  back up once the provider stops. It used to stay slow for the rest of the run.
  It never goes faster than its normal starting rate.
- Extracting from a file stored on the device is no longer refused because
  another extraction appeared to be running. That guard protects a shared debrid
  connection and has no purpose for a local file.
- A translated line whose start time the AI mistyped is now given its correct
  start back, in the cases where the surrounding lines make its position certain.
  Such a line appeared several cues early, sat on screen over unrelated dialogue,
  and vanished at the moment it was actually spoken. Only the timing is touched;
  text, entry count and order are never changed, and anything less than certain
  is left exactly as it is.
- Stray Arabic letters are now also removed when they appear glued inside a
  Hebrew word rather than as a separate word, and one Arabic character block that
  was missing from the check is covered. Subtitles already stored on your device
  are cleaned again on this update.

v0.2.445
- Fixed embedded subtitles disappearing from the subtitle list for anyone who
  turned OFF automatic Hebrew search on playback. The two were wired together by
  mistake: the list of a file's built-in subtitle tracks was only ever recorded
  as part of the automatic search, so switching that off silently removed every
  built-in entry as well, including "built-in translated to Hebrew (AI)". They
  are separate now. With automatic search off you still get the entries and can
  pick them yourself; you just do not get an automatic search.
- Fixed stray Arabic words and fragments appearing inside translated Hebrew
  lines. Hebrew and Arabic both mark gender, so a human Arabic translation of
  the same scene is given to the AI purely as a gender reference, and it
  sometimes copied a word out of it. Such a word is now removed on delivery.
  This is deliberately cautious: it only removes Arabic from a line that also
  has Hebrew, never removes a line, never removes a subtitle entry, and never
  touches a line that is entirely Arabic -- so nothing can fall silent while
  people are talking. Subtitles already translated and stored with the problem
  are cleaned on this device as they are delivered.
- The "searching for subtitles" banner no longer flashes on live TV channels.
  The check that recognises a live stream ran after the banner was already
  drawn, so a channel briefly showed a search that had in fact been cancelled.
- Built-in subtitle tracks tagged with a three-letter language code -- Spanish,
  German, Dutch, Japanese, Swedish, Czech and about fifteen more -- are matched
  correctly again, so translating them works instead of reporting that no track
  was found.

v0.2.444
- Fixed a translated Hebrew line staying frozen on screen while the rest of the
  dialogue came and went underneath it. The AI is handed whole subtitle entries
  including their timecodes and is asked to copy the timings verbatim; when it
  mistyped one digit, a single entry could last an hour. Every translated entry
  now gets its ORIGINAL timecode back, so the AI is never trusted with timing.
- Added a conservative safety bound on how long any subtitle entry may stay on
  screen, as a second line of defence for the cases the first cannot cover. It
  is deliberately gentle: long holds and deliberate overlap that real subtitles
  legitimately contain are left exactly as authored, and a correct subtitle is
  never altered.
- Subtitles that were already translated and shared with a frozen line are
  repaired on this device as they are delivered -- from the community pool,
  from the local cache, and from a file saved next to the video. Shared and
  cached source files stay immutable, so nothing is re-uploaded and no cache is
  purged. Entry count, text and language are provably untouched, so no subtitle
  becomes newly rejected.

v0.2.443
- Fixed Hebrew RTL display for human subtitles previously processed by the
  built-in sources engine: dialogue dashes and trailing ellipses now render on
  the correct visual edge. Existing cache and community-pool source files stay
  immutable; the correction is applied to a local display copy, so it causes
  no cache purge, provider refetch, or pool re-upload.
- Community-pool subtitles are cached locally by hash after their first fetch,
  and Ktuvit release-name de-dup now protects both the immediate and durable
  upload paths. Re-picking the same pool subtitle does not spend another /sub
  request, while future Ktuvit rows retain pristine logical punctuation.
- Kodi POV IL profiles still using the exact shipped subtitle appearance now
  use white text with a black glyph outline and no rectangular background.
  The migration is retry-safe and preserves the entire style when the user has
  customized any visual subtitle setting.

v0.2.442
- Gender-reference search can now examine up to 50 subtitle candidates across
  the researched language-priority chain, while still trying at most 10 inside
  one language, stopping on the first aligned subtitle, and retaining the same
  30-active-second latency guard.

v0.2.441
- Embedded-subtitle translation now has one explained Advanced-mode selector:
  Automatic (recommended), timing alignment only, direct text extraction only,
  local files only, or disabled. Existing hidden choices migrate safely, and
  every failed method still falls back to the normal external-subtitle path.
- Gender-reference selection now follows the researched language priority and
  tries up to 10 candidates inside a language before moving to the next one.
  Downloads and alignment remain strictly serial and first-aligning-candidate
  wins, with a 15-download / 30-active-second safety budget for hard titles.

v0.2.440
- Settings: hide three internal implementation toggles that Kodi exposed as
  blank rows in Expert mode. They were never user-facing controls, and their
  stored values and runtime behavior are unchanged. The fix applies to every
  MoranSubs installation.

v0.2.439
- Hotfix: restore the community-pool signing credential that was accidentally
  omitted from the packaged 0.2.438 update. The request-saving quality and
  capacity gates from 0.2.438 remain unchanged and now operate with authenticated
  lookup/contribution requests again. No settings or user action are required.

v0.2.438
- Efficiency: the add-on no longer uploads a translation to the community pool
  when the server would only reject it -- a partial/low-quality result (fails the
  Hebrew-ratio / length gate) or an episode already holding the maximum number of
  variants. It now runs the SAME checks the server does, BEFORE the upload, so
  those wasted network requests are never sent (they were the dominant source of
  requests against the pool's free-tier quota). Invisible to users: a genuine
  Hebrew subtitle is never withheld -- the checks fail open, and you still get
  every subtitle you did before.

v0.2.437
- New: the "My Movies / My Series (MDBList)" tile now also appears as a native row
  in the FENtastic personal area (navigator.db) and the Arctic Fuse 3 home layout
  (skinvariables), not just in the favourites shortcuts. It shows only when MDBList
  is connected and -- like the TMDB/Trakt rows -- routes to the newest-first
  mdblist_watchlist list, so it inherits that sort. Add-only: a tile you remove
  stays removed, and existing personal-area customizations are left untouched.

v0.2.436
- Fix: the "My Movies / My Series (Trakt)" home lists now also lead with the most
  recently added title. Those lists merge your Trakt collection + watchlist +
  favorites, and the merge was concatenating them (so a freshly-added watchlist
  title landed after the whole collection). It now sorts the combined list by
  date-added, newest first. (MDBList was already fixed in 0.2.435; TMDB has no
  per-item date so it keeps its own newest-first order.)

v0.2.435
- Fix: the "newest added first" sorting from 0.2.433 wasn't actually taking
  effect (POV serves its sort setting from a cache our background write couldn't
  refresh). Now handled directly in POV's sort logic, so the My Movies / My
  Series lists (MDBList/Trakt/TMDB) really do lead with the most recently added
  title. If you deliberately pick a different sort from POV's own sort menu, that
  still wins. In-progress / watched lists are unchanged.

v0.2.434
- Gemini models updated to the newer versions. The default free model moves from
  3.1 Flash Lite to 3.5 Flash Lite (identical free daily quota, better quality),
  and the paid "newest" option moves from 3.5 Flash to 3.6 Flash. Existing users
  on those two are upgraded automatically on next start; if you deliberately chose
  a different model, your choice is kept. No action needed.

v0.2.433
- Personal lists now sort by "recently added" (newest first) by default, instead
  of A-Z. Applies to the My Movies / My Series lists across MDBList, Trakt AND
  TMDB (and the combined views) -- so a title you just added leads the list. One-
  time default; if you later pick a different sort from POV's own "sort by" menu,
  that choice is kept. Same on every skin.
- "My Movies / My Series (MDBList)" now shows BOTH your Watchlist and your
  Collection ("Recently Added" on mdblist.com, where a Trakt import lands) in one
  list, newest first -- no extra tiles. Titles you add via the long-press manager
  and titles synced from Trakt now appear together in the same place, most-
  recently-added first. Reading only (no crash risk); MDBList's cached list is
  reused so there's no extra API cost.

v0.2.432
- MDBList home tiles: "הסרטים שלי (MDBList)" and "הסדרות שלי (MDBList)" now appear
  in your personal-tiles area (next to the TMDB/Trakt/POV "My Movies/My Series"
  tiles), each opening your MDBList Watchlist filtered to movies / to series
  respectively -- routed through POV's own cached watchlist read (no extra API
  cost). They show up ONLY if you've connected MDBList; branded MDBList artwork
  included. Add-only and one-time -- if you delete them, they stay deleted.

v0.2.431
- MDBList list manager is now Watchlist-only. The "Collection" button kept
  crashing on some POV builds (a version-specific POV bug in its post-add sync
  that we can't patch reliably across every build), and MDBList "Collection"
  marks media you OWN -- which never applies to a streaming setup. We removed the
  Collection button entirely: adding/removing to your MDBList Watchlist works as
  before, and there's nothing left to crash. (Shows still get the "Dropped"
  toggle POV adds; only Collection was removed.)

v0.2.430
- MDBList "Collection" no longer crashes on the post-add refresh. After the
  previous fix correctly routed the Collection button, POV's own activity-sync
  step could still crash: when POV's cached "last activity" row can't be read
  back as a table, POV handed the raw database row (a tuple) to code expecting a
  table -> "tuple indices must be integers" -> crash on every sync (the periodic
  monitor and the Collection button alike). Fixed: we guard that step -- if the
  cached value is malformed we clear POV's MDBList cache once (it rebuilds clean
  on the next pass) and skip the bad run instead of crashing. Also covers the
  case where MDBList briefly returns a non-JSON response. Watchlist unaffected.

v0.2.429
- MDBList "Collection" in the list manager no longer crashes on a Hebrew UI. POV
  routes the Watchlist/Collection buttons by their English label text; our Hebrew
  translation of "Collection" -> "קולקציה" made POV treat it as a user list by
  that name, POST to a non-existent list (404), and crash on the empty result.
  Fixed: POV now uses stable ids for those two buttons, and the add/remove calls
  no longer crash on a failed request. Watchlist was unaffected.

v0.2.428
- MDBList "No Account Active" repair: if you connected MDBList but it showed as
  inactive -- the list manager said "No results", the sync monitor kept aborting
  ("No MDBList Account Active"), and marking watched didn't sync back -- it is now
  fixed automatically on the next start. POV keys the whole MDBList account on the
  stored username, and a connect that couldn't capture it (a transient network
  blip at pairing time) left the account looking empty. We re-fetch the username
  from MDBList once and restore it. Direct scrobbling was never affected (that
  only needs the key), which is why some titles still synced.

v0.2.427
- MDBList watched-sync fixes (when MDBList is your Watched Status Provider):
  (1) your MDBList API key is no longer written to kodi.log -- POV logged the
  full request URL, apikey and all, on any MDBList error; it is now redacted.
  (2) "Mark as watched" now clears the paused state on MDBList and counts the
  title in your Watch Stats. POV was posting to a scrobble/clear endpoint that
  returned 404, so the title stayed "paused" and never registered; we now send
  a scrobble/stop at 100%, which MDBList treats as finished (marks watched and
  clears the resume). Applies to POV 6.x; harmless no-op otherwise.

v0.2.425
- MDBList can now be paired by scanning a QR code from your phone, like the
  other services -- until now it was the only one that required typing the API
  key by hand into Kodi. In "My Services" pick MDBList and either scan the QR
  and paste the key on your phone (the phone page verifies it against MDBList
  first, with the same iOS/WebKit paste fix used for Gemini pairing) or type it
  in Kodi. Connecting and disconnecting now mirror POV's own MDBList setup
  exactly -- account name, token, and the watched-status provider -- and a
  mistyped or unverifiable key is never saved.

v0.2.424
- iPhone Gemini pairing fix: the "pair from phone" page now works on iOS. iOS/
  WebKit was silently corrupting a pasted API key in the text field (smart-
  punctuation / autocorrect), so Kodi rejected it with a 401 even though the
  same key worked from a computer or Android. The page now uses a password
  field, reads the raw clipboard on paste, and verifies the key with Google
  directly from the phone before sending it -- so a mangled or invalid key is
  caught on the phone with a clear message instead of a confusing error in Kodi.
  Client-only; the local pairing transport and everything else are unchanged.

v0.2.423
- When the community pool already holds an embedded ("תרגום מובנה") translation for
  YOUR exact release, the local "translate the embedded track" action is no longer
  offered separately -- you get the instant pool copy (shown as "· מאגר קהילתי · 100%")
  instead of re-running the whole extract + AI translate for a result that is already
  one click away. No behaviour change when the pool does not have your release.

v0.2.422
- Further reduced background traffic to the community server: within a single playback,
  the availability check behind the sources-screen badge and the auto-subtitle search now
  SHARE one pool lookup (they run in the same background process, so the first result
  serves the second from a short in-memory cache) -- one request per playback instead of
  two. No change to what you see; purely fewer redundant requests.

v0.2.421
- The Hebrew-availability badge on the sources screen now also reflects community-pool
  translations. The lookup behind the badge was being rejected as unauthenticated, so the
  badge only ever showed human-subtitle availability; it now includes the community AI
  translations too. The add-on also makes far fewer background requests to the community
  server for the same information -- it was querying twice per title and re-checking every
  few hours, and now queries once and caches a known result for longer.
- Fix: a completed AI translation is now recorded reliably in usage stats -- the last
  translation of a viewing session could previously be missed. No user-visible change.

v0.2.420
- Fix: SDH song lines showed an ugly garble ("gimel-TM-x") at the start/end of a line
  instead of a music note. Downloaded subtitles were always decoded as legacy Hebrew
  (cp1255), which corrupted the ONE non-ASCII character in an English caption -- the
  music note -- while leaving the English text intact (so it slipped through and was
  even translated-through into the shared pool). Subtitles are now decoded as UTF-8
  first, falling back to cp1255 only for genuinely legacy files, so the music note
  survives. Already-corrupted lines (including ones already in the community pool) are
  also repaired on read, so the garble disappears for everyone who downloads them.

v0.2.419
- Fix: an English (or mixed) subtitle that merely CONTAINED a little Hebrew -- e.g. a
  Hebrew "translated by..." credit line -- was misdetected as "already Hebrew" and
  passed through UNtranslated. It showed the source untranslated and silently cached
  it, so every retry discarded that cache and did nothing (an endless no-op that read
  as "said it's translating but never does"). Field case: on one movie the English
  embedded track never translated while the Russian one did. The "already Hebrew"
  check now measures the Hebrew share of the WHOLE source and only skips translation
  when Hebrew clearly dominates -- so English/mixed sources translate as expected.
- Hardening: notifications now fire on their own short-lived thread so a wedged GUI
  (e.g. during heavy debrid extraction) can never stall a translation; the toast
  count is bounded. Adds a "language ok, mode=..." diagnostic log.

v0.2.418
- More translation progress logging (cast / prompt / blocks milestones) to pin down
  a translation that stalls before it starts sending chunks. Diagnostic only -- no
  behaviour change.

v0.2.417
- Fix: a very large subtitle (e.g. a long SDH / embedded track) could silently fail
  to translate when "Translate whole subtitle in one request" was ON -- the single
  request overflowed the model's output limit, got truncated, and the whole result
  was discarded as empty, with no progress shown. Such large sources now
  automatically fall back to chunked translation (with the normal progressive
  display), so they translate reliably.
- Added translation progress logging (chunk count + mode + first-chunk-returned) so
  a stalled translation can be diagnosed from the log instead of being a black box.

v0.2.416
- "RTL base" is now the DEFAULT RTL rendering mode (was opt-in in 0.2.414-0.2.415,
  now verified on-device). Hebrew subtitles get correct placement of numbers,
  English names, quoted English, parentheses AND end punctuation out of the box --
  no setting to change. If a player does not honour the directional marks, switch
  "RTL punctuation fix direction" back to "Reverse" (the previous default, which
  only fixes the sentence period). Purely a rendering change; nothing is sent to
  the network and the community pool is unaffected (every mode adapts shared text
  on read).

v0.2.415
- "RTL base" mode polish: a username / handle that ends a Hebrew sentence on its
  own wrapped line (e.g. "Modelbehavior36.") now shows its trailing period on the
  correct side. Such single-token Latin tails are wrapped too so the player's BiDi
  places the period correctly; multi-word English lines and names that genuinely
  start with a dot (".NET", ".exe") are left untouched. Only affects the optional
  "RTL base" mode; the default is unchanged.

v0.2.414
- New optional RTL rendering mode "RTL base" (Settings > RTL punctuation fix
  direction). It wraps each Hebrew subtitle line in an explicit right-to-left
  embedding so the player's own BiDi puts numbers ("50 miles"), English names
  ("cutie-patotie87"), quoted English ("Model behavior") and parentheses on the
  correct side -- not just the end-of-sentence punctuation the default handles.
  This is a root-cause fix for setups whose base text direction defaults to
  left-to-right. It is OPT-IN because it relies on the player honouring the
  directional marks: turn it on and verify on your device; the default
  "reverse" mode is unchanged.

v0.2.413
- SDH detection is now stricter, to stop false "SDH (מדויק למגדר)" labels: a
  subtitle is marked SDH ONLY when the release name has a whole-token "SDH" /
  "Hearing Impaired" marker, or its CONTENT was detected as SDH (dense speaker
  labels / sound cues). The subtitle provider's own hearing-impaired flag is no
  longer trusted -- it flagged ordinary subs as SDH, and "has sound cues" is not
  the same as "has speaker labels" (what the gender-accuracy claim needs).

v0.2.412
- SDH-aware AI translation, Phase 3b: content-detected SDH subtitles are now
  shared through the community pool, so a subtitle one user found to be SDH is
  preferred + labelled "SDH (מדויק למגדר)" at everyone else's pick list too --
  even before they download it. Opt-in (rides the existing pool share/use
  settings), cost-frugal (deduped, fetched at most once every 3 days), and it
  reads a local cache on the pick path so it never slows subtitle search.

v0.2.411
- SDH-aware AI translation, Phase 3: an SDH (hearing-impaired) subtitle is now
  also recognised from its CONTENT -- a subtitle densely marked with bracketed
  sound cues, ALL-CAPS speaker labels and music glyphs -- even when the provider
  didn't flag it and the release name doesn't say "SDH". The check is deliberately
  conservative (a regular subtitle is never misclassified), runs after download,
  and remembers the release locally so next time it's offered it sorts first and
  is labelled as the gender-accurate pick.

v0.2.410
- Idan Plus self-heal now runs FIRST at startup (before any other build repair),
  so a corrupt channel file is healed before you can navigate to the addon.
- Fixed the quick-update notification popup: the notification file had two
  entries, and the wizard's parser expects exactly one, so it raised
  "too many values to unpack" and showed no notification. Back to a single entry.

v0.2.409
- Idan Plus (עידן פלוס): fixed the failure where no channel loaded or played and
  nothing was even shown. idanplus keeps its channel map in displayChannels.json
  as a JSON object; if that file is ever corrupted (a partial/interrupted write),
  its own reader falls back to an empty LIST and every channel lookup then dies
  with "'list' object has no attribute 'items'" -- and the rebuild path crashes on
  the same list, so it can never recover on its own. A startup self-heal now moves
  a corrupt displayChannels.json aside so idanplus rebuilds a fresh list from its
  server (its own reset does exactly this), and best-effort hardens idanplus so a
  future corruption rebuilds instead of crashing. No-op if idanplus isn't
  installed; a valid channel file (including your renames/reorder) is never touched.
- SDH-aware AI translation, Phase 2: ALL-CAPS speaker labels (e.g. "MABEL:") in the
  source are now kept through to the model, so it can match them to the TMDB cast
  and choose the correct זכר/נקבה form per line, then drop the tag from its Hebrew
  output. Any tag it fails to drop is now stripped ONLY from a line that actually
  became Hebrew -- so an English caption/chyron/URL the model deliberately left in
  place ("WARNING: ...", "PART 2: ...", "HTTP://...") is never eaten -- and the
  interim English placeholder no longer shows raw speaker tags.

v0.2.408
- Cross-language embedded align now reads the video's head + Cues index ONCE and
  slices the timing skeleton per track, instead of re-reading it per language on
  the fallback -- fewer debrid requests when the picked language has no synced
  external subtitle (safer on a strict token like TorBox).
- SDH / hearing-impaired subtitles are now detected and preferred for AI
  translation: they carry the COMPLETE dialogue (and speaker labels), so they are
  the most complete source and the best for זכר/נקבה accuracy. Embedded: the
  Matroska FlagHearingImpaired flag + track name are read from the head (free,
  works on a strict token -- it is NOT the full extract), and an SDH track is
  preferred when translating its own text (never as a mere timing skeleton, so
  the alignment path is unchanged). External: an SDH sub (provider flag, or a
  WHOLE-TOKEN "SDH"/"Hearing Impaired" release marker -- never a false match like
  "Highlander"/"Chihuahua") sorts first within its language and is labelled
  "תרגום AI לעברית · SDH (מדויק למגדר)".

v0.2.407
- Embedded (תרגום מובנה) translations are now tracked on the owner dashboard.
  They already run through the exact same AI + gender pipeline as every other
  translation -- so they were always counted, just never MARKED as embedded.
  Each AI translation now tags whether it was embedded-sourced, letting the
  dashboard surface them in the recent feed, per-title, and in an embedded-only
  gender-coverage view. Telemetry-only; no user-visible behaviour change.

v0.2.406
- Multiple embedded translations of the same title are now ordered by gender
  accuracy, and each shows the language it was translated FROM. Hebrew renders
  speaker gender; the gender-reference chain covers most lines, but on the rest
  the AI falls back to the source text's own gender -- so a source that marks
  gender (Spanish/French/Russian/Arabic/Hindi...) is more accurate than English
  or German (which don't mark predicative gender and must guess). Strong-gender
  sources are listed first, e.g. "תרגום מובנה AI (ספרדית)" above "(אנגלית)".

v0.2.405
- Embedded relabel now also fixes titles clicked on 0.2.403. That build wrote the
  ai_emb one-shot marker WITHOUT actually posting the promote (its _post still had
  the old dedup pre-check), so after updating, the relabel would have been skipped
  forever for any title a 0.2.403 user already opened. The marker suffix is bumped
  (.emb -> .emb2) so those stale markers are ignored and the promote fires exactly
  once. No user action needed -- just re-pick "embedded translation" once.

v0.2.404
- Embedded pool relabel actually lands now. The community-pool "תרגום מובנה AI"
  upgrade could never reach the server: pool._post short-circuits the upload when
  the source is already pooled (dedup), so the ai_emb "promote" signal was
  swallowed and the entry stayed labeled plain AI. ai_emb contributions now
  bypass that pre-check (still one-shot per file via the .emb marker; the server
  dedups by hash, so no duplicate row -- only the kind is upgraded). Requires the
  pool worker's promote logic (already deployed).
- Clearer cross-language fallback message. When you pick an embedded track in a
  language whose only external subtitles are CAM/other-release (they don't sync
  to the embedded timeline and are correctly rejected), the add-on falls back to
  a language that does sync (usually English) and now says so explicitly ("אין
  כתובית מסונכרנת ב<שפה> — מתרגם מ<שפה>") instead of a confusing "from cache".
- Exhaustive synced-source search. When you pick an embedded track, the aligner
  now tries EVERY external subtitle candidate for a language (was capped at 3)
  until one syncs -- release-NAME similarity isn't timing similarity, so a
  lower-ranked release can be the best timing match. No download cap; the search
  is bounded only by the existing 45s time ceiling (and pause/stop), and the
  picked language is searched first.

v0.2.403
- Embedded pool item polish. The community-pool "תרגום מובנה AI" row is surfaced
  only for your EXACT source, so it now shows "100%" (like the regular AI rows)
  instead of a blank percentage that looked broken beside a "· 100%" sibling for
  the same release. Also, re-picking "embedded translation" for a title whose
  Hebrew was already cached now UPGRADES the pool entry to embedded ('ai_emb'):
  the early-cache-hit backfill contributes the embedded kind under its own
  one-shot marker, so a file previously shared as a plain AI translation gets
  relabeled server-side instead of being skipped by the "already shared" guard.
  Client-side only; no change to the translation itself.

v0.2.402
- Embedded translation now works in EVERY language, not just the ones whose
  2-letter code happens to prefix their 3-letter one. The picker hands a 2-letter
  ISO 639-1 code (es, de, nl, ja, sv, el, zh, ...) but a Matroska track tags its
  language with the 3-letter ISO 639-2/B code (spa, ger, dut, jpn, swe, gre,
  chi, ...); the old prefix match silently failed for ~20 languages, so clicking
  "embedded translation" in e.g. Spanish or German never found the right track,
  fell through to English, and served English's cached Hebrew instead of a fresh
  translation from the language you actually picked. Track selection now
  canonicalizes both codes (ISO 639-1 / 639-2B / 639-2T) before matching, and no
  longer hands back a lone English text track for a different-language request.
  Fully fail-open: any miss still defers to the external-subtitle path.

v0.2.378
- Pool efficiency (client side): a single browse/play flow used to call the
  pool's /lookup several times for the same title (picker + sync_map + the
  contribute pre-check). They now share one short-lived (90s) in-memory
  response cache, collapsing them into ONE request per title -- fewer Worker
  requests, same results (negative results cached briefly too; fully
  fail-open). AUTO sync reports (/syncrep) are also de-duped client-side (the
  same verdict isn't re-sent for 6h); human delay-fixes are never throttled.
  No behavior change for users -- purely fewer redundant network calls.

v0.2.377
- Fixed (POV Trakt add crash -- the REAL root cause, from a second field crash
  log): the crash was NOT POV's Trakt widget-refresh setting (that path never
  ran -- zero "Widget Refresh Performed" lines in the log). It was OUR OWN
  patch: pov_combined_discover_patcher used to rewrite POV's container_refresh()
  to also fire UpdateLibrary(video,special://skin/foo) so AF3 home widgets
  reload after mark-watched. But container_refresh() runs from ~30 sites incl.
  every Trakt add (indexers/trakt_api.py); that ping reloads ALL POV home
  widgets at once -> concurrent router.py on POV's reuselanguageinvoker
  interpreter -> CPython dict corruption (SystemError: dictobject.c:1756) ->
  native crash. Removed the ping from the discover patcher and added
  pov_container_refresh_crash_fix to revert it on already-patched devices
  (restores stock Container.Refresh, cycles POV so it applies this session).
  FENtastic already reloads its widgets on Container.Refresh, so nothing is
  lost. The 0.2.376 setting-guard is kept as harmless defense-in-depth.

v0.2.376
- Fixed (POV Trakt add crash, confirmed from a field crash log): adding a
  title to a Trakt watchlist/favourites/collection could crash Kodi to the
  Android home screen. Chain: POV's SyncMonitor, when the setting
  "Attempt to Refresh Widgets After Refresh" (trakt.sync_refresh_widgets) is
  ON, runs UpdateLibrary(video,special://skin/foo) after the sync -> every
  home widget reloads at once -> many POV router.py invocations run
  concurrently on POV's reuselanguageinvoker interpreter -> CPython dict
  corruption (SystemError: Objects/dictobject.c:1756) -> native crash. A new
  guard (pov_widget_crash_guard) forces that single setting OFF at startup,
  but only when it is actually on -- widgets then refresh on the next
  navigation instead of in a crash-inducing burst. No POV source files are
  touched.

v0.2.375
- Fixed (POV Anime Hebrew, the real root cause): POV renders its main menus
  from a STORED copy in navigator.db (seeded once from menu_lists.py) and an
  in-memory Window cache -- so patching the .py only helped fresh installs and
  existing devices kept the English anime menu forever. The patcher now also
  rewrites the anime labels directly in navigator.db (AnimeList submenu +
  RootList 'Anime' entry, all list types) and clears POV's in-memory menu
  cache, so the anime section turns Hebrew this session. Only anime rows and
  only the name tokens are touched; user menu edits and all other lists stay
  byte-identical.

v0.2.374
- Fixed (POV Anime Hebrew, field log + screenshots): on POV 6.07 the anime
  breadcrumb titles stayed English because navigator.py moved from indexers/
  to menus/ between POV releases -- the patcher now handles both locations.
  And because POV runs with reuselanguageinvoker, the freshly-patched menu
  files were only picked up on the NEXT restart (so an updated device still
  showed the anime menu in English); the patch now cycles POV so it applies
  the same session, like the other POV code patchers.

v0.2.373
- Improved: Gemini API calls now authenticate via the x-goog-api-key HEADER
  instead of the ?key= query parameter. Google's newer 'AQ.'-prefixed keys
  reject the query-param method with 401; the header method works for every
  key type. One credential per request (never both).
- New (POV): the Anime section is now in proper Hebrew on every skin -- the
  anime menu (trending/most watched/popular/recent/genres/years for both
  series and movies), the root Anime entry, the Anime Calendar label and the
  anime year/genre breadcrumb titles. Genre names inside were already Hebrew
  via the existing genres patcher. Self-healing on every start, so a POV
  self-update cannot revert it.

v0.2.372
- Fixed (standalone, field log + screenshot): the addon disabled itself
  entirely ("no subtitles found") on Kodi installs whose PLAYBACK subtitle
  language names a non-Hebrew language, even when Hebrew was wanted. The
  language gate now treats Hebrew in "languages to download subtitles for"
  as the strongest signal (it wins over the playback preference), and the
  standalone service one-shot-seeds Hebrew into Kodi's subtitle-language
  settings on first run (a later manual change sticks).
- Fixed: every search on engine-enabled installs logged "EXCEPTION: Unknown
  addon id 'service.subtitles.All_Subs'" -- the DarkSubs hook check now
  skips entirely when the engine is on (DarkSubs is deliberately disabled
  then), mirroring the service-side gate.
- New: automatic subtitle search is now SKIPPED for live/IPTV playback --
  PVR channels, streams with no duration, and a configurable addon
  exclusion list (Settings -> engine; default: plugin.video.idanplus), so
  channel zapping no longer triggers the Hebrew search overlay.

v0.2.371
- New: Gemini 3.5 Flash is now available in the model list (Settings ->
  Translation -> Model). NOT the default -- 3.1 Flash Lite stays the
  recommended default; pick 3.5 Flash if you have quota to spare.
- New: the gender-reference feature now falls through a PRIORITY CHAIN of
  languages when no Arabic subtitle exists (or none aligns): an out-of-sync
  human HEBREW sub first (the strongest oracle -- it IS Hebrew), then Arabic,
  then Spanish / French / Russian / Italian / Portuguese / Polish / Ukrainian /
  Hindi / Czech / Romanian / Greek / Bulgarian / Serbian / Croatian / Slovak /
  Urdu / Dutch. Each language's block teaches the model that language's gender
  markers; machine-translated Hebrew is never used (poisoned oracle). One
  engine search covers the whole chain; still fully fail-open.
- New (standalone): the repo-channel addon now runs the SAME auto-on-play
  Hebrew search as the full build -- making MoranSubs a complete PRIMARY
  subtitle addon on a clean Kodi with independent POV/Umbrella (like DarkSubs):
  embedded-Hebrew detection, human sources, community pool, and AI translation
  when no Hebrew exists. The machinery moved verbatim to
  resources/lib/autosub_service.py (one source of truth for both editions);
  the full build's behavior is unchanged.

v0.2.370
- Improved (SubSync): small-offset acceptance now leans on OVERLAP + magnitude,
  not the coarse "vote". Four correct small offsets for The Flash pinned it down:
  the right shift ran vote 54-64%, overlap 87-90%, tight 40-45% -- and one at 54%
  vote was still being rejected on the vote floor. Since every spurious match is
  a LARGE offset (already blocked by the magnitude-scaled bar), a SMALL shift is
  now accepted whenever the overlap corroborates strongly (>=85%), with vote kept
  only as a low >50% majority floor. This lets these genuinely-off community subs
  self-sync while large/low-overlap/noisy matches stay rejected. Verdict cache
  bumped.

v0.2.369
- Improved (SubSync): apply small real offsets more reliably. Two field subs for
  the same episode made the tuning precise: a genuinely-correct -436ms (64% vote,
  87% overlap, 40% tight) that was still being rejected, vs. a garbage +230s
  match (51% vote, 31% tight). The garbage is always a LARGE offset, already
  rejected by the magnitude-scaled bar; so for SMALL shifts (<1.5s) the tight
  floor now drops to ~35% when BOTH the vote (>=60%) and overlap (>=85%)
  corroborate. Different-subber subtitles cap around ~40% tight even at the
  correct offset, so this lets the real small fix apply while large/weak/noisy
  matches stay rejected. Verdict cache bumped.

v0.2.368
- Fixed (SubSync): auto-sync now actually APPLIES a small, real offset that a
  different-subber reference can only loosely confirm. Real subtitles from
  another translator split their lines differently, so even at the CORRECT
  offset the "tight" agreement caps around ~45% -- which the previous floor
  rejected, leaving a genuinely ~1s-early sub unfixed even though the offset was
  found (field: The Flash Pilot, -954ms, 89% overlap, 45% tight). The tight
  requirement is now relaxed for SMALL shifts (<1.5s) when the overlap strongly
  corroborates (>=85%). This is safe: a small shift is low-harm, and the aligner
  only picks a small non-zero offset when the sub is genuinely off (an
  already-synced sub resolves to "confirmed" and is never touched). Large shifts
  keep the strict, magnitude-scaled bar from 0.2.364, so the old spurious -20s
  case is still rejected. Verdict cache bumped.

v0.2.367
- Improved (SubSync): prefer an ENGLISH timing oracle. The Hebrew subtitle is
  almost always translated from English, so an English reference sub splits its
  lines the same way and its cue onsets line up -- while a foreign-language
  oracle of the same release (e.g. a Dutch sub) segments differently and tanks
  the tight-agreement check (field: The Flash Pilot got a real -926ms offset but
  only 45% tight against a Dutch oracle, so the fix was correctly rejected).
  pick_oracle now prefers English within the same match tier (tier still wins
  first, so this never downgrades the match), which should let the genuine small
  offset align tightly and apply. Verdict cache bumped so affected episodes
  recompute and re-pick the oracle.

v0.2.366
- Improved (SubSync): make the same-source oracle alignment actually land the
  small real offset it finds. When aligning against a same-source (BluRay/DVD)
  oracle in a different LANGUAGE, cross-translation cue segmentation depresses
  the coarse "vote" metric -- a genuine ~1s offset (field: The Flash Pilot,
  -926ms) scored 61% and was dropped just under the 65% floor, and a full scale
  search on the cross-language oracle produced a spurious FPS fit (+560s). Now
  same-source oracle alignment is pinned to identity scale (same disc master =
  same framerate) and uses a relaxed vote floor, with the graduated tight-
  agreement gate from 0.2.364 as the real correctness guard -- so a genuine
  small offset applies while a large/loose one is still rejected. The tight
  agreement is now shown in every sync diagnostic. Verdict cache bumped.

v0.2.365
- Improved (SubSync): auto-sync can now actually FIX small real offsets on files
  that previously found no timing reference. It now accepts a same-source-class
  oracle for PHYSICAL-DISC masters -- a BluRay sub anchors a BluRay REMUX even
  when the codec/group differ (REMUX/NOGRP vs x264/ROVERS), because they come
  from the same disc master and share timing. This matters for season-pack-named
  files ("...S01..." with no episode token), where every episode-matched sub
  scored just below the old exact/group-only bar, so subsync had nothing to
  anchor to and fell back to the noisy embedded-track probe -- leaving a
  genuinely ~1s-early sub unfixed (field: The Flash Pilot on a BluRay REMUX).
  WEB/HDTV still require an exact/group match (their "same class" can be
  different services/airings with different timing), and every offset is still
  verified by the graduated tight gate from 0.2.364, so a wrong reference is
  rejected. Verdict cache bumped so affected episodes recompute and pick up the
  now-eligible oracle.

v0.2.364
- Fixed (SubSync, important): auto-sync could push an ALREADY-SYNCED subtitle
  many seconds out of sync. On files whose embedded-subtitle "file probe" yields
  few, mixed-language cues (BluRay REMUX with many forced foreign PGS tracks),
  the aligner could land on a spurious large offset (field: a 31-cue union voted
  -20.3s at only 68% tight agreement) and the old gate applied it, de-syncing a
  sub that needed no more than a few ms. Three coordinated fixes:
  * The tight-agreement check is now REQUIRED for every non-trivial shift (not
    only sparse references) and scaled by the size of the jump -- a multi-second
    move must clear ~85-93% agreement, a small nudge stays lenient. A genuine
    offset easily clears it; a coincidental peak (~68%) is rejected and the sub
    is delivered untouched. Small ms-level corrections are unchanged.
  * A large shift from an AUTO (machine) community-pool record is no longer
    applied blindly -- only human-confirmed records jump a sub by many seconds;
    otherwise the device re-verifies locally. This also neutralises any spurious
    auto record already shared before this fix.
  * The verdict cache version is bumped so any shift stored under the looser
    gate recomputes and is dropped if it can't clear the new bar.
  Net: subsync only moves a subtitle when it is highly confident, and never
  turns a good sub into a badly-timed one.

v0.2.363
- Fixed: the "synced" mark added in 0.2.362 used a check-mark glyph (✓, U+2713)
  that is missing from some skin fonts (NOX among them), so it rendered as an
  empty tofu box instead of a tick. Replaced it with the plain ASCII word
  "SYNC" ("HEB NN% SYNC", or "HEB SYNC" when there's no name match), which
  renders in every font on every skin. Same bright-green highlight, same
  meaning; no glyph risk. Nothing else about the badge or syncing changed.

v0.2.362
- Added (SubSync final polish): the source screen now marks releases the
  community has CONFIRMED as having a synced Hebrew subtitle. The existing
  "HEB NN%" badge gains a green tick and turns bright green ("HEB NN% ✓", or
  "HEB ✓" when there's no name match) for exactly those releases, so the pick
  that is verified in-sync stands out at a glance. Purely visual — it changes
  nothing about how syncing works, adds ZERO network requests (the confirmed
  set rides the /lookup the availability warm already makes and is cached next
  to the release names), and the tick is an LTR symbol so it never disturbs the
  badge layout on any skin. Only human-CONFIRMED records are shown; provisional
  auto verdicts are not surfaced as synced. This completes the subtitle-sync
  feature (S1-S5 + community registry); no server/pool change was required.

v0.2.361
- Fixed: the home/favourites screen reverted to default on every quick update.
  The startup favourites patcher carried three "forced restore" migrations
  (full canonical surface, Premiumize status tile, personal tiles) that were
  meant to repair, once per device, an old buggy reseed that had wiped the
  per-service network rows. While active they also RE-ADDED tiles the user had
  deliberately deleted, so each update looked like it reset the home screen back
  to default. That repair is long finished for the whole fleet, so all three
  forced restores are now retired: deletions are honoured permanently, on every
  skin (nox, fentastic, estuary, arctic fuse 3). The only remaining canonical
  restore is the already-disabled heuristic, so a missing tile is treated as
  intentional user customisation. Action-repair fixes (debrid/torbox tiles) and
  clean-install seeding from the build zip are unchanged.

v0.2.360
- Fixed: Disney+ (and other) MOVIE tiles showed only 2-3 unrelated films. The
  0.2.359 param-key fix un-hung the tiles, but the query correction did not
  actually apply on many devices: it matched POV's whole query line exactly,
  and any small difference in POV's surrounding params made it silently no-op,
  leaving `with_companies=<id>`. Netflix (company 8 -> 44 films) looked okay;
  Disney+ (company 337 -> exactly 3 unrelated films) looked broken. The patch
  now rewrites only the minimal `with_companies=%s` fragment to watch-provider
  discovery (adding the REQUIRED watch_region), independent of POV's other
  params -- verified against TMDB: Netflix ~4680 movies, Disney+ ~1560.

v0.2.359
- Fixed: Netflix/Disney (and other streaming-service) MOVIE tiles on the home
  screen spun forever and never loaded. Root cause: the movie tiles pass a
  TMDB watch-provider id via network_id (Netflix=8, Disney+=337...), but
  POV's native movies.py maps the tmdb_movies_networks action to the 'company'
  param -> params_get('company') is None -> `if not function_var: return`,
  which exits the list builder BEFORE end_directory() is ever called, so Kodi
  waits on the directory forever (the infinite spinner). Two fixes:
  * The movie-networks param key is normalized to 'network_id' (matching every
    other network tile in the build) in pov_menus_patcher and the bundled
    native movies.py, so the id is actually read and the folder finishes.
  * The TMDB query is corrected from `with_companies=<id>` (which treated the
    provider id as a production-company id -> ~44 unrelated films) to
    watch-provider discovery `with_watch_providers=<id>&watch_region=US&
    flatrate` -- verified against TMDB: Netflix now returns its ~4600-movie
    catalogue, Disney+ ~1500. This also explains why the earlier 0.2.305 try
    "hung": it fixed the query but not the param key, so the tile stayed empty
    and spun. Series-by-network tiles were already correct and are unchanged.

v0.2.358
- SubSync: sub-100ms offset precision. The alignment histogram bins offsets
  at 500ms, so a computed fix could over/under-shoot the true offset by up to
  half a second -- field case: a real ~+1s offset landed on the +1500ms bin
  and left the subtitle ~0.5s early after the fix. The offset is now REFINED
  after the coarse peak: the median of the actual per-cue deltas nearest the
  peak, which lands within ~50ms of the truth. Applies to every anchor
  (oracle, file probe, audio). Existing fixes recompute (verdict cache).

v0.2.357
- SubSync: removed the "couldn't verify sync" toast entirely. It was firing
  on subtitles that were ALREADY perfectly synced on screen (the verification
  simply couldn't PROVE it -- e.g. a high-bitrate 2160p file whose embedded
  reference is too sparse to sample densely), which read as a failure when
  nothing had failed and was exactly the kind of toast noise this build works
  to avoid. Policy now: SubSync speaks only when it actually APPLIED a timing
  fix ("synced automatically") -- a sub it can't verify is delivered silently
  as-is, unchanged, no toast.
- Sparse container-probe references (few cues within the byte budget on
  high-bitrate files) now use identity-scale-only alignment like the audio
  anchor, so a handful of points can't be fit to a spurious FPS stretch
  (field: 16 cues -> bogus scale=1.0427/-77s). A dense reference keeps the
  full FPS-scale search.

v0.2.356
- SubSync probe: PTS-origin rebase -- the root cause of "synced subtitle
  fails verification" on certain remuxes/re-encodes. Field case: an AI-AV1
  BluRay whose container timestamps START AT ~313s (the source disc's PTS
  origin, preserved by the muxer). Players rebase to the first timestamp at
  playback -- so the subtitle looked perfectly synced on screen -- but the
  probe read RAW cluster timestamps and concluded the sub was +313s off
  (vote=90%, overlap=100%: a strong, consistent, WRONG verdict, refused only
  by the implausible-offset cap). The probe now reads the file's FIRST
  cluster timestamp (the playback zero point) and rebases every cue -- both
  the subtitle-track reference AND the audio-segment anchors. Zero-based
  files are unaffected.
- Window/budget balance: bitrate-scaled windows are now also capped so the
  byte budget always spans the WHOLE runtime (field: 8.4MB windows burned
  the 40MB cap after 5 of 10 fractions, so the reference only covered the
  first half of the file).
- Probe cue cache is now versioned (v2): entries stored by the un-rebased
  engine re-probe. Verdict cache v5: UNKNOWNs judged against un-rebased
  references recompute. Regression test reproduces the exact +313s field
  file: the synced subtitle now verifies CONFIRMED.

v0.2.355
- SubSync probe: three field fixes from a Rick&Morty season-pack BDRip where
  a SYNCED subtitle came back "couldn't verify" (spurious -48.5s peak
  refused at tight=22%) and the probe re-ran per subtitle:
  * CRITICAL: the probe cue cache write failed since 0.2.348 ("bytes is not
    JSON serializable" -- the audio feature added raw CodecPrivate to the
    track dict), so EVERY probe re-fetched ~30MB. Track dicts are sanitized;
    results cache again.
  * The reference now UNIONS all non-forced embedded subtitle tracks (they
    share one timeline) instead of sampling a single track -- the field file
    had rus+en+en tracks but only a sparse en signs track was used (9 cues).
  * Windows are bitrate-aware: the head's Duration + file size scale the
    window to span ~8s of media (a fixed 3MB window covers ~2s of a BDRip),
    with an adaptive midpoint top-up when the union is still sparse.
  Verdict cache v4: UNKNOWNs stored by the sparse-probe engine recompute.
  Expected outcome for the field case: dense union -> the synced subtitle
  now verifies CONFIRMED instead of "couldn't verify".

v0.2.354
- SubSync S3: the community sync registry goes live (Worker /syncrep +
  /lookup `sync` field deployed by the maintainer).
  * CONSUME: before any local computation, subsync checks the community map
    for this exact (subtitle, release) pair -- it rides the pool /lookup the
    picker already made, so the common case costs a dict lookup, zero
    requests. A community FIXABLE applies instantly (one gentle toast on the
    first play on this device, then silent); CONFIRMED delivers as-is.
    Client-side plausibility gates mirror the engine's before applying.
  * CONTRIBUTE: every freshly-computed deep verdict (CONFIRMED/FIXABLE) is
    reported once, fire-and-forget, share-gated (pool_share) -- the verdict
    cache guarantees no recompute means no re-report.
  * HUMAN ANCHOR: a new service delay-watcher samples the viewer's manual
    subtitle delay while a MoranSubs sub plays. A settled >=0.4s delay after
    5+ minutes watched becomes a human FIXABLE report (correctly folded with
    any auto-fix already applied); a clean 15+ minute watch becomes a
    CONFIRMED vote. One report per pair per session. Humans override auto on
    the server. This closes the class no algorithm can anchor (dubbed
    re-encodes with no subs anywhere, e.g. the ColdFilm field case): one
    viewer fixes the delay once -- every future viewer gets it instantly.
  pool.py: sync_map()/report_sync() + worker_norm_release() (exact mirror of
  the Worker's key normalization). All existing pool endpoints unchanged.

v0.2.353
- SubSync goes fully background (self-healing delivery). Field complaints:
  the "searching subtitles" overlay stayed up for the whole deep
  verification (10-30s of oracle download / container probe / Gemini audio),
  and the sync toasts never appeared (they went through the translate
  status channel, which autosub deliberately silences).
  * resolve() now delivers the subtitle IMMEDIATELY; deep verification is
    queued to the long-lived service (subsync_queue, same disk-queue pattern
    as the he_warm drainer) -- the overlay drops right away.
  * When the worker proves a fix, it swaps the playing subtitle in place --
    but ONLY if the user still watches the same stream and hasn't picked a
    different subtitle meanwhile (pending-marker check).
  * GENTLE toast policy (most toasts in this build were deliberately
    silenced after user complaints -- this respects that): at most ONE
    notification per (subtitle, release) pair EVER -- either "synced
    automatically (+X.Xs)" when a fix is applied, or "couldn't verify sync"
    when a fresh deep verification came up empty. Repeat plays, cached
    verdicts, trusted tiers and nothing-to-verify cases are all SILENT.
    Toasts go directly through kodi_utils.notify (respecting the master
    subs_notifications switch), bypassing autosub's quiet mode.
  Cached verdicts still apply instantly and synchronously as before.

v0.2.352
- Verdict-cache version bump (v3): UNKNOWN verdicts stored by 0.2.350/351
  (before the adaptive second pass existed) are recomputed, so an already-
  judged (subtitle, release) pair gets its pass-2 chance instead of being
  short-circuited by the cached refusal. The audio-cue cache is reused, so
  the recompute costs no new downloads or Gemini calls beyond pass 2 itself.

v0.2.351
- SubSync audio anchor: adaptive second pass. When the coarse histogram
  finds a STRONG peak (vote>=80%) that fails only the tight +/-450ms check
  -- i.e. promising but under-determined by too few speech cues -- the probe
  now samples TWO additional audio positions once, merges the speech cues
  with the cached first pass, and re-judges. The extension is marked in the
  probe cache so it never repeats per release. Gemini prompt sharpened to
  demand word-onset precision (two decimals) -- the tight check depends on
  it. Field context: the ColdFilm case now correctly REFUSES the spurious
  +72s peak (tight=20%), and the second pass gives genuine alignments a
  fair chance to reach confidence instead of giving up at 10 cues.

v0.2.350
- CRITICAL SubSync fix: a spurious alignment could shift subtitles out of
  sight. Field case: a sparse 10-cue audio reference against a dense
  (600-cue) candidate reported vote=120% on a bogus offset of -350s -- the
  gate passed and the subtitles "disappeared" (they were 6 minutes late).
  Four-layer fix in the alignment engine:
  * vote counting deduped -- ONE vote per reference cue per histogram bin
    (a dense candidate could previously multi-vote a single ref cue, which
    is how 120% happened);
  * audio references now search identity scale ONLY and a narrow +/-3min
    offset window (a sparse VAD reference cannot support scale estimation;
    every extra scale candidate multiplied the spurious-peak chance);
  * a global plausibility cap: FIXABLE offsets beyond +/-4min are refused
    (real wrong-release desyncs are seconds, recuts are refused anyway);
  * sparse references (<40 cues) additionally require 65% of ref cues to
    agree at a TIGHT +/-450ms tolerance -- random matches at that tolerance
    are rare, killing peaks that survive the coarse 500ms bins.
  Verdict-cache version bumped: verdicts stored by the old engine
  (including the field device's bad -350s entry) are ignored and recomputed.
  Regression tests reproduce the exact field failure (must refuse) and the
  genuine sparse case (must still fix).

v0.2.349
- SubSync S5 quota hardening (free-tier discipline, same care as the
  translation path): a Gemini failure (429 quota / key / network) during the
  audio anchor now STOPS the remaining segment calls immediately instead of
  burning two more, and -- critically -- no longer caches a permanent
  "no audio anchor" negative for the release: quota exhaustion is transient,
  so the next play (e.g. tomorrow, quota refreshed) gets a fresh chance.
  Genuine negatives (no AAC track / Gemini answered but found no speech)
  are still cached so they never cost calls again. No key = the audio tier
  is skipped before any network work; every other tier works keyless.

v0.2.348
- SubSync S5: AUDIO as the last-resort timing anchor. The field case
  (From.S04E10.ColdFilm) turned out to have NO embedded subtitle tracks at
  all (dubbed re-encode: video + Russian audio only), so S4's file probe had
  nothing to anchor to either. Now, when no matching sub exists in any DB
  AND the file has no embedded subtitle track:
  * mkv_probe.audio_segments extracts 2-3 short CONTIGUOUS AAC audio clips
    (~20s each) by demuxing cluster blocks (full lacing support: Xiph/
    fixed/EBML) and wrapping raw frames as playable ADTS -- no decoding,
    hard byte/time caps, longest-contiguous-run selection so a window gap
    can never skew timestamps.
  * gemini.generate_media (new, separate from the translation path) sends
    each clip to Gemini with the user's existing key and gets back speech
    intervals; those become reference cues on the file's own timeline.
  * subsync verifies/retimes the Hebrew sub against them with a slightly
    relaxed gate (VAD boundaries are softer than subtitle cues). Works for
    dubbed audio too -- dubs are lip-synced, so speech happens at the same
    moments. AAC only (Gemini can't take AC3/DTS; a secondary AAC track is
    used when present). Gated by subsync_audio (default on) + requires a
    Gemini key; results (and negatives) cached per release; fail-open.
  Anchor chain is now: matched sub -> embedded track -> audio -> honest
  no-op. E2E test reproduces the exact ColdFilm scenario end to end.

v0.2.347
- SubSync S4: the playing FILE itself is now a timing anchor. New
  mkv_probe.py reads the EMBEDDED subtitle track's cue TIMESTAMPS straight
  out of the playing MKV via HTTP Range requests (debrid direct links) --
  head + ~10 small sample windows, hard-capped at 40MB / 25s, no decoding,
  works for text AND bitmap (PGS) tracks, any language, forced tracks
  skipped. When no release-matched subtitle exists in ANY database (the
  field case: From.S04E10.1080p.ColdFilm, a re-encode absent from every
  subtitle DB -- best candidate 46%/fuzzy), subsync now probes the file and
  verifies/retimes the chosen Hebrew sub against the file's own embedded
  track. Probe results are cached per release (including "nothing there"),
  so the ranged reads happen once. Gated by the subsync_probe setting
  (default on); fail-open as always.
- sync_align: scale tie-break now prefers the identity map -- neighbouring
  FPS ratios (23.976/24=0.999) could tie with 1.0 inside the histogram bin
  tolerance and accumulate seconds of drift by the end of a long movie.
- Offline test suite for the probe: synthetic Matroska builder + a real
  Range-supporting HTTP server + the full probe->verify->retime->CONFIRMED
  loop, plus an end-to-end simulation of the exact ColdFilm field scenario.

v0.2.346
- "Last watched" source now actually stays at the TOP of the source list.
  source_capture.reorder moved it to the top and badged it, but the
  quality/size re-sort injected by pov_source_quality_patcher (0.1.373) runs
  AFTER the reorder inside make_items and pushed it back down (seen in the
  field at position 80/212, badge intact). The re-sort has always exempted
  rows flagged '_pin_top' -- but nothing ever set that flag; reorder now pins
  the remembered source, so it keeps the top spot and is also exempt from the
  filter-drop.
- SubSync diagnostics: when no timing oracle is found, the log now lists the
  5 closest candidates with their score/tier plus the addon version, so field
  logs distinguish "genuinely no matching release" from a scorer gap or a
  stale interpreter.

v0.2.345
- SubSync field fix: releases with an UNTAGGED source (e.g. Russian groups
  like ColdFilm that ship no WEB/BluRay token in the name) could never
  anchor a timing oracle -- a real device hit "no oracle for
  From.S04E10.1080p.ColdFilm (88 foreign candidates)" and skipped the
  auto-retime. Two scorer upgrades:
  * same release GROUP now reaches the trusted GROUP tier even when the
    source tag is missing on one/both sides (contradicting sources still cap
    LOW as before);
  * a normalized release name fully CONTAINED in the other (provider
    decorations like a trailing ".rus") now scores 96/GROUP -- after the
    edition/source/PROPER contradiction guards, which containment never
    bypasses.
  Regression tests added for the exact field case. No other behaviour
  changes.

v0.2.344
- SubSync S2: automatic subtitle re-timing. When a delivered Hebrew subtitle
  (engine human pick or community-pool entry) belongs to a DIFFERENT release
  than the one playing, it is now verified against a timing oracle -- a
  release-matched subtitle in any language from the engine's cached search --
  and, when a confident linear map exists (offset and/or FPS drift), a
  RETIMED copy is delivered instead, with a single toast
  ("הכתובית סונכרנה אוטומטית"). New modules:
  * sync_align.py -- language-agnostic aligner generalized from
    arabic_gender.py's production estimator: voting-histogram offset search,
    FPS-ratio scale candidates, overlap-rate confidence gate, SRT retiming,
    credit/watermark filtering, oracle selection.
  * subsync.py -- delivery orchestration: release tier-check (matching
    releases are trusted and untouched), per-device verdict cache keyed
    (subtitle content, playing release) so each pair is computed once,
    fail-open at every step (any problem delivers the file exactly as
    before), gated by the subsync_verify setting (default on).
  Recuts/extended cuts are detected and REFUSED rather than mangled. Pool
  links now carry their source release name for the tier check. No pool /
  worker / translation-engine changes.

v0.2.343
- SubSync S1: honest sync percentages everywhere. A new structured release
  scorer (release_match.py) replaces the three divergent token-similarity
  scorers behind the subtitle picker %, the source-screen HEB badge and the
  engine's result ordering -- all three now show the SAME number, and the
  number reflects sync likelihood instead of raw text overlap:
  * identical release -> 100%; same release group + source -> ~90%+;
  * same source class (e.g. two different WEB rips) -> mid range;
  * contradicting source (WEB vs BluRay), edition (Extended vs Theatrical)
    or PROPER state -> capped LOW (the old scorer gave such pairs a
    misleading 60-75% on token overlap alone);
  * synthesized player filenames are recognized and never treated as a real
    release identity.
  Badge look/format unchanged -- same colors, same position; only the number
  is now truthful. Also sidesteps a legacy scoring bug where the video's
  token arrays were mutated across iterations, skewing later results. Each
  wiring point keeps the old scorer as an import-failure fallback. No
  pool/translation behaviour changes.

v0.2.342
- User customizations now survive updates and restarts. An audit of every
  startup patcher found four mechanisms that silently reverted a user's own
  changes -- the classic "every update resets my home screen / settings"
  complaint -- and all four now seed their defaults ONCE per device and then
  leave the user's choice alone:
  * AF3 home layout (HomeSwitcher modes/vertical) and the CustomRating rows
    were re-forced on every quickfix rebuild, resetting a customised Arctic
    Fuse 3 home to Standard after each update. Now seeded once (same pattern
    as the spotlight seed); only infra settings (TMDbHelper etc.) stay forced.
  * Audio language (English), keyboard layouts and the two FENtastic home
    settings (favourites button, choose-OSD-player) were re-forced on EVERY
    Kodi start, reverting users who changed them. Now a one-time seed.
  * FENtastic Movies/TV-Shows page widgets: a widget row the user deleted in
    the skin's widget editor was force-re-inserted on every start. Inserts are
    now a one-time seed; the old->new URL migrations (which only match our own
    baselines) still self-heal every start.
  * POV scraper tune (prerelease/3D/piratebay) now remembers the value it
    wrote per key, so a future re-tune never overrides a key the user changed
    deliberately.
  No pool/translation/subtitle behaviour changes in this release. Existing
  devices apply each seed once more after this update (their markers don't
  exist yet), then never again.

v0.2.341
- Corrected the "My Movies / My Series" home tiles for the current POV build.
  The previous version misdiagnosed the empty tiles: the merged-list actions
  (tmdb_my_tvshows / trakt_my_tvshows and the movie equivalents) ARE valid on
  the current POV -- they are the UNION lists we inject into POV's menu
  (TMDB favorites+watchlist; Trakt collection+watchlist+favorites). The tiles
  looked empty only because that injection had not gone live in POV's warm
  interpreter yet. So the tiles now point back at the merged actions (adding an
  item to EITHER underlying list makes it show in the tile), the POV tile uses
  POV's real "favorites" spelling, and the startup patcher now cycles POV once
  after injecting the merged menu so the tiles fill in the same session instead
  of after the next restart. Devices that took the previous version are repaired
  in place -- name-anchored, so only these tiles change and any tile you
  customised or deleted is left alone; no duplicates. Seed fixture corrected too.

v0.2.340
- Fixed empty "My Movies / My Series" home tiles (TMDB / Trakt / POV). The
  tiles invoked POV plugin actions that POV doesn't have (trakt_my_tvshows,
  tmdb_my_tvshows, favorites_tvshows, and the movie equivalents), so they opened
  EMPTY while POV's own menu was full. Corrected to POV's real actions
  (trakt->trakt_collection, TMDB->tmdb_favorite, POV->favourites_tvshows/
  favourites_movies), for both movies and shows. The patcher now also REPAIRS an
  already-broken tile in place (previous versions treated the wrong action as
  "present" and never fixed it); correct and user-customised tiles are left
  untouched, and no duplicate tiles are created. The seed fixture was corrected
  too so fresh restores are right.

v0.2.339
- Fixed a crash when adding a search result to a list (TMDB/Trakt/MDBList/
  favourites). The add-refresh introduced earlier re-ran the OPEN container
  after an add; when that container was a search-results list, re-fetching it
  crashed the screen ("Error getting plugin://"). The refresh now skips search
  containers (a refresh there wouldn't show the added item anyway) and still
  refreshes normal browse lists on add. Most visible on Arctic Fuse 3, whose
  search feeds a browsable results list. The guard is applied to both the
  unified list manager and the local favourites path, and upgrades installs
  that already carried the un-guarded refresh.

v0.2.338
- Sources flagged to always stay at the top (a truthy '_pin_top' flag on the
  source, e.g. built-in-language sources) are now kept at the top and exempt
  from the quality upgrade, the resolution-filter drop, and the quality/size
  re-sort added in 0.2.333-335. Previously the re-sort reordered such sources
  by quality/size like any other; now they keep their position at the top.

v0.2.337
- Auto-on-play subtitle search now honors the one-shot 'skip_autosub' marker
  (set when the item being played is already in the target language), matching
  the subtitle-search path. Previously only the search path checked it, so the
  service-side auto-on-play player could still run for flagged content.

v0.2.336
- Estuary home no longer jumps to the first tile after an update (real fix).
  Root cause found: on a post-update boot, three patchers freshly patch
  Estuary's player-OSD XML (VideoOSD.xml) and each called ReloadSkin(), which
  rebuilds every window -- and Estuary/FENtastic's home menu is a fixedlist
  (defaultcontrol 9000, focusposition=0) that then snaps focus to the first
  tile. None of those reload paths preserved focus (the earlier pov_reload
  focus-restore was correct but on a different code path, so it couldn't help).
