  ReloadSkin() is now skipped whenever the home screen is showing: the OSD XML
  is re-read by Kodi on the next OSD open anyway, so nothing is lost and the
  focus jump is gone. (Applies to Estuary, FENtastic and NOX reload paths.)

v0.2.335
- Quality fix is now filter-aware (fixes "I hid 4K but 4K still shows"). The
  0.2.333/334 fix relabelled SD rows to their true resolution AFTER POV's own
  resolution filter had already run, so a source really 4K but mislabelled SD
  (which slipped past the filter) reappeared as 4K once we corrected its badge.
  Now the patcher reads your quality filter (results_quality_movie/episode):
  if the corrected resolution is one you allow it upgrades the badge; if it is a
  resolution you filtered OUT it DROPS the row entirely. Net result: 1080p is
  still shown correctly (not as SD), AND hiding a resolution actually hides it,
  even for rows POV had mislabelled. Never downgrades; genuine SD stays SD.

v0.2.334
- Quality fix now also keeps the ORDER correct. Because POV sorts the source
  list by its stored quality (upstream), a row we upgrade from SD to 1080p would
  otherwise stay stuck in the SD region (a "1080P" badge sitting below an "SD"
  row). The patcher now, once per window, upgrades the mislabelled rows AND
  re-orders the whole list by quality high->low then size (GB) high->low --
  matching the default "Quality, Size" order -- so 4K first, then 1080p (GB
  descending), 720p, then SD, with no SD stranded among the 1080p rows and no
  720p above 1080p.

v0.2.333
- Quality badge fix: a source whose NAME clearly reads 1080p/2160p/720p is no
  longer shown with an SD badge. POV derives the badge from a scraper-supplied
  name_info field (or the URL), not from the release name on the row, so a
  well-named release could still land on SD. A new patcher re-checks the visible
  name with POV's own get_release_quality and upgrades the row ONLY to a real
  resolution (4K/1080p/720p) -- upgrade-only, it can never downgrade a row or
  invent a quality the name doesn't contain. Same source-results window as the
  Hebrew-match badge, compile-checked so it can't break POV.

v0.2.332
- Estuary home no longer jumps to the FIRST tile after the startup refresh.
  Root cause: the focus-restore captured the on-screen SLOT via
  Container(id).Position, which on Estuary's home is a fixedlist pinned to
  slot 0 -- so it always reported "0" and restored focus to the first tile.
  It now captures the ABSOLUTE selected index (Container(id).CurrentItem) and
  restores with SetFocus(...,absolute), which round-trips correctly on
  Estuary's fixedlist as well as regular lists/panels on FENtastic/NOX.

v0.2.331
- Reverted the 0.2.330 change per request: "Include Pre-Release Results"
  (CAM/SCR/TELE) and "Include 3D Results" are restored to ON. The default-ON
  "piratebay" provider stays ON (more sources). This does NOT touch the
  separate "1080p-named source shown as SD" report, which is a display issue
  tracked on its own.

v0.2.330
- Source list now matches a clean POV: the build shipped POV with
  "Include Pre-Release Results" (CAM/SCR/TELE) and "Include 3D Results" turned
  ON, which flooded the results with low-quality rows a clean POV hides -- this
  is the "lots of SD in our build vs clean POV" report. Both are now turned OFF,
  and the default-ON "piratebay" provider (which the build had left off,
  reducing source counts) is turned back ON. Applied once, only where the value
  still differs, so any manual change you make afterwards is kept. The foreign/
  single-audio filter is deliberately left off so Hebrew-audio releases are not
  dropped. The 4K/1080p/720p labels themselves were never wrong -- POV derives
  them by regex from the release name, identical code on a clean POV and here.

v0.2.329
- Built-in Hebrew badge now matches reliably: a source's built-in flag is stored
  at play from the file's 'name', but the source row is scored by its 'URLName'
  (POV makes those differ), so a just-played release could drop to "HEB NN%"
  instead of "HEB BUILT-IN 101%". The badge now scores the row against BOTH
  identifiers and takes the best, so it lights up whichever field matched.
- Home screen no longer jumps back to the FIRST tile after the startup refresh:
  applying POV patches cycles POV (disable/enable), which rebuilds the home tiles
  and lost your focused tile. Your focused tile is now captured before the cycle
  and restored after it.

v0.2.328
- Fixed POV getting STUCK when you press BACK on the "Resume / Play from
  beginning" prompt (all skins - NOX/Estuary/FENtastic/AF3). Picking a source
  for a mid-watched title opens a modal resolving window; POV's player showed
  the resume prompt over it, and pressing BACK ('cancel') returned WITHOUT
  closing that window - so the UI froze and needed a full Kodi restart. The
  cancel path now closes the dialog(s) first (self-healing POV patcher).

v0.2.327
- Diagnostics for the built-in Hebrew badge: logs the exact cached built-in list
  the source screen reads (embedded_names), what merge_embedded writes at play,
  and the match score per candidate row (built-in check) -- to pinpoint the one
  source that still isn't flagged despite correct detection.

v0.2.326
- Built-in Hebrew badge no longer gets wiped by a background refresh: a source's
  built-in-Hebrew flag detected at play was written to the local cache, but the
  next availability warm overwrote the whole cache entry with the pool's list --
  which didn't have that release yet -- erasing the just-detected flag (so the
  source you played kept showing a % instead of BUILT-IN). The warm now UNIONS
  the built-in list with what's already cached instead of replacing it, since a
  built-in track is a permanent fact. Added media-id fields to the diagnostic log.

v0.2.325
- No more recurring "הורדה נכשלה (ktuvit)" popup during playback. When Ktuvit
  "refuses" a single background/secondary download (its rate-limit "request not
  found" response), the subtitle you actually picked still loads -- but the
  failure was still popping a notification every time. It's now logged only (the
  exact reason stays in the log). Same for the rare "unsupported source" popup.

v0.2.324
- Built-in Hebrew badge: wider detection + diagnostics. Embedded tracks labelled
  with a descriptive name ("Hebrew SDH", "Forced Hebrew") or Hebrew script
  ("עברית") -- which a language-code lookup missed -- are now recognised. Added a
  one-line "embedded-report" log at play (Hebrew detected? which release was
  stored, from which field) so any remaining source that isn't flagged can be
  diagnosed precisely instead of guessed at.

v0.2.323
- Built-in Hebrew badge FINALLY works (root cause fixed): the embedded-Hebrew
  track WAS being detected at playback, but the release name a debrid stream
  carries in ListItem.FileNameAndPath (li_filename) was invisible to
  _release_from(), so both the pool report and the local write-through got an
  empty release and silently did nothing -- the source screen never learned the
  release had built-in Hebrew, locally OR from the shared pool. _release_from()
  now consults li_filename (basename, debrid-token-guarded) like _release_ready()
  already did, so playing a built-in-Hebrew source now flags it BUILT-IN for you
  immediately and for everyone via the pool.

v0.2.322
- Home tiles no longer come back after you delete them: the "one-time" forced
  restores of favourites tiles (service / personal / full-build) were gated on
  XML comment markers that Kodi strips whenever favourites are edited, so they
  re-fired on every update and re-added deleted tiles. They now persist their
  "already restored once" flag in the JSON sidecar Kodi never touches, so each
  forced restore fires exactly once ever and deletions are respected forever.

v0.2.321
- Built-in Hebrew badge: when a source's embedded Hebrew track is detected at
  play, its release is now written through to the local availability cache
  immediately (merge_embedded), so the source screen flags it "HEB BUILT-IN
  101%" the moment you go back -- instead of only after the next background
  warm re-read the pool. The pool report still propagates it to everyone else.

v0.2.320
- Maintenance and stability improvements.
- Hebrew-availability %: fixed the two real causes the first-entry % was still
  missing (and the source window felt slow):
  (1) the background availability worker now starts at the very beginning of
      Kodi startup, so it is already running when you play something right after
      an update/restart -- before it started too late and the first title's check
      sat waiting several seconds;
  (2) the check now stores its fast result (pool + Wizdom + OpenSubtitles) right
      away and does the slow כתובית/Ktuvit lookup in the background, so the source
      window is no longer held up by it. The % is ready on the first entry and
      the window opens promptly.

v0.2.319
- Maintenance and stability improvements.
- Hebrew-availability %: the source window now briefly waits (up to ~3s) for the
  availability answer before showing the list, so the % is reliably there on the
  FIRST entry -- including titles whose Hebrew lives only on OpenSubtitles/כתובית
  (previously those showed only on the 2nd/3rd entry). Titles already known to the
  community pool still show instantly with no wait.

v0.2.318
- Maintenance and stability improvements.
- Hebrew-availability %: preload the lookup engine when the service starts (so
  the first check of a session isn't slowed by a one-time cold start), poll the
  warm queue more frequently, and add precise timing diagnostics to the log so
  the first-entry timing can be measured exactly.

v0.2.317
- Maintenance and stability improvements.
- Hebrew-availability %: made the first-entry check reliably ready in time. The
  availability warm now runs inside the already-running background service
  (started via a tiny on-disk hand-off) instead of booting a fresh helper
  process each time -- the old helper took a few seconds just to start up, which
  was slower than the source scrape, so the % often only appeared on the 2nd/3rd
  entry. The warm also queries the sources concurrently now. No extra lookups.

v0.2.316
- Maintenance and stability improvements.
- Hebrew-availability %: now shows on the FIRST entry for titles whose subs are
  on OpenSubtitles/Ktuvit too -- the availability check now starts together with
  the source scrape (running in parallel), instead of only when the source list
  opens, so it's ready in time. No extra lookups.

v0.2.289
- Default translation chunk lowered 100 -> 50 lines. Live full-subtitle tests
  (Super Mario Bros. 872 lines, From S03E09 924 lines, both with the real Arabic
  gender reference) showed 50-line chunks translate with ZERO content blocks and
  no loss of gender accuracy or quality, whereas 100-line chunks of graphic
  dialogue block. Smaller requests stay under Google's volume threshold, so most
  translations never hit a block at all (the strip + surgical bisection remain
  as safety nets). Existing installs are moved to 50 once via a marker-gated
  migration (a smaller manual choice is left untouched).

v0.2.288
- UX: shortened the long on-screen notifications. Several toasts lasted 8-15
  seconds (some error/status messages up to 15s) and lingered annoyingly; all
  toasts are now capped at 5 seconds.

v0.2.287
- BLOCK FALLBACK is now SURGICAL: if a chunk still gets blocked, we no longer
  drop the Arabic gender reference for the whole chunk. Instead we bisect while
  KEEPING the Arabic, isolating the specific offending line(s); only that line
  (at size 1) falls back to English-only (or source), while every other line in
  the chunk keeps its Arabic gender oracle. Previously one bad line cost the
  entire chunk its gender (and the dashboard showed e.g. "100 entries dropped
  Arabic" when a single line was the culprit).

v0.2.286
- BLOCK FIX (the real one, validated live against the API): the prompt-level
  PROHIBITED_CONTENT block is driven by the VOLUME of explicit terms in one
  request. The English subtitle alone translates fine, but the Arabic gender
  reference REPEATS those same explicit words (rape/torture/slur) and tips it
  over. Since the Arabic is only a gender oracle -- and the gender of such lines
  is clear from context anyway -- we now OMIT just the explicit Arabic reference
  lines from the prompt. Confirmed: gender stays correct (the hard feminine
  forms came out right) while the block trigger is removed. The whole-chunk
  drop-Arabic -> bisect -> keep-source cascade stays as a safety net.
- top_p is now always sent (default 0.95), for all models. Tested decisively:
  top_p has ZERO effect on the block (a blocked prompt blocks identically with
  top_p unset / 0.9 / 0.95 -- the block is decided on the INPUT before sampling)
  but it shapes output quality, so it's kept explicit and consistent instead of
  left to the model default (earlier builds skipped it on Gemini 3).

v0.2.285
- TELEMETRY (dashboard only, anonymous as always): each translation now reports
  per-chunk outcome -- how many entries went through WITH the Arabic prompt, how
  many fell back to English-only (Arabic dropped on a blocked chunk), how many
  were kept as source -- plus how many prompt-block events were hit. The owner
  dashboard uses this to show, for a translation that hit a block on some chunk,
  exactly how much was translated with Arabic vs without.
- TELEMETRY FIX: failed translations no longer count as successful "ai_ar" in
  the dashboard breakdowns/top-titles -- the new-Arabic % now counts only
  translations that actually produced a subtitle.
- TELEMETRY FIX: a failure now reports the real cause + detail (e.g. the actual
  Gemini error string) instead of a bare "abort:error", and the dashboard no
  longer shows the misleading Arabic-alignment "ok" next to a failure.

v0.2.284
- TUNING (based on live A/B tests against the API): PROHIBITED_CONTENT prompt
  blocks come in two kinds -- FLAKY (the same prompt passes on a retry) and
  PERSISTENT (the same prompt blocks every time, driven by the sheer VOLUME of
  explicit content in one request). Tests proved a text disclaimer does NOT
  help (generic or film-specific, in systemInstruction or in the prompt -- all
  still blocked), and that what actually escapes the block is reducing volume:
  dropping the Arabic block and bisecting. So same-prompt retries were cut from
  10 to 3 -- enough to catch the flaky case, without stalling ~70s on a
  persistent block before degrading to the real fix (drop Arabic -> bisect ->
  keep source). Blocked chunks now resolve much faster.

v0.2.283
- FIX (continued): a PROHIBITED_CONTENT prompt block is usually FLAKY -- the
  exact same prompt frequently succeeds on a retry (observed live). So a blocked
  chunk is now retried with the SAME prompt up to 10 times (modest waits) BEFORE
  degrading. Only if all 10 attempts block do we drop the Arabic gender hint,
  then bisect, then keep source. This keeps the Arabic gender quality on chunks
  that were only blocked by a transient hiccup, instead of losing it on the
  first block.
- The transient subtitle shown WHILE a translation is still running is now named
  after the release (e.g. "Movie.2024.1080p.a") instead of the opaque
  "progressive_<hash>_a". Clean-addon users no longer see a hash in the subtitle
  picker mid-translation; the final delivered name was already clean.

v0.2.282
- The custom "בחר כתוביות" window (non-FENtastic skins) now PAUSES playback while
  it's open and resumes when you close it -- matching the native dialog (the
  video used to keep playing behind it).

v0.2.281
- FIX (continued): a translation could still be blocked by Gemini at the PROMPT
  level (promptFeedback blockReason = PROHIBITED_CONTENT) -- not overridable by
  safety settings. The Arabic gender block adds Arabic dialogue to the prompt,
  which can trip Google's prohibited-content policy. Now, when a chunk is
  prompt-blocked, it is retried WITHOUT the Arabic block first (you keep the
  English->Hebrew translation, just without the Arabic gender hint for that
  chunk), then bisected, and at worst that one line is left as source. So a
  blocked chunk never aborts the whole subtitle.

v0.2.280
- FIX: AI translation could abort mid-way with "no candidates (filtered)" -- a
  chunk with profanity/violence/sexual dialogue was being blocked by Gemini's
  safety filter. Subtitle translation now sends safety = BLOCK_NONE (we only
  translate existing dialogue), and as a safety net a still-blocked chunk is
  bisected and, at worst, that one line is left in the source language instead of
  failing the whole subtitle. No more aborted translations on strong content.

v0.2.279
- Telemetry: each AI translation now also reports its duration. The owner
  dashboard adds an "avg translation time" card and a "daily (last 14 days)" bar
  graph (green = used the Arabic path). Requires re-deploying the pool Worker.

v0.2.278
- Telemetry v2 (owner dashboard): each AI translation now reports WHY it used the
  method it did -- for fallbacks, whether the Arabic option was off, no Arabic
  subtitle was found, or the Arabic didn't time-align (with the scale/vote/overlap
  detail). The dashboard adds a "why fallbacks happened" breakdown, per-source-
  language and per-version breakdowns, a failures list, and clarifies the success
  metric. (Requires re-deploying the pool Worker.)

v0.2.277
- Subtitle chooser v2 (non-FENtastic skins): the custom window is now FULL-SCREEN
  and far more polished -- Tal-inspired fanart background, poster + plot, a wide
  two-line results list with language flags and a colour-coded match %, and a
  smooth focus highlight. Fixed per-row colours not rendering (now baked into the
  labels). FENtastic still uses Tal's own native window.

v0.2.276

  subtitle window (kept exactly as he designed it); on every other skin (NOX /
  Estuary / Arctic Fuse 3) it opens our new custom window with the same gorgeous
  look. Best of both -- Tal's real design where it exists, ours everywhere else.

v0.2.275
- "בחר כתוביות" is now a brand-new, gorgeous custom window (Tal-inspired) that
  looks the SAME on every skin (FENtastic / NOX / Estuary / Arctic Fuse 3): the
  title's fanart + poster background, a clean two-line list with a language flag,
  bold title line + dim release line, a colour-coded match % on the right, and a
  smooth focus highlight. It's a self-contained window bundled in the add-on
  (no per-skin dependency), so it no longer falls back to each skin's plain
  download dialog. Picking AI still shows the source immediately and swaps Hebrew
  in the background. Also fixed the NOX subtitle button still calling the old
  All_Subs add-on.

v0.2.274
- "בחר כתוביות" now opens in the gorgeous redesigned subtitle window (on
  FENtastic, Tal's DialogSubtitles; every other skin uses its own native one),
  instead of the basic pyxbmct list. It shows the same candidates -- language
  flag, name + match %, and now a meaningful star rating derived from the match %
  (101%/100% = 5 stars, 60% = 3...). Picking an AI-translate option still shows
  the source immediately and swaps in the Hebrew in the background (non-blocking).

v0.2.273
- Internal: anonymous usage telemetry for AI translations (owner-only). Each
  translation reports, best-effort and fully guarded, which method was used --
  the new Arabic-gender path (ai_ar), a fallback to plain when no Arabic aligned
  (ai_fallback), or plain because the option was off (ai_plain) -- plus title,
  source language and success. The only identifier is a random per-install id;
  no account/IP/paths. Powers a private stats dashboard on the pool Worker. Never
  blocks or affects translation.

v0.2.272
- Fix (FENtastic, all player styles): the pause info panel still covered the
  "בחר כתוביות" chooser. Kodi doesn't report a script window via

  shared window property while it's open and the skin's pause panel hides on that
  property -- reliable. The panel returns the moment you close the chooser.

  (2) the player OSD (top/bottom bars) now auto-hides after ~4 seconds of no

  gated migration when FENtastic is the active skin; a later manual change in the
  skin settings sticks). The pause-info panel fix is in the skin window itself.

v0.2.270
- "בחר כתוביות": restored the Hebrew match % on every row (it had moved to the
  list's right column, which pyxbmct doesn't render, so it vanished on device).
  The % is back inline and highlighted in bright white so it stands out. Also
  refined the colour palette (gold/green = ready Hebrew by match strength, amber
  = foreign-to-be-translated, magenta = currently applied) alongside the flag
  icons. Same on every skin (FENtastic, Estuary, NOX).

v0.2.269
- "בחר כתוביות" chooser, much clearer: each row now shows a real FLAG icon for
  the subtitle's language (bundled images that render on every skin), instead of
  the symbols that showed as empty boxes in some skins. Foreign-language rows
  also get a Hebrew language tag (e.g. "[אנגלית]") and an orange tint, so it's
  obvious at a glance which subtitles are foreign (and will be AI-translated)
  versus ready Hebrew (gold/green). Match % stays on the right; the colour/flag
  legend was rewritten with no font-dependent glyphs.

v0.2.268
- Tuned the Gemini 3 translation settings (validated with real A/B tests on the
  free flash-lite model): temperature back to Google's recommended 1.0 (lowering
  it degrades Gemini 3's reasoning), thinking level set to MEDIUM, and top_p left
  at the model default. The old effective setup ran at the HIGH thinking default,
  which burned up to ~15k thinking tokens per chunk -> hit the output cap ->
  truncated/duplicated lines. MEDIUM finishes cleanly, uses ~8x fewer thinking
  tokens (much easier on the free 250K/min quota), and was the MOST accurate on
  gender in testing (From episode 93%, Mario 100% on clearly-marked lines; no
  truncation). One-shot migration moves existing users to temp 1.0 + thinking
  medium, but only if they were still on the old defaults -- a manual choice is
  kept.

v0.2.267
- The Arabic gender reference is now ON by default for everyone (it tested
  clean: gender accuracy ~27% -> ~90%+ with no loss of translation quality, and
  a full silent fallback when no Arabic aligns). One-shot: it's force-enabled
  once even if you'd turned it off before; a later manual opt-out still sticks.
- Arabic is now fetched LAZILY: the first aligning Arabic release is the only
  one downloaded (instead of pre-fetching four), and the Arabic language filter
  is restored to OFF right after, so stray Arabic results no longer leak into
  your normal subtitle searches.
- "בחר כתוביות" chooser: redesigned rows -- a found-count header with a
  colour/symbol legend, taller rows, a per-row category symbol (★ human / ◆ AI /
  ■ built-in / ● current) and the match % moved to the right column.
- Built-in (embedded) Hebrew now shows 101% on the source-selection screen in
  every skin (was 100%), so it always sorts to the very top.

v0.2.266
- NEW (opt-in, OFF by default): "Improve gender accuracy using an Arabic
  reference". Turn it on in MoranSubs settings. When on, an AI translation also
  fetches a human Arabic subtitle for the same title (OpenSubtitles / SubSource
  / YIFY), time-aligns it, and uses it ONLY as a per-line gender oracle -- Arabic
  marks he/she + verb/adjective gender almost 1:1 with Hebrew, which English
  can't. In our tests this lifted gender accuracy from ~27% (cast-only) to ~90%+
  with no loss of general translation quality (a strict "use Arabic for gender
  only, translate the English" rule keeps wording faithful). Works for ANY source
  language (English/Spanish/German/Russian/...).
- It's robust: tries several Arabic subs, each must pass a strict time-alignment
  confidence check (handles different releases / frame-rates / SDH); if none
  align, it silently falls back to the normal translation. Logs each step so you
  can confirm it ran. Sound-effect/music lines are filtered (no gender there).
- Re-translation + upgrade with NO duplicates: clicking an English sub that only
  has an older (non-Arabic) Hebrew translation re-translates it with the Arabic
  reference and shares the upgraded version to the pool (preferred for everyone);
  if the upgraded version already exists it's pulled straight from the pool/cache
  -- never re-translated, never duplicated.

v0.2.265
- AI translations now keep the SOURCE subtitle's real release name. The
  delivered Hebrew file is named after the full release (e.g. "Movie.2010.1080p.
  BluRay.x264-GROUP") instead of a random "progressive_<hash>_final", so Kodi
  shows the real name in the subtitle list.
- That same real release name is now sent when sharing an AI translation to the
  community pool (live AND via "share my cached translations") -- it's stored in
  a small ".release" file next to each cached translation. Previously the pool
  got a generic "Title.Year", which gave almost no match-% for people who
  download AI subs from the pool; now they get a proper release to match against.

  the last subtitle jumps to the first, and Up on the first jumps to the last,

  everywhere). The "הורדת כתוביות (Kodi)" and "סגור" buttons are reached with
  Left/Right, and Back still closes.
- FENtastic: applied Tal's player update (update_20) -- refreshed the player OSD

  on every player style and regardless of the "choose subtitles opens Kodi's
  window" skin setting. Before, the button had a second hidden branch (and two

  instead -- so if your setting was on "Kodi window", the button skipped our
  chooser. Now every branch in all five player layouts (and Estuary) is rewired;

  כתוביות (Kodi)").
- If the chooser can't open (e.g. nothing found yet), the button now falls back

- FENtastic: the "החלף מקור" pause now actually works. FENtastic's OSD files use

  separate menu item), and carefully only once per click so it can never toggle
  playback back on.

v0.2.260

  control. The real cause was the NEW "בחר כתוביות" button we'd added: NOX's

  and pushed "החלף מקור" left into the play controls. Fix: we no longer add a

  full native subtitle search/download dialog -- for when you want the regular

  and since it lives inside the window it adds no OSD button (no collision).
- "החלף מקור" pauses the video again before opening the source-selection
  screen, instead of letting it keep playing in the background. Restored on NOX,
  Estuary and FENtastic.

v0.2.255
- Source-screen Hebrew-match % is now refreshed by the actual search: the
  moment the add-on searches a title (on play, or when you open the subtitle
  picker) the real Hebrew releases it finds are written into the badge cache --
  so the % on the poster matches what the picker shows, instead of lagging
  behind (it could read 25% on the poster while the picker had a 33% match).
- Match % now shows on the FIRST entry to a title, on every device: on a cache
  miss the add-on does one quick (capped) lookup to the shared pool, so the
  badge appears from the shared data right away instead of only after that
  device's own background refresh. (A title you've never opened on this device
  used to show nothing the first time.)
- FENtastic skin/player refresh (Tal's update 19).

v0.2.254
- Fixed: built-in (embedded) FOREIGN subtitles -- e.g. French/English baked
  into the file -- were listed ABOVE the Hebrew options. Hebrew now always
  ranks above embedded-foreign; the foreign built-in tracks still appear, just
  lower down.
- Fixed: the Hebrew-match % on the source screen now matches the % shown in the
  subtitle picker. They used two different formulas, so the same source could
  read e.g. 29% on the poster but 33% in the picker -- now both use the same
  calculation.

v0.2.253
- Community-pool sharing is now ON by default for everyone, to grow the shared
  Hebrew pool. With it on, every human Ktuvit Hebrew sub for something you play
  is mirrored to the pool in the background (no need to download it yourself),
  and your AI translations are shared too -- so Hebrew loads instantly for the
  next person. You can turn it off in settings (built-in engine / pool); if you
  do, it stays off.
- Source screen no longer freezes while loading the Hebrew-match %: the
  community-pool/Wizdom lookup is now fully background-warmed (like the
  OpenSubtitles one already was), so the source list opens instantly. The
  badges fill in from a shared cache (the next open is always instant).
- The Hebrew-match % now also covers Ktuvit, via a SHARED registry: the first
  user to browse a title checks Ktuvit once and publishes the result to the
  community pool, so everyone else reads it back without ever hitting Ktuvit
  (~one check per title globally -- it never overloads the shared account). A
  Ktuvit-only title now shows a % for everyone. (Can be turned off.) Only HUMAN
  Hebrew counts toward the % (an AI translation, which can be made for anything,
  doesn't). New releases are re-checked every few hours and KEEP being re-checked
  as long as more Hebrew subs are still appearing (1 today, 3 more tomorrow...),
  so they all show up fast for everyone; a title only settles to a slow check
  once its subs have been stable for a couple of weeks.
- NEW: built-in Hebrew is now shared for everyone. When the add-on detects a
  source that ships an embedded Hebrew track, it flags that release in the
  community pool (automatically, no action needed), so on everyone else's source
  screen that source shows a green "HEB BUILT-IN 100%" badge -- telling you it
  already has Hebrew and is well worth picking. Matched by release name, so it
  works across debrid providers (TorBox / Real-Debrid / ...).

v0.2.248

v0.2.247
- "Remember picked source" now shows the source dialog with your last-played
  source FIRST and marked with a star (⭐), instead of silently auto-playing
  it and skipping the dialog. So you can re-pick it in one click -- or choose a
  different one, and that becomes the new remembered source for next time.
  Opt-in (the existing "Remember picked source" setting); off by default, so
  nothing changes for anyone who hasn't enabled it.

v0.2.246
- Fixed "Play from start" resuming from where you stopped. A previous one-shot
  had forced POV "Automatically Resume Playback" to Always, which made even an
  explicit "Play from start" (context menu) jump back to the stop point. It's
  now set back to POV's default (ask), so the resume prompt still appears and
  "Play from start" really starts at 0. One-shot + marker-gated: a user who
  manually wants Always keeps it.

v0.2.245
- Fixed embedded subtitles not appearing in the "Download subtitles" dialog. The
  play-start stream snapshot was kept in the background service's memory, but the
  subtitle dialog runs in a SEPARATE process that couldn't see it -- so embedded
  Hebrew/English never showed when you opened the picker manually. The snapshot
  now lives on a shared Kodi window property both processes read. (Embedded still
  only shows when the file genuinely has an embedded stream; many web/debrid
  releases simply have none.)

v0.2.244
- Fixed: an AI-translated (or any external) subtitle being mistaken for an
  "embedded Hebrew" track. The picker read Kodi's live subtitle-stream list,
  which includes subs WE load -- so after an AI translation it showed a bogus
  "embedded Hebrew 101%", marked it current, and got stuck on it (you couldn't
  switch to other subtitles). Now the embedded streams are snapshotted at the
  moment playback starts -- before anything external is loaded -- so an external
  sub can never be misread as embedded.
- Embedded subtitles in OTHER languages (e.g. English) now show too, as
  selectable "[מובנה] EN" entries (they were silently dropped before; only
  Hebrew was offered). An embedded English no longer counts as "Hebrew exists",
  so the AI-translate options stay available.

v0.2.243
- Ktuvit pool now PREFERRED over live Ktuvit for the same release. When a
  release exists both live (from Ktuvit) and in the pool, the list shows the
  pool copy -- labelled "כתובית · מאגר" -- and hides the slower live one, in the
  same match-% position. The pool copy loads instantly from the channel and
  never hits Ktuvit, so there's no reason to use the live one (exactly as you'd
  expect). A live result still shows normally when it isn't in the pool yet
  (labelled "Ktuvit"), and pooled releases Ktuvit didn't return this time still
  appear. So: "כתובית · מאגר" = from the pool (instant); "Ktuvit" = fetched live.

v0.2.242
- Ktuvit pool uploads now happen immediately, like the AI pool. A finished
  contribution used to sit in the queue until the background service's next
  pass (every 20-60s) before reaching the channel; now queuing one kicks an
  instant background upload -- so the subtitle you just watched reaches the
  Telegram channel right away, exactly like an AI translation does at the end
  of translating. (Still one-at-a-time, deduped, and rate-limit-safe -- the
  shared lock + per-send throttle are unchanged; the periodic pass stays as a
  retry/backfill safety net.)

v0.2.241
- Ktuvit pool: the title you JUST played is now mirrored first. The background
  harvest used to work strictly oldest-first, so a freshly-played movie's
  subtitles waited behind a big title's 20+ queued releases (you'd see nothing
  for the new movie for many minutes). It now processes newest-first, and pulls
  a few more per pass, so what you just watched shows up in the channel quickly
  while older items backfill when idle. (On-device logs confirmed downloads at
  this rate succeed; a failure still just retries.)

v0.2.240
- Ktuvit pool now reliably mirrors EVERY release of a title, not a random
  subset. The old grab downloaded all of a title's Ktuvit subs in a quick
  burst the moment you pressed play -- but Ktuvit rate-/quota-limits downloads,
  so most of them silently failed and were never retried (hence "it only
  uploads some, whenever it feels like it"). Now every Ktuvit release is queued
  to disk on any search (play OR a manual "download subtitles"), and the
  background service pulls them from Ktuvit GENTLY -- a couple at a time,
  spaced out, retrying failures across sessions and even days -- until the
  whole set is in the pool. Nothing depends on you staying on the video, and
  the upload to the channel is still one-at-a-time and deduped. Clear log lines
  added for the whole path (queued N, fed K to upload, uploaded M).

v0.2.239
- New: Ktuvit backup pool. When you open a movie/episode, ALL of its human
  Ktuvit subtitles (every release, not only the one you're watching) are
  mirrored in the background to the community channel -- the same place the AI
  pool lives, but clearly tagged as a human Ktuvit translation, never "AI". So
  the backup grows fast. This means: (1) if Ktuvit ever goes down again, its
  subtitles keep working from the backup, and (2) a release someone already
  grabbed loads instantly from the channel. Ktuvit-pool results appear ABOVE
  the AI pool in the list, labelled "כתובית · מאגר · NN%", with no duplicates
  against a live Ktuvit result. Releases already in the pool are skipped (no
  re-download from Ktuvit, no re-upload), so a title costs nothing once seeded.
  The lookup is folded into the existing pool request -- no extra calls per play.
- Reliable, rate-limit-safe sharing: a subtitle to be shared is now queued to
  disk the instant it's downloaded and uploaded by the background service one
  at a time with a throttle. So it still gets uploaded even if you stop or
  leave the video right after (the old background upload could be cut off), and
  a burst can never trip Telegram's send limit and drop a subtitle. Failed
  uploads retry automatically; nothing is ever sent twice.

v0.2.238
- Faster subtitle loading on first open: the source search now waits up to 10
  seconds instead of 15. Results almost always arrive within 5-8s; the engine
  no longer sits an extra ~5-7s waiting on a slow source that returns nothing.

v0.2.237
- Fixed AI translation that "said it translated but showed blank / the original
  language". The model (flash-lite) sometimes returns an EMPTY response or
  ECHOES the source untranslated; both used to pass the entry-count check and
  get cached + served as "the Hebrew translation" (so re-picking showed
  "from cache" but blank/foreign). Now the output is validated to be real
  Hebrew before it's cached -- if it isn't, we don't cache it, we fall back to
  Google Translate so you still get Hebrew, and otherwise tell you to retry.
  Existing bad cached files (blank/echoed) are detected and re-done automatically.

v0.2.236
- Ktuvit source is back ON by default for everyone (it's working again). Done
  once; if you turn it off yourself afterwards it stays off.
- FENtastic player refreshed again to Tal's latest video-OSD update.

v0.2.235
- Auto-translate on play when there's NO Hebrew at all: if a movie/episode has
  no Hebrew subtitle anywhere (no embedded, no human source, nothing in the
  community pool), MoranSubs now automatically takes the best-matching English
  subtitle and translates it to Hebrew on play -- no need to open "choose
  subtitles". Only when a Gemini API key is connected and AI mode is on, and
  ONLY when there's genuinely no Hebrew (a ready Hebrew sub is always preferred,
  so quota is never spent when one exists).

v0.2.234
- Arctic Fuse 3: your home customizations no longer revert on exit/update.
  Deleting items in the submenu (and search/power menus) is now MERGED like the
  home widgets were -- we keep your removals/additions and never overwrite them.
  The Spotlight (path/target/label, incl. setting it to None) is seeded once on
  a fresh AF3 setup and then left alone, so your change survives every restart
  and update. (Existing users keep whatever spotlight they currently have.)
- FENtastic player refreshed to Tal's latest video-OSD ("switch source" /
  player) rework.

v0.2.233
- Re-enable the built-in engine for everyone, once (rollout v2). Some users who
  updated were left with the engine toggled off, so DarkSubs came back and they
  got no Hebrew translation. This re-forces use_builtin_engine + engine_autosub
  ON a single time (now that the engine SyntaxError is fixed) -> DarkSubs is
  disabled again and MoranSubs translates. If you intentionally turn the engine
  off afterwards, that sticks.

v0.2.232
- THE fix for "only community-pool subtitles + no on-play loading panel": the
  sources-engine bridge had a SyntaxError (a duplicate `global` declaration
  added in 0.2.226), so `import subs_engine_bridge` failed every time -- the
  engine never ran at all, leaving only the community pool, and the autosub
  overlay couldn't load. Removed the duplicate declaration. Now every source
  (Ktuvit/Wizdom/OpenSubtitles/...) and the on-play loading panel work again.
  Also added a real compile() check to the build so a parse-clean-but-compile-
  broken file can never ship again.

v0.2.231
- Fix: subtitle search showed ONLY the community pool (no Ktuvit/Wizdom/
  OpenSubtitles/etc.) and the on-play loading panel stopped appearing. The
  engine's embedded-stream probe and its provider search were sharing one
  try-block, so a single silent failure in the probe aborted the whole engine
  step -- and the failure was logged at DEBUG (invisible). They now run in
  SEPARATE guarded steps (a failure in one can't stop the other), failures are
  logged at WARNING (visible), and a one-line "engine gate" log records whether
  the engine is active -- so the providers run even if the embedded probe trips.

v0.2.230
- CRITICAL fix: "no results" for STREAMING sources (couldn't watch). POV's own
  debrid resolver (resolve_external_sources) crashed with an UnboundLocalError
  ('torrent_id') from its error handler whenever a source failed to resolve
  early (e.g. a torrent not cached on the debrid). That crash aborted POV's
  "try the next source" fallback loop, so a single bad source killed the whole
  list and left you with no playable source and no source dialog. We now harden
  that function (initialise its locals) so it fails gracefully and POV moves on
  to the next source -- fixing both auto-pick and manual source selection. (This
  is a guard around POV's existing error path; compile-checked before applying.)

v0.2.228
- CRITICAL fix: subtitles disappeared from every source (only the community
  pool showed) after the caching update. The 24h result cache was storing
  EMPTY search results -- so a single transient failure (timeout / rate-limit /
  network blip) hid all sources for 24h. Now an empty/failed search is never
  cached, and an existing empty cache entry is ignored and re-searched, so
  affected devices recover immediately. (This matches DarkSubs, which never
  caches an empty result.)

v0.2.227
- Fixed: after the show auto-advanced to the next episode, the subtitle list
  showed no match percentages until you exited and re-entered it. Right after an
  auto-advance the player's release name is still transitioning, so the first
  search matched against an empty name (0%) -- and since results are cached for
  24h, that empty-percent list stuck. Now the search waits for the release name
  to settle, and a search done before it's ready is no longer cached, so the
  correct percentages show on the first open.

v0.2.226
- On-play subtitle search now shows the prominent DarkSubs-style status panel at
  the top of the screen (dark box) with LIVE per-source counts as each source
  reports how many subtitles it found -- restored the missing background art so
  the panel is clearly visible over the video.
- Replaced the success toasts with DarkSubs's in-place status: at the start of a
  movie/episode the top panel now shows "כתובית מוכנה" + the full subtitle name
  (and "(נטענה מהקאש)" when it was served straight from the cache), instead of
  popping a toast for every successful load. Error/abort notifications still
  show as before.

v0.2.225
- Ktuvit source turned OFF again by default for everyone (its downloads are
  failing on the server again). Done once; if you turn it back ON yourself
  afterwards it stays on -- we never force it off again.
- Faster subtitle loading, copied one-to-one from DarkSubs:
  * Persistent downloaded-file cache ("Cached_subs"): a subtitle you already
    picked once is served straight from disk next time (same source + language +
    file) with no re-download -- so re-picking is instant, exactly like DarkSubs.
  * Search-result cache extended to 24h (was 6h), matching DarkSubs. Because the
    on-play auto-search and the in-player "choose subtitles" picker share this
    cache, opening the picker after play returns instantly instead of
    re-searching every provider.

v0.2.224
- Translation modes wired up properly:
  * "Google" in settings now really uses Google Translate (like DarkSubs did) --
    not Gemini. Google output is machine quality, so it is NEVER shared to the
    community pool.
  * When the daily Gemini quota runs out, AI users now auto-fall-back to Google
    Translate (so you still get Hebrew) -- also never shared to the pool.
  * No Gemini API key (or a fresh install): you are guided to connect a free
    Gemini key for real AI-quality translation. (Only API users get the Gemini /
    quota-fallback behaviour; anyone can force Google in settings.)

v0.2.223
- Ktuvit source is back ON by default (its server-side issue is resolved). It's
  re-enabled for everyone once; if you turn it off yourself afterwards it stays
  off.

v0.2.222
- Embedded Hebrew is now auto-applied FIRST on play whenever the file has one
  (it's the best, perfectly-synced subtitle). On play we wait for the embedded
  streams to appear and, if there's a Hebrew ('heb') track, switch to it
  immediately -- before trying any downloaded source.

v0.2.221
- Embedded Hebrew: fixed the error when picking it. Copied DarkSubs's exact
  sequence -- close the dialog, then a 100ms pause, then end -- so Kodi no
  longer shows "download failed" after switching to the embedded stream.

v0.2.220
- Fix: Ktuvit results kept showing after it was turned off, because search
  results are cached for 6h and the old cache still contained Ktuvit. The
  result cache key now includes which sources are enabled, so toggling a source
  on/off invalidates stale results immediately.

v0.2.219
- Ktuvit source temporarily turned OFF by default (it has a current server-side
  problem). Turn it back on in settings if you want; it will be re-enabled for
  everyone once Ktuvit is fixed.

v0.2.218
- Reverted the Ktuvit account back to the previous shared one.

v0.2.217
- Auto-subtitle on play now really falls through: if the best Hebrew source
  fails (e.g. Ktuvit "refused/rate-limited"), it skips the rest of THAT source
  and immediately tries the next one -- OpenSubtitles, community pool, Wizdom --
  applying the first that works, instead of just showing the error. Same
  fallthrough now also applies when you pick a sub manually.

v0.2.216
- "Choose subtitles" button in the player now works on Estuary + FENtastic when
  the engine is on. It used to launch DarkSubs's picker (now disabled, so it did
  nothing); it now opens Kodi's native subtitle window, which runs MoranSubs.
- Ktuvit now uses a dedicated build account instead of the global shared
  DarkSubs account, so it carries far less load (the shared account was
  rate-limited from worldwide use). Password stored only in its encrypted form.

v0.2.215
- Subtitle download failed? It no longer leaves you stuck on it -- MoranSubs
  automatically loads the NEXT available ready Hebrew subtitle (human/pool)
  instead of staying on the failed pick.
- Embedded Hebrew ("תרגום מובנה") now matches the EMBEDDED stream exactly like
  DarkSubs (code 'heb'), so it no longer grabs an external Hebrew sub and
  "switches to nothing" -- it switches to the real embedded track.

v0.2.214
- Source-screen "HEB NN%" badge now uses OpenSubtitles (not Ktuvit) for its
  background lookup. Ktuvit runs on ONE shared, rate-limited account; hitting it
  on every browse pushed it past its limit and broke real Ktuvit downloads
  ("request not found"). Proven by reproducing the flow directly: a single
  Ktuvit download succeeds, rapid/repeated ones get throttled. OpenSubtitles
  uses rotating API keys, so the badge adds no load to that account -- which
  also lets genuine Ktuvit downloads work again.

v0.2.213
- Embedded Hebrew: now re-finds the Hebrew stream at the moment you pick it
  (instead of a stale index captured when the dialog opened), so it actually
  switches to the right stream and shows -- matching how DarkSubs does it.
- Ktuvit: log in FRESH for each download (not the cached login the search
  uses), to rule out a stale cookie making the server answer "request not
  found". Verified our Ktuvit code + credentials are byte-identical to DarkSubs,
  so a remaining failure is on Ktuvit's side.

v0.2.212
- Embedded Hebrew ("תרגום מובנה") now works exactly like DarkSubs: picking it
  switches Kodi's subtitle stream and closes the dialog (Dialog.Close), so it
  applies with NO "download failed" error. (Re-enabled the entry; the previous
  version had hidden it.)
- Ktuvit download code reverted to be byte-identical to the working DarkSubs
  (same poll timing); on a genuine failure the exact Ktuvit server message is
  shown on screen. Verified by diffing our engine against the build's DarkSubs:
  the code is the same, so a remaining Ktuvit failure is server/account-side.

v0.2.211
- Ktuvit downloads: wait longer between download polls (700ms instead of
  100ms). Ktuvit answers "request not found, try again" until the file is
  prepared; polling too fast burned all the retries before it was ready. If it
  still fails, the exact Ktuvit server message is now shown on screen.
- Embedded Hebrew ("תרגום מובנה") is no longer listed as a pickable item: a
  subtitle service can't deliver an embedded stream as a file, so picking it
  always produced a "download failed" error. Embedded subtitle streams remain
  selectable from Kodi's own subtitle button.

v0.2.210
- FIX: Hebrew subtitle downloads from Ktuvit failed with "the received file is
  not a valid subtitle". Ktuvit answers the first download polls with a "request
  not found, try again" message until the file is ready; the server changed the
  wording of that message, so our poll loop no longer recognised it and gave up
  after one try, writing the error text out as the "subtitle". The poll now
  matches the stable part of the message and bails cleanly if it never succeeds.
- FIX: the source-select screen took several seconds to appear. The Ktuvit
  match-% lookup was waiting (blocking) for its result on the first open of a
  title; it now runs fully in the background again, so the source list is
  instant and the % badge fills in on the next render.

v0.2.209
- Engine download robustness: the download path now seeds the engine's
  internal settings itself (a download can be the first engine call after a
  quick update / on auto-play), accepts a valid subtitle even when a provider
  returns a mangled/quoted file extension, and -- when a download genuinely
  fails -- shows the exact reason on screen (unsupported source / server
  returned no file / not a valid subtitle / the error text) instead of Kodi's
  generic "subtitle download failed", so problems are reportable at a glance.

v0.2.208
- The standalone add-on now fetches subtitles on its own via its internal
  sources engine (Ktuvit, Wizdom, OpenSubtitles and more), enabled
  automatically -- so it is a complete sources + AI-translation add-on, not
  only a translation layer on top of DarkSubs. While the engine is on, DarkSubs
  and All Subs Plus are disabled so only this add-on runs; turn the engine off
  in settings and they come back. One-time switch -- a later manual opt-out is
  respected and never forced back on.

v0.2.207
- MoranSubs is now the default for EVERYONE: the built-in sources engine is
  turned on automatically (one-time), DarkSubs + All Subs Plus are disabled,
  and MoranSubs becomes the default subtitle service -- so MoranSubs alone
  does the sourcing, auto-on-play and AI translation, replacing DarkSubs and
  the separate AI add-on. This is a ONE-TIME switch (marker-gated): if you
  later turn the engine off (or any seeded option), it STAYS off -- we never
  force your choice back on. Existing per-source / provider toggles are left
  exactly as you set them.

v0.2.206
- The Ktuvit "HEB NN%" badge now shows on the FIRST time you open a title's
  source list, not only the next time. On a title that isn't cached yet the
  source window waits briefly (bounded, max ~8s) for the background Ktuvit
  lookup to finish so the % is there on this open; after that it's cached for
  7 days and reads instantly with no wait. The wait aborts at once if Kodi is
  closing.

v0.2.205
- Source-screen "HEB NN%" badge now also covers Ktuvit (the largest Hebrew
  source), not just the community pool + Wizdom -- so titles whose Hebrew subs
  exist only on Ktuvit finally show a match %. Ktuvit needs a login + multi-step
  search too slow to run while you browse, so it is fetched ONCE per title in
  the background (fire-and-forget) into a 7-day shared cache; the badge fills in
  on the next time you open that title's source list. Never blocks the source
  browser, and does nothing when the badge feature is off.

v0.2.204
- Fix (recurrence): FENtastic loads but ALL text is blank (only icons show).
  A truncated/blanked skin strings.po blanks every label. v0.2.143 stopped US
  from truncating it, but a file already corrupted (older build, interrupted
  extract, power loss) stayed broken forever because nothing repaired it in
  place. MoranSubs now ships a pristine copy of the skin's he/en strings.po and
  self-heals on every startup: if the live file is missing/empty/much shorter
  than the known-good copy, it is atomically restored -- independent of the
  quick-update extractor. Healthy/newer files are left untouched.

v0.2.203

  in-app code it automatically tries an SMS too. During real login, if the code
  was sent only to the Telegram app you are now asked whether to receive it by
  SMS instead -- so you can log in even without the Telegram app installed.

v0.2.201

v0.2.194

v0.2.191

v0.2.186
- Built-in sources engine, parity pass 2: (1) sync % is now accurate -- it

v0.2.185
- Built-in sources engine, parity pass: (1) sync % is now correct -- it
  matches against the release name from the Tagline like DarkSubs does, so a

  pool entries show a sync % when they carry a release name.

v0.2.184
- Built-in sources engine now actually loads and searches. The hidden engine
  settings weren't getting their defaults (Kodi doesn't apply a default to a
  hidden label setting), so the engine crashed on int('') at import and read
  every language flag as empty. Defaults are now written in code before the
  engine loads, and the engine's int() reads are empty-safe. With the engine
  on, Ktuvit/Wizdom/OpenSubtitles/etc. Hebrew results now appear in MoranSubs.

v0.2.183
- Built-in sources engine: added a "Test the built-in engine (diagnostic)"
  button that reports, per provider, how many results came back (or the exact
  error) for the currently-playing video, plus the Hebrew candidates that
  would be shown. Engine search failures now surface as a notification instead
  of silently showing nothing.

v0.2.182
- Built-in sources engine fixes: it now actually returns the human Hebrew
  sources (Ktuvit, Wizdom, OpenSubtitles, etc.) when enabled -- a stray
  top-level chardet import made the engine fail to load and the results were
  silently dropped. Community-pool entries are now clearly marked "[AI · מאגר]".
  Hidden engine settings no longer render as blank toggles in Expert level.

v0.2.181
- Unification groundwork (Phase B2): the built-in subtitle sources engine is
  now wired in behind a new experimental "Built-in sources engine" toggle in
  settings, OFF by default. When off, behavior is unchanged and DarkSubs keeps
  running as its own add-on. Adds a "Translation mode" setting (AI / Google /
  none) for the upcoming three-mode translation.

v0.2.166
- Home tiles: stop resurrecting personal "my movies/my shows" tiles (Trakt /
  POV) after you delete them. The favourites patcher now remembers which tiles
  you removed and won't re-add them. It tells a real deletion (the tile is gone
  but its TMDB anchor tile is still there) from a skin-switch reseed (which
  restores everything), so it respects deletions without breaking the
  survive-skin-switch behaviour. Covers FENtastic + NOX (shared favourites.xml).
  (First run after this update behaves as before; delete once more and it
  sticks. AF3's separate home nodes will follow.)

v0.2.165
- Remember picked source is now ON by default (new installs via the default;
  existing users via a one-shot migration -- a manual opt-out still sticks).
- "Change source" mid-playback now bypasses auto-pick: if a video is already
  playing, the source dialog is shown so you can switch, and your new pick is
  remembered (overwrites the old one). Auto-pick still fires on a fresh open /
  resume. (POV is reloaded once when the toggle is on so this applies the same
  session; compile-checked, OFF-users untouched.)

v0.2.164
- Remember picked source -- PHASE 2 (auto-pick), still OFF by default. When the
  toggle is on and you've already picked a source for a movie/episode, opening
  it again auto-plays the same source and skips the source dialog (first watch
  still shows the dialog -> that's when it's remembered). Match is exact by
  source hash, with a conservative fallback to a same-quality + same-provider
  cached source; anything less confident falls back to the dialog, and a dead
  remembered source just falls through to POV's other sources. Injected at the
  top of POV display_results(), compile-checked, reverts cleanly, idempotent.

v0.2.163
- Remember-source capture: make it actually fire + log each step. The capture
  is now a tiny stable hook that calls source_capture.py (which logs
  "[remember_source] ..." so capture is diagnosable). And because POV keeps its
  old sources.py warm (reuselanguageinvoker), the patch only applied a restart
  later -- so when the toggle is ON we now cycle POV once (deferred, idle-only,
  re-enable verified) so capture works the same session. Gated by the setting,
  so users with it OFF never get POV cycled. Still capture-only, OFF by default.

v0.2.162
- Fix the "remember picked source" capture hook: the old anchor only matched
  one POV phrasing, so on many installs it reported "didn't match POV version"
  and recorded nothing. Now it anchors on the unique `yield link` site with a
  tolerant match that handles every POV variant (new/old phrasing, and the
  form left after the source-name patcher), still compile-checked so it can't
  break playback. Capture stays OFF by default.

v0.2.161
- Diagnostic for "remember picked source": a "Check remembered sources" button
  (Advanced) shows whether the setting is on, whether POV's capture hook is
  installed, how many sources were recorded, and the most recent one. Helps
  confirm capture works (or pinpoint why it didn't) before building auto-pick.

v0.2.160
- Groundwork (capture only, OFF by default) for "remember the source you
  picked": a new experimental setting "Remember picked source" (Advanced).
  When ON, the source you choose for a movie/episode is recorded
  (name/hash/quality/provider) so a future phase can auto-pick the same or a
  similar one. This release ONLY records -- it changes no playback behavior.
  The POV patch that records it is compile-checked before it's written, so it
  can never break playback, and it does nothing while the setting is off.

v0.2.159
- Fix: 0.2.158 wrongly enabled POV Auto Play, which skipped the source/servers
  dialog even on the FIRST watch. Reverted Auto Play to off (one-shot), so you
  always pick the source yourself. Kept "Automatically Resume Playback = Always"
  so an in-progress item still continues from where you stopped once a source
  plays. (POV has no "auto-pick only on resume" option, so the source dialog
  shows every time -- this is the closest correct behaviour.)

v0.2.158
- "Continue Watching" in one click: enabled POV Auto Play + Always-Resume by
  default (auto_play_movie/episode = on, auto_resume_movie/episode = Always).
  Clicking an in-progress item now auto-picks a source and resumes from where
  you stopped, with no source-selection dialog. One-shot, marker-gated
  (_pov_autoplay_default_v1); a later manual change in POV settings sticks, and
  no other POV setting (Trakt/debrid/etc.) is touched.

v0.2.157
- Community pool quality gate: a translation is shared only if it looks
  complete (didn't lose blocks vs the source) and is genuinely Hebrew (not a
  failed/mostly-English result). Broken or partial translations are kept for
  the local user but NOT uploaded, so they can't pollute the pool. (Applies to
  auto-share, re-watch backfill, and the bulk "share my cache".) Note: this
  can't detect a mis-synced source -- the text is correct, only timing differs.
- Restored the remote "Back" button to STOP playback in fullscreen video
  (it had been lost when the keymap was emptied), via a minimal, safe
  FullscreenVideo-only keymap.

v0.2.156
- CRITICAL fix: after a quick update some users were left with DarkSubs
  DISABLED and therefore no subtitles / no AI translation (the post-update
  DarkSubs reload cycle could fail to re-enable it). Two guards: (1) the reload
  cycle now verifies DarkSubs actually came back enabled and retries the enable
  several times; (2) a new early-startup check re-enables DarkSubs if it's
  found installed-but-disabled -- which also recovers anyone already stuck.
  No manual re-enabling needed.

v0.2.155
- "Share my cached translations" now runs fully in the BACKGROUND: the button
  confirms, then hands off to a detached worker, so the settings UI is freed
  immediately and you can keep using Kodi. Progress shows in the bottom-right
  banner and a toast reports the result when done -- no blocking dialog and no
  waiting on a loading screen.

v0.2.154
- Community pool hardening against Telegram rate limits (for the wider
  rollout): the bulk "share my cache" run now paces uploads (~6s apart) so a
  large cache can't burst past the channel's ~20-messages/min limit. The Worker
  honours Telegram's 429 retry_after (one bounded retry) on every channel call,
  and caches each subtitle at Cloudflare's edge by file_id -- so repeated pulls
  are served from the edge and never hit Telegram's getFile limit (also much
  faster). (Worker redeploy required.) Note: this is unrelated to the
  "userbot blocked across IPs" issue -- that affects Telethon user accounts
  (like DarkSubs), not a server-side Bot API token.

v0.2.153
- Community pool is now ON by default (both pull and share). New installs get
  it via the settings defaults; existing installs are flipped on once by a
  marker-gated startup migration (a later manual opt-out sticks).
- "Share my cached translations" now has a run-lock so an accidental double
  click can't post the same file to the channel twice.
- Pool filenames: prefer the subtitle's real release name (e.g.
  "Show.2026.1080p.WEBRip.x265-GRP") when it looks like one, falling back to
  the TMDB title for tokenized stream/temp names. (Worker redeploy required.)

v0.2.152
- Community pool: "Share my cached translations now" button (under Community
  pool). One-time migration that uploads every Hebrew translation already in
  your local cache -- including ones DarkSubs serves from its own cache, which
  the automatic on-watch sharing never sees. Cached files only carry an IMDb
  id, so it resolves IMDb->TMDB (via TMDB Find) so they key exactly like live
  uploads. Safe to run repeatedly: each file is marked once shared, and the
  pool now also dedups by the Hebrew RESULT hash (not just the source hash),
  so a bulk upload can never create a second copy of a translation that was
  already shared -- whether it came from a fresh translation or another user.
  (Requires the updated Worker; redeploy pool/worker.js.)

v0.2.151
- Silence Kodi log spam: every setting now declares a <control> (toggle for
  booleans, label for the hidden status/storage strings). The hidden hook
  heartbeat settings were read/written on every DarkSubs fire without a
  control, flooding the log with "missing <control> tag" errors. No visible
  change; just a cleaner log.

v0.2.150
- CRITICAL fix: with "Use community pool" ON, every fresh (non-cached) AI
  translation crashed with "cannot access local variable 'pool'" and fell back
  to the original English ("AI translation failed"). Cause: resolve() uses a
  ThreadPoolExecutor named `pool`, which made Python treat the module-level
  `pool` import as a function-local everywhere in resolve() -- so the pool
  checks earlier in the function referenced it before assignment. Renamed the
  executor to `executor`. Translation works again with the pool enabled.

v0.2.149
- Community pool: check before uploading. The background sharer now asks the
  pool whether this exact source is already there (its /lookup lists each
  variant's source hash) and, if so, skips the upload and just marks the file
  locally. Saves sending an SRT that the server would discard, and stops a
  device re-trying when another user already shared the same source. The
  server-side dedup stays as the guarantee (defense in depth).

v0.2.148
- Community pool reliability: the ".shared" marker is now written only AFTER a
  successful upload. Before, it was set before the POST, so a transient network
  failure would mark the file as shared and it would never retry. Now a failed
  contribution simply retries on the next watch; a successful one is marked and
  never re-uploaded. (The server still dedups by source hash regardless.)

v0.2.147
- Fix (for real this time): the "Community pool" settings tab didn't appear at
  all. Its two toggles were the only settings in the category and neither had
  an explicit <control> element; Kodi 21 hides a category with no controllable
  setting. Added <control type="toggle"/> to both, matching every other toggle
  in the add-on. The tab now shows with both switches.

v0.2.146
- Fix: the "Community pool" settings category showed up empty (no toggles).
  In Kodi 21's settings format every category must wrap its settings in a
  <group>; the pool category was missing it, so "Use community pool" and
  "Share my AI translations" never rendered. Wrapped them in a group.

v0.2.145
- Community pool: automatically share translations you ALREADY have cached.
  When "Share my AI translations" is on, re-watching something you translated
  before quietly contributes that cached Hebrew to the pool (no user action,
  no re-translation). Backfills the existing local cache over time. Guarded
  against duplicates two ways: the server keys every variant by the source
  content hash and rejects repeats, and a local ".shared" marker means we
  never re-upload the same file -- not on a re-watch, not after a quick
  update. Reading the source for the content hash runs on a background thread,
  so cache hits still return instantly.

v0.2.144
- Community subtitle pool (opt-in, OFF by default; two settings under
  "Community pool"). With "Use community pool" on, the add-on checks a shared
  pool of Hebrew AI translations BEFORE spending Gemini quota: an exact source
  match (same content hash) is reused instantly with perfect sync, and any
  available variants are offered in the subtitle dialog. With "Share my AI
  translations" on, each fresh translation is contributed back (fire-and-forget
  on a daemon thread, never blocks playback). Works for any source language the
  add-on already translates, not just English. Fully gated and wrapped so it
  can't affect playback, and it respects the preferred-subtitle-language gate
  (nothing Hebrew is pulled when you've chosen a different subtitle language).

v0.2.143
- Fix: FENtastic skin loads but ALL text is blank for some users (after a quick
  update). The build-edition hebrew_build_ui_patcher rewrote the skin's Hebrew
  strings.po in place with a plain open('w'); if Kodi was force-closed mid-write
  (the quick-update flow force-closes), strings.po was left truncated, and a
  truncated strings.po blanks every Hebrew $LOCALIZE label. Now it writes
  atomically (temp file + os.replace, never a half-written live file), preserves
  the blank line between .po entries (the in-place replace was gluing entries
  together), and refuses to write a suspiciously short/empty strings.po. Same
  atomic write applied to the Home.xml / settings.xml / guisettings.xml edits.

v0.2.142
- Respect the user's preferred subtitle language. If you set Kodi's
  "preferred subtitle language" to a specific non-Hebrew language (e.g.
  English), the AI translator now stays out of the way: list_candidates
  offers nothing and resolve() hands back the source subtitle untranslated,
  so you get the language you asked for instead of a forced Hebrew
  translation. Conservative by design -- only skips when we can positively
  read a non-Hebrew preference (locale.subtitlelanguage / subtitles.languages
  via JSON-RPC); any ambiguity or read failure keeps the long-standing
  AI-Hebrew default. This is an extra gate only and does NOT change the
  DarkSubs auto_translate / force_ai_when_auto_translate_off behaviour: if
  automatic translation is off, there is still no AI translation.

v0.2.141
- Build edition only: fix Kodi hanging on the first launch after an
  install / quick update (force-stop required). The DarkSubs reload
  (darksubs_reload) disabled+re-enabled service.subtitles.All_Subs INLINE
  during our service's boot pass -- while All_Subs's own service was still
  starting -- so two autosub.py interpreters fought ("didn't stop in 5
  seconds - let's kill it") and wedged the GUI. The cycle now runs on a
  background thread that waits for the Home window to be up and nothing
  playing before toggling the addon, so All_Subs is idle and stops
  cleanly. Confirmed against the hang log (the cycle line appeared only in
  the frozen boot, never in the boot that recovered).
- Standalone AI subtitle package is unaffected (it does not ship the
  DarkSubs patchers/reload).

  then migrates existing favourites.xml paths to those new filenames on startup.
  This avoids Kodi's image cache keeping the old Real-Debrid tile artwork.

v0.2.109
- Favourites personal-tile self-heal now respects intentional user deletion.
  Once the shipped personal tiles have existed in favourites.xml, we mark
  that state. If the user later deletes those tiles manually, the AI addon
  will not re-add them on the next Kodi startup.
- The restore still works for the original build problem: when the Wizard
  overwrites favourites.xml during a skin switch, the marker is lost with
  the overwritten file, so the build can still restore missing personal
  tiles after that accidental wipe.

v0.2.105
- Replaced the TorBox-only startup status toast with a unified build-only
  premium status notifier for Real-Debrid, TorBox, Premiumize and AllDebrid.
  Each toast uses the matching POV media icon and service name as the
  notification title. Connected services are shown one after another so
  multiple accounts do not collide on screen.
- The notifier now respects POV's existing per-service "Premium Expires
  Notification (days)" settings. In this build, 0 means "show status on
  every Kodi startup"; any positive number means "show only when days left
  is less than or equal to that number" (for example 3 = last 3 days only).
  A build-only patch expands those POV sliders from 0-7 to 0-365 days and
  disables POV's generic English expiry toast to avoid duplicate notices.
- DarkSubs hook diagnostics are less misleading after Kodi restarts: the
  hook now persists its last-fire heartbeat/outcome in this addon's settings,
  not only in a volatile Window property. If the hook is correctly installed
  and configured but has not yet been exercised on this device, the "fired
  in practice" line no longer marks the whole integration as broken.

v0.2.104
- Build-only TorBox subscription startup status: if the user is connected
  to TorBox in POV, Kodi POV IL now shows a Hebrew TorBox premium status
  toast on Kodi startup with the number of days remaining. When Real-Debrid
  is connected too, TorBox waits a few seconds so the two status toasts are
  shown one after the other instead of colliding. Standalone AI-subtitle
  installs are not affected.
- Arctic Fuse 3 quick-update notice protection: AF3 now waits while the
  wizard's quick-update changelog notice is still open/unread before running
  script.skinvariables rebuild + ReloadSkin. It checks once per second for
  up to 180 seconds and continues immediately after the notice is dismissed,
  so frequent quick updates still install normally while the changelog is
  no longer closed by the Skin Variables rebuild.

v0.2.103
- RTL reverse-mode dialogue dash fix: dashed dialogue lines now move the
  leading "- " marker to the logical line end so Kodi renders the dash on
  the right side, while preserving the existing reverse punctuation behavior.
  Mixed Hebrew/English lines are now treated as RTL whenever they contain
  Hebrew anywhere in the line, so punctuation is also fixed when the final
  token is English or a number (for example: Hebrew text + Netflix.). The
  post-processor still works line-by-line and does not swap stacked subtitle
  line order. The prompt now explicitly tells the model not to swap physical
  line order inside two-line entries. Bumped the RTL cache repair marker to
  v4 so existing cached translations are reprocessed once.

v0.2.102
- Split the addon startup into managed-build mode and standalone mode.
  Standalone installs keep the AI subtitles / DarkSubs / Gemini integration,
  but no longer run build-only POV mutations that can rewrite favourites,
  navigator.db, Trakt/TMDB/local-list caches, AF3/FENtastic home nodes, or
  POV context menus. Managed Kodi POV IL build installs are auto-detected via
  the wizard, build marker, and build assets, so existing/new build users keep
  the full self-healing build behavior across wizard, APK, IPK/CoreELEC, and
  Windows installer routes. Also adds a conservative standalone cleanup that
  restores only exact known personal-area navigator.db rows written by older
  addon versions, leaving unknown/user-customized data untouched.

  cleanly reversible, and for Estuary exactly control 801 was changed with
  no other bytes touched.

- FENtastic ("simple" skin): the home SEARCH button now opens POV's

v0.2.99
- AF3 home widgets now SURVIVE updates: customizing your widget rows
  (removing "My Movies & Shows", reordering, adding your own) is no longer
  wiped on the next update. Previously we rewrote the homewidgets node
  verbatim every boot, so any user edit was overwritten. Now we 3-way
  MERGE (per the build owner's intent -- respect user edits, but still
  deliver important changes to everyone):
    * a removed widget stays removed;
    * reordering and user-added rows are preserved;
    * widgets we still ship get their fields refreshed (so future tweaks
      propagate) in the user's chosen position;
    * BRAND-NEW widgets we add in an update are appended for everyone.
  Implemented via a per-node "baseline" of what we last shipped, stored in
  our OWN addon_data (widget_baselines/) so script.skinvariables never
  parses it. On the first boot with this version the device's current
  widget layout becomes the baseline, so existing customizations are
  respected from here on. (homesubmenu/powermenu stay plain-overwrite --
  they're not user-curated lists.) Self-tested: removal persists, new
  tiles delivered, reorder/user tiles kept, field updates propagate,
  idempotent, nodes dir stays clean.

v0.2.98
- AF3 home widgets are now a clean "teaser + drill-down": each row shows
  7 posters and ends with a "More..." tile that opens the FULL category
  list. A group member rightly noted the rows should show only a handful
  as a teaser, with a button for the full list -- so a user on a weak
  device gets a fast home screen and can disable rows they don't use in
  skin settings, keeping just the 3-4 they care about.
  Two parts:
  * Widget display lowered from 20 -> 7 posters per row (widget_limit on
    every home/network widget node). Note: this is the DISPLAY count;
    POV's own page_limit (20) is unchanged per the build owner, so POV's
    build cost is the same -- the row is just a tidy 7-item teaser.
  * "More..." tile enabled: AF3 supports it natively via widget content
    browse="$VAR[Defs_BrowseLimitedLists]", which is "auto" ONLY when the
    skin setting Widgets.EnableShowMore is on (off by default, which is
    why we never saw it). With "auto", because POV returns more (20) than
    the row shows (7), Kodi appends a "More..." tile; selecting it opens
    POV's normal list view for that path -- paginated 20/page WITH a Next
    button + jump-to-letter -- so all items (e.g. all 70 Trakt-collection
    shows) are reachable, clickable into sources as usual.
  Fix: _set_af3_runtime_defaults() now sets Skin.SetBool(
  Widgets.EnableShowMore). It runs every boot when AF3 is active, so
  existing devices get it automatically; PATCH_VERSION v16 -> v17 also
  forces a clean rebuild so the change appears right away.
  (Verified against the real AF3 skin: Defs_BrowseLimitedLists at
  Includes_Defaults.xml:333-336 gates on Widgets.EnableShowMore; the
  widget <content> at Includes_Hubs.xml:121/166 consumes it.)

v0.2.97
- The "ניהול רשימות (Trakt)" context-menu item now actually opens the
  TRAKT list manager (add to Trakt collection/lists), instead of opening
  the TMDB list manager. Root cause was POV's own design, not a skin bug:
  modules/dialogs.py trakt_manager_choice() began with
    if get_setting('tmdb.account_id'): return tmdb_manager_choice(params)
  i.e. whenever a personal TMDB account is connected it deliberately
  redirected the Trakt manager to the TMDB manager (POV's comment: "TMDB
  beats Trakt -- no rate limits"). Identical on both skins. Per user
  request, pov_combined_discover_patcher gains a 4th edit (own marker) that
  removes ONLY that redirect line, so the Trakt item opens Trakt; the rest
  of the function is unchanged (still guarded by trakt_user), and the
  separate "ניהול רשימות (TMDB)" item still opens the TMDB manager for
  those who prefer it. Self-tested against real POV source: redirect gone,
  function still compiles + keeps its trakt_user guard, idempotent.

v0.2.96
- AF3: "Clear Progress" / "Mark as watched" now take effect IMMEDIATELY
  (previously the change showed only after a manual skin reload). Root
  cause: POV's clear-progress + mark-watched both funnel through
  kodi_utils.container_refresh(), which only did Container.Refresh -- that
  updates POV's own focused list but NOT AF3's home widgets, so on AF3 the
  movie stayed in "Continue Watching" until a skin reload (FENtastic's
  widgets re-run the raw POV path on Container.Refresh, hence it worked
  there). The DB delete itself always worked (verified: progress table
  emptied). Fix (pov_combined_discover_patcher, 3rd edit, own marker):
  make container_refresh() ALSO fire POV's existing widget-reload ping
  UpdateLibrary(video,special://skin/foo) -- the standard no-op
  library-scan signal AF3/TMDbHelper widgets listen for -- so home widgets
  re-query right away. Self-tested against real POV source (all 3 POV
  edits apply, compile, idempotent). (The earlier "Continue Watching shows
  only 1 movie" report is now resolved on the user's device -- the
  widget_limit=20 widget shows all in-progress movies.)

v0.2.95
- AF3 Discover is now UNIFIED: movies AND tv shows together, ranked by
  popularity. Empty (nothing typed) -> trending movies+tv mixed; typed ->
  a combined movie+tv search. Done the SAFE way (unlike the earlier
  ai_pov_combined_search attempt): reuse POV's existing mixed-media list
  builder menus/tmdb.py build_tmdb_list(), which already merges
  Movies+TVShows and sorts, rendering Hebrew posters that click straight

  missing data source to POV (exact-string, marker-gated, idempotent,
  re-applied each boot, .pyc invalidated, self-tested against real POV
  source):
  * indexers/tmdb_api.py: tmdb_search_multi(query) -> TMDB /search/multi,
    and tmdb_trending_all() -> /trending/all/week, each filtered to
    movie/tv (drops person).
  * menus/tmdb.py build_tmdb_list(): action=search_multi uses the query
    (or trending when empty); everything downstream (merge/sort/render) is
    unchanged.
  af3_discover_pov_patcher (v5_rollback -> v6_unified) repoints the
  Discover grid (Custom_1105_Search.xml onload + Includes_Search.xml
  content) at plugin://plugin.video.pov/?mode=build_tmdb_list&action=
  search_multi with the single-encoded typed query appended, so the grid
  follows the search. Existing devices on the v5 popular-movies grid
