v0.2.49
- Subtitle picker (round 3, the actually-right target): the dialog
  the user sees with the services panel + per-row flag + filename
  is Kodi's NATIVE DialogSubtitles, rendered by the ACTIVE SKIN
  (FENtastic in this build). DarkSubs is just a listed service.
  The previous two attempts targeted the wrong files. New
  skin_dialog_subtitles_row_patcher rewrites
  skin.fentastic/xml/DialogSubtitles.xml directly: bumps the
  per-row itemlayout + focusedlayout heights from 100 to 140 px
  AND bumps the inner textbox control (the $INFO[ListItem.Label2]
  release-name display) by the same +40. Two wrapped lines of
  font12 now fit cleanly without the second line getting clipped.
  Verified against the actual FENtastic XML shipped in the build:
  both itemlayout and focusedlayout rewritten, both inner Label2
  textboxes rewritten, final XML stayed well-formed (ET clean).
- Same patcher works on any skin whose DialogSubtitles.xml uses
  the standard <itemlayout height="N"> + textbox-referencing-
  Label2 idiom (matches Estuary, Arctic Zephyr variants and most
  modern skins). Skips layouts without ListItem.Label2 so it
  never touches unrelated containers. Skips layouts already >=140
  px so a skin author who already handled this gets a no-op.

- DarkSubs picker (round 2 after #157 was a no-op): the picker is
  actually a pyxbmct.AddonDialogWindow built in Python, not skin
  XML -- DarkSubs ships no resources/skins/ folder at all, so the
  XML patcher I wrote in #157 had nothing to bite into. The real
  fix lives in resources/modules/sub_window.py, where the dialog
  creates a pyxbmct.List() with the default _itemHeight=27 (one
  line). Long release names wrap to a second line that the
  27-pixel row clips. New darksubs_picker_height_patcher rewrites
  the call to pyxbmct.List(_itemHeight=60), doubling the row so
  both lines fit. Regex-anchored to the exact one-line call so it
  refuses to touch a future DarkSubs version that already passes
  arguments. Marker-gated, idempotent, .pyc cache invalidated so
  the change takes effect on next DarkSubs import without a Kodi
  restart strictly required. Verified end-to-end against the
  actual sub_window.py shipped in the build.

- DarkSubs picker: long release-name labels in each row now
  marquee-scroll horizontally instead of getting cut off mid-wrap.
  User screenshot showed entries like "Legends.2026.S01E03.This.is.
  Liverpool.720p.NF.WEB-DL..." wrapping to a second line that the
  fixed row height clipped, hiding the release group at the end --
  the part the user actually needs to identify the file. New
  darksubs_picker_label_patcher walks DarkSubs's resources/skins/
  XMLs and adds `<scroll>true</scroll>` to label controls that
  reference ListItem.Label / ListItem.Label2 (only the per-row
  labels, never the dialog header or buttons). Idempotent via
  marker, self-healing on every Kodi startup so a DarkSubs update
  can't permanently revert it. Skips files that already have
  scroll set; skips files with no matching labels (no false
  positives on misc XML).

- DarkSubs download_sub patch now matches flexibly. User report:
  a wizard-on-existing-Kodi install had a DarkSubs whose
  download_sub auto_translate elif didn't byte-match our hardcoded
  line (cosmetic differences: quote style, spacing, indentation,
  addon-handle variable name). The patch returned 'unmatched', so
  with auto_translate=OFF the AI hook never fired on a manual
  English pick and English stayed on screen. The matcher is now a
  per-line regex that captures the indentation + addon-handle var
  and reconstructs the rewrite from them -- tolerant of build
  variations, still requires EXACTLY ONE match or it bails
  (never rewrites the wrong line / a file it doesn't recognise).
- Diagnostic detail for the "won't fire" case now tells the user
  the immediate workaround: turn ON auto_translate in DarkSubs.
  The main hook (machine_translate_subs) is verified installed,
  so auto_translate=ON routes manual picks through it -> AI. No
  Google fallback because the v3 hook returns Hebrew before
  Google is ever reached.
- CRITICAL fix: Gemini's new "AQ.Ab..." API key format (rolled
  out late 2025) was being silently corrupted by our paste
  sanitizer. The allow-list regex only kept A-Z, a-z, 0-9, `_`
  and `-` -- the PERIODS in the new format were being stripped,
  so what we sent Google was `AQAb...` instead of `AQ.Ab...`,
  and Google rejected the key with a 400 "API key not valid".
  Existing users on the legacy AIza... format were unaffected
  because that format has no periods. Fix: add `.` to the
  sanitizer's allow-list. New-format keys now pass through
  intact while the iOS Safari smart-quote / NBSP / dash-variant
  protection stays in place.
- HTML form placeholder updated to show both formats so users
  aren't surprised when their new key doesn't start with AIza.

- fast_first_chunk now default ON. New users get the fast
  experience out of the box. service.py includes a one-shot
  migration (gated by _fast_first_chunk_default_v2 marker)
  that flips existing users currently on the old default 'false'
  to 'true' once. Users who later turn it off intentionally
  won't be re-flipped on subsequent startups.
- Setting label dropped "(experimental)" since the feature is
  no longer behind an opt-in flag.

- Two fixes addressing the (10/10) subtitle-stream pile-up and
  the "Kodi reverts to a pre-existing Hebrew stream" reports:
  * Alternating-slot writes during translation: chunk_ready now
    cycles between progressive_<id>_a.he.srt and ..._b.he.srt
    instead of incrementing _v1, _v2, _v3.... Each setSubtitles
    call ADDS a stream to Kodi's player list -- with N chunks
    the user saw N Hebrew entries pile up in the picker. The
    alternation caps live streams at 2 (a + b) during
    translation, plus _final after done = 3 max.
  * Explicit `setSubtitleStream(last_index)` after the canonical
    setSubtitles in the done callback. Kodi's language-preference
    auto-selection sometimes reverts to the FIRST Hebrew stream
    after we add a second one (user report: "I pick English with
    existing Hebrew present, translation finishes, picker jumps
    back to the existing Hebrew and I have to re-click English").
    Pinning the most-recently-added stream stops this. Wrapped
    in its own try/except so an old Kodi build without the API
    just no-ops.
- Cleanup pattern extended to catch BOTH the legacy _v* names
  (so users coming from #151/#152 builds get those files cleaned
  up on their next translation) AND the new _a/_b slots. The
  _final.he.srt is left alone so Kodi keeps its canonical stream.

- Robustness on the final-canonical swap (3 fixes after user
  reported "Hebrew at start, NOTHING crossing into chunk 2's
  time range" persisted even after the first canonical-swap fix):
  * Copy canonical bytes to a FRESH `progressive_<id>_final.he.srt`
    path before calling setSubtitles. Kodi caches the parsed SRT
    per-path; reusing the canonical path can no-op silently on
    some Kodi builds. The fresh _final name guarantees reload.
  * Drop the isPlayingVideo() gate -- the player may briefly
    flip False mid-`done` (user paused, end-of-chapter handoff)
    and we don't want to silently skip the swap. setSubtitles
    on a not-yet-resumed player is safe.
  * Cleanup of progressive_<id>_v*.he.srt files now happens ONLY
    after canonical swap actually succeeded. On abort/failure we
    keep the .vN files: they're the user's only Hebrew and
    deleting them strands Kodi pointing at a removed file = no
    subtitles for the rest of playback. TTL prune sweeps them
    later if we don't clean up.
- Final-canonical swap when translation completes: on success the
  `done` callback now calls Player().setSubtitles() with the
  CANONICAL Hebrew cache file -- not the last in-flight .vN. User
  report: subtitles Hebrew at start of file, but seeking 20 min
  ahead -> no subtitles at all. Root cause: each chunk_ready
  ADDS a subtitle stream in Kodi (screenshot showed "(10/10)").
  The user was stranded on the last mid-translation .vN, which
  had English placeholders for chunks completed AFTER that .vN
  was written. The final-canonical swap eliminates that window.
- Progressive .vN cleanup: on `done` (success or failure) we now
  remove all progressive_<source_id>_v*.he.srt files for this
  translation. Without it, every translation left ~5-10 stale
  files on disk for the 180-day TTL prune to handle, AND every
  setSubtitles call accumulated a subtitle stream in Kodi's UI
  ("10/10" instead of "1/1"). Now we end clean.
- Subtitle-display safety net: after our hook returns the English
  fallback to DarkSubs we now ALSO load it as the active subtitle
  via xbmc.Player().setSubtitles() + showSubtitles(True). User
  reports showed the picker closing + our toast firing but NO
  subtitle ever appearing on screen -- consistent with DarkSubs's
  caller either filtering on Hebrew chars or relying on a code
  path that doesn't fire on the fast return. We now drive Kodi's
  subtitle player directly rather than trusting the handoff. Same
  showSubtitles(True) added after each chunk_ready setSubtitles
  swap (DarkSubs + native picker paths).
- Brief 3-second poll at first_ready to wait for the player to
  flip isPlayingVideo() -> True before calling setSubtitles, so
  the call doesn't no-op when DarkSubs's hook returns slightly
  before Kodi finishes its own handoff.
- New opt-in `fast_first_chunk` setting (Advanced > default OFF).
  Only the DarkSubs auto-translate flow uses it; the regular
  subtitle picker is unchanged in this version.
- What it does: when DarkSubs hands an English SRT to translate,
  resolve() now emits a `first_ready` callback the moment the
  source is in memory (~3 seconds in). default.py uses that to
  write the English fallback to DarkSubs's output path and touch
  the `.ai_done` sentinel so DarkSubs releases immediately --
  Kodi shows English subtitles in seconds instead of 1-3 minutes.
- The same RunScript subprocess keeps translating (existing
  parallel chunked Gemini flow). As each chunk lands, resolve()
  emits a `chunk_ready` callback with a Hebrew-where-done /
  English-where-pending merged SRT; default.py writes that to a
  versioned `progressive_<id>_vN.he.srt` file in cache_dir and
  calls `xbmc.Player().setSubtitles(new_path)` to swap subtitles
  in flight. By the time the user is 1-2 minutes into the movie,
  all Hebrew has arrived.
- Quality preserved: NOT a fork of resolve(). Same chunks, same
  prompt, same model, same bisection, same final stitch. The
  callback is invoked AFTER `out_blocks_by_index[idx] =
  srt.parse_blocks(response); completed += 1` -- it only OBSERVES
  the existing translation pipeline. The canonical Hebrew bytes
  saved via `cache.save_text(translated, final)` at the end are
  byte-identical to v0.2.48 for the same source SRT. Only the
  timing of delivery differs.
- Edge cases handled: if the user stops playback / picks a
  different subtitle / reopens the dialog mid-translation, the
  `ai_subs.live_translate_active` Window(10000) property is
  cleared (or never set) so chunk_ready stops calling
  setSubtitles and stops writing .vN files. The translation
  itself still runs to completion (and lands in the canonical
  cache) so the NEXT pick of that same source shows `[CACHE]`
  and resolves instantly.
- Abort path: a partial Hebrew translation never lands in the
  canonical cache. The .vN files are left on disk for the
  existing TTL prune to clean up.
- Also wired into the native subtitle picker -- click an AI
  entry there and you get the same fast experience.
- English-source only. The fast path briefly shows the SOURCE SRT
  to the user (until Hebrew chunks land) -- that's fine for
  English (broadly readable in our user base) but disorienting
  for Spanish / Portuguese / German / French sources where the
  user can't read the placeholder text. For non-English sources
  the addon transparently falls through to the legacy slow path
  (1-3 min wait, Hebrew only). DarkSubs path detects via
  language_detect.detect() on the source bytes (the AI payload
  hardcodes source_lang='en' for prompt-robustness, so we can't
  trust the label here); native-picker path uses the configured
  source_lang in the link payload directly.

v0.2.48
- Fix cache collision: every distinct source SRT for the same
  movie/episode/source-language now gets its OWN cache slot.
  User report: "אם לחצתי על כתוביות מסוימות וזה תרגם עם ai ואז
  אני מנסה כתוביות אחרות לתרגם עם ai (שלא נמצאות עכשיו בcache)
  זה כותב לי שיש כבר כתוביות בcache". Root cause: cache._key()
  only mixed `source_id` into the digest when imdb_id was MISSING,
  so two different SRTs for the same movie collided on one cache
  filename and the second click was served the first SRT's
  translation. Now source_id is ALWAYS part of the digest.
- Cache hits are surfaced in the subtitle picker list: any AI
  entry whose translation is already cached is sorted to the top
  of the AI section (just under Hebrew passthrough, if any) AND
  labeled with `[CACHE]` so the user can spot the instant pick
  at a glance.
- Two-tier cache strategy:
   * Early lookup uses the input source identity (Wyzie URL or
     sha1 of local file content). Same key the [CACHE] marker in
     the list uses, so a list-time `[CACHE]` mark is honoured by
     resolve() without any redundant work.
   * After the SRT is in memory we ALSO check (and store at) a
     content-hash slot, catching the rare case where two
     different Wyzie URLs / local paths point to byte-identical
     SRTs (so the same translation answers both).
- Notification wording unified: "AI: כתוביות מ-cache (תרגום קודם)"
  for an early-source-id hit, "AI: כתוביות מ-cache (זהה לתרגום
  קיים)" for the content-hash hit.
- Cache TTL stays at 180 days (default unchanged). The eviction
  pass refreshes atime+mtime on every read AND every cache hit
  in resolve(), so a file that gets used regularly never ages
  out -- only genuinely abandoned translations get pruned.

v0.2.47
- v0.2.46 added RLE/PDF wrapping to force RTL paragraph direction
  in toast notifications. That correctly fixed the within-line
  character order so each Hebrew word renders properly, but a
  user screenshot showed the toast STILL appearing to read
  "backwards" -- because Kodi's notification widget SCROLLS long
  text and the scroll direction is hard-coded LTR in most skin
  XMLs. Unicode BiDi markers can't change the scroll direction;
  only the per-line character layout.
- Fix: shortened every long notification so it fits on one
  screen without scrolling at all. Kodi only scrolls text past
  ~50 visible chars; staying under that ceiling sidesteps the
  scroll-direction problem entirely.
- Three messages cropped:
    * Kickoff: 118 -> 44 chars. "AI מתרגם (כדקה-שתיים). תתעלם
      משגיאות ביניים." -- dropped the obsolete "התקדמות תופיע
      בפינה" line (replaced by 25/50/75 % milestone toasts in
      0.2.45) and the verbose "Kodi might show download failed"
      explanation (kept just the "תתעלם משגיאות" bottom line).
    * Cache hit (by IMDB): 70 -> 30 chars. "AI: תרגום מ-cache
      (כבר תורגם)". Dropped the "click Clear Cache in settings"
      pointer; that's documented in settings UI anyway.
    * Cache hit (by content hash): 83 -> 32 chars. Same
      treatment: "AI: תרגום מ-cache (זהה לסרט אחר)".
    * Missing source: 67 -> 30 chars. "מקור הכתוביות לא נמצא
      — בחר שוב".
- The RLE/PDF wrap from 0.2.46 stays in place. Combined: short
  text + forced RTL paragraph direction = the toast both
  RENDERS in correct Hebrew order AND doesn't scroll at all.
- Other toasts (Wyzie download fail, save fail, end-of-job
  success, milestone toasts, etc.) were already under 50 chars
  and unchanged.

v0.2.46
- Fix Hebrew toast notifications reading left-to-right (visually
  reversed for Hebrew readers). User report: "ההודעה של התוסף
  שקופץ במסך בזמן תרגום, הפוך. הוא מקריא מהסוף להתחלה (משמאל
  לימין)."
- Previous attempt used U+200F (RIGHT-TO-LEFT MARK) as a prefix.
  RLM is just a strong-RTL invisible character that BIASES the
  BiDi algorithm's first-strong heuristic but doesn't OVERRIDE
  the paragraph direction. When messages like "AI: 25% תורגם
  (5/20 chunks)" have more LTR character weight than RTL, RLM
  loses to first-strong from "AI:" and Kodi renders the toast
  left-to-right -- which to a Hebrew reader looks reversed.
- Switched to U+202B (RIGHT-TO-LEFT EMBEDDING) + U+202C (POP
  DIRECTIONAL FORMATTING) wrap. RLE/PDF is the proper Unicode
  mechanism to FORCE a paragraph's base direction to RTL while
  still letting embedded Latin/digit runs ("AI: 25%", "(5/20
  chunks)") read left-to-right within the RTL paragraph. The
  whole toast now scrolls and aligns from the right as a Hebrew
  reader expects, with Latin segments rendered in their natural
  LTR order inside the RTL frame.
- notify() also strips any existing BiDi-marker prefix from old
  messages before wrapping, so we don't end up double-wrapped
  if a caller (or a future re-call) re-passes an already-RTL'd
  string.

v0.2.45
- Reverted the custom WindowDialog progress overlay entirely. The
  v0.2.42-0.2.44 attempts kept breaking in successive ways:
    * v0.2.42 -- WindowDialog.show() rendered BEHIND DarkSubs's
      pyxbmct MySubs picker (z-order issue, invisible to user)
    * v0.2.42 doModal() -- finally rendered on top, but ...
    * v0.2.43 -- the card came up as a solid BLACK BOX because
      dark_bg.png * dark colorDiffuse = unreadable
    * v0.2.43 white_pixel.png fix -- card looked right, but ...
    * v0.2.44 -- percent / chunks labels never updated, stuck at
      "0% / מתחיל תרגום..." while translation proceeded silently
      in the background. Cross-thread xbmcgui control updates
      apparently don't land reliably even via Window properties
      and $INFO[] binding on this Kodi configuration.
- Stripped the _AITranslateProgressWindow class, the
  _resolve_with_progress_window helper, the doModal+worker
  architecture, the heartbeat ticker, and the white_pixel.png
  asset. _handle_download and _handle_translate_file now use the
  simple inline DialogProgressBG (bottom-right banner) + 25/50/75 %
  milestone toast notifications -- the exact pattern from v0.2.41
  that the user confirmed worked in their first screenshot.
- Toasts always layer above any window (DarkSubs MySubs, video OSD,
  Kodi standard dialogs). The user sees:
    - The existing "AI: מתחיל תרגום..." kickoff toast
    - DialogProgressBG bottom-right banner when no full-screen
      window covers it
    - 25/50/75 % milestone toasts -- guaranteed visible regardless
    - The existing "AI: תרגום הסתיים בהצלחה" completion toast
- No more fancy overlay; this is the reliable path. The "nicer
  visual window" goal will be revisited later, possibly via a
  skin XML approach instead of WindowDialog, when we have more
  bandwidth to debug Kodi's cross-thread rendering quirks.

v0.2.44
- v0.2.43 finally rendered the modal correctly (white_pixel.png as
  the colorDiffuse base fixed the all-black box). User screenshot
  confirmed: the card LOOKS right -- yellow title, navy panel,
  branded subtitle, status text. But the percent number stayed at
  "0%" and the status at "מתחיל תרגום..." for 3 minutes while
  translation actually progressed in the background. The labels
  were never updating.

- Root cause: xbmcgui control updates (setLabel, setPercent) called
  from a non-UI thread don't reliably land on screen. The doModal()
  architecture in v0.2.42 moved translate.resolve into a daemon
  worker thread; that thread's calls to window.update_progress()
  hit the controls but Kodi's UI thread, busy rendering doModal,
  apparently didn't pick the changes up.

- Fix: switch to Kodi's canonical cross-thread UI pattern --
  Window(10000) properties + $INFO[] binding in the label text.
  setProperty is documented thread-safe; the UI thread re-reads
  $INFO[Window(10000).Property(...)] on every render tick so
  updates land within ~50 ms regardless of which thread wrote them.

  Properties used:
    ai_subs.progress_pct        -- current %
    ai_subs.progress_chunks     -- "chunk X / Y" or status text
    ai_subs.progress_heartbeat  -- "מתרגם... Ns" elapsed-seconds ticker

  Labels in the modal now contain literal $INFO[...] markers that
  Kodi auto-expands; update_progress() and update_heartbeat()
  just call setProperty.

- NEW heartbeat ticker thread updates `progress_heartbeat` every
  second with the elapsed-seconds count + an animated dots glyph
  ("מתרגם... 12ש"). Critical for the Gemini-overload-retry case:
  the first chunk can take up to 230 s before completing if Google
  is rate-limiting (5 backoff steps of up to 120 s each). With the
  heartbeat, the user sees the elapsed counter advancing the whole
  time -- the modal feels alive, not frozen.

- Dropped the visible ControlProgress bar in this revision. It
  can't bind to a Window property the way ControlLabel can, so it
  would still need cross-thread setPercent() calls. The big
  percent number + chunk text + heartbeat counter convey the same
  information and DO auto-update correctly.

v0.2.43
- v0.2.42's doModal() switch fixed z-order -- the progress overlay
  finally renders on top of DarkSubs's MySubs picker. But a new
  screenshot revealed it renders as a SOLID BLACK BOX: title,
  percent, chunks counter, all invisible. The card looks broken
  rather than busy.

- Root cause: ControlImage uses colorDiffuse as a MULTIPLIER on
  the base texture. The previous code used dark_bg.png (already
  dark) as both backdrop and card, then multiplied by more dark
  colors (DD000000, F0172635). Dark * dark = black. The yellow
  title, the percent number, the chunk counter were all there but
  invisible against the unintentionally-black card.

- Fix: ship a 16x16 fully-opaque WHITE pixel PNG
  (resources/lib/icons/white_pixel.png, 79 bytes) and use it as
  the base texture for every colorDiffuse fill in the modal.
  White * navy = navy. White * yellow stripe = yellow. Colors
  finally come through.

- Bonus polish while we were already in the file:
  * Card widened 800 -> 900, repositioned to better center.
  * Added a soft shadow (4px offset, semi-opaque black) behind
    the card for depth.
  * Top accent stripe (yellow, 6px tall) marks the title area.
  * Title moved to textColor on the ControlLabel (instead of a
    [COLOR=...] BBCode wrap inside the label string), same for
    all other text controls. Cleaner; same render.
  * Progress bar widened 600 -> 640 and made taller (26 -> 32 px)
    for TV-viewing-distance visibility.
  * "Back תסגור את החלון; התרגום ממשיך ברקע" copy at the bottom
    now explicit about the early-dismiss behaviour added in v0.2.42.

v0.2.42
- v0.2.41 introduced _AITranslateProgressWindow but a user screenshot
  confirmed it STILL doesn't show on top of DarkSubs's MySubs picker.
  The previous WindowDialog.show() approach left our window as a
  non-modal child window -- pyxbmct's AddonDialogWindow is itself
  modal and captures z-order, so our overlay rendered BEHIND it
  (invisible to the user).

- Switched the progress overlay from `show()` to `doModal()` +
  worker-thread translation. doModal() forces the window above
  every other window unconditionally because it IS modal. The
  trade-off: doModal blocks the calling thread, so translate.resolve
  now runs in a daemon thread; when it finishes, the worker calls
  window.close() which un-blocks doModal and lets the helper return
  the translated path.

- User can dismiss the modal early via Back / Esc -- new onAction
  override on _AITranslateProgressWindow catches the two
  Kodi action codes (10 and 92) and calls self.close(). Translation
  continues in the background thread even after the user dismisses
  the modal; the final SRT is still written to disk so DarkSubs
  picks it up via its normal trans_file path. Early-dismiss costs
  only the visual, not the translation.

- worker.join() has a 10-minute cap (covers the worst-case Gemini
  retry chain: 5 backoff steps of up to 120s each). If translation
  somehow takes longer than that, we return None and the user falls
  through to DarkSubs's standard behaviour for an empty trans_file
  -- same as if our hook had timed out.

- The 25 / 50 / 75 % toast milestones remain in place as a backup,
  primarily for the early-dismiss case (modal closed, translation
  still running, toasts surface the ongoing progress).

v0.2.41
- v0.2.40 added DialogProgressBG to the DarkSubs-triggered path, but
  a user screenshot confirmed the dialog isn't visible in practice:
  DarkSubs's MySubs picker is a full-screen pyxbmct AddonDialogWindow
  that completely covers the bottom-right corner where
  DialogProgressBG normally appears. The dialog was being updated but
  hidden behind the picker -- user saw the start toast and then
  silence until translation finished.

- Two-layer fix:

  1. NEW WindowDialog overlay (_AITranslateProgressWindow). A
     proper full-screen centered card with dimmed backdrop, big
     title, branded "Gemini AI · Kodi POV IL" subtitle, visual
     progress bar, large percentage number, chunk counter, and
     reassuring "translation continues even if you close this
     window" copy. WindowDialog is a top-level Kodi window so it
     layers above EVERY other window -- including DarkSubs's
     pyxbmct picker. This is now the primary progress UI for
     both translation entry points.

  2. Kodi notification toasts at 25 / 50 / 75 % milestones as a
     guaranteed-visible backup. Toasts always layer above
     WindowDialog too, in the rare case some Kodi-skin combination
     hides our overlay. Throttled via a closed-over milestone dict
     so a 20-chunk file fires at most 3 progress toasts.

- Both _handle_download (direct AI Subs path) and
  _handle_translate_file (DarkSubs hook path) now route through a
  shared `_resolve_with_progress_window(link, info)` helper that
  creates the overlay, wires the progress callback, and closes
  the overlay in `finally`. Identical visual UX regardless of
  which entry point triggered the translation.

- The existing start toast ("AI: מתחיל תרגום...") and end toast
  ("AI: תרגום הסתיים בהצלחה") in translate.resolve are unchanged.

v0.2.40
- User report: didn't see ANY progress feedback when DarkSubs picks
  a subtitle and the AI translation runs. AI works (translated SRT
  appears) but during the 30-90s wait there's nothing on screen and
  the user can't tell if the hook even fired.
- Root cause: `_handle_download` (when the user picks our AI Subs
  service directly) creates a DialogProgressBG and passes its
  updater as `progress_cb` to `translate.resolve`. But
  `_handle_translate_file` (the entry point DarkSubs's hook
  RunScripts into) called `translate.resolve(link, info)` with NO
  progress_cb, so no UI update path existed. Translation ran
  silently end-to-end.
- Fix: `_handle_translate_file` now creates its own
  DialogProgressBG ("Kodi POV IL - AI Subtitles") and passes a
  matching `report` callback to `translate.resolve`. The
  background progress bar at the bottom-right of Kodi now ticks
  through "מתרגם chunk X/Y..." for the DarkSubs path too. Closes
  cleanly on completion, error, or crash via a finally block;
  if DialogProgressBG creation itself fails, translation
  proceeds silently (no regression vs. v0.2.39 behaviour).
- The existing initial "AI: מתחיל תרגום..." toast and the final
  "AI: תרגום הסתיים בהצלחה" toast are both already in place from
  earlier versions -- this PR is purely about the missing
  MID-translation progress feedback.

v0.2.39
- AI translation is now the DEFAULT whenever a Gemini API key is
  set -- no second toggle to find. Users had been hitting the
  "two-toggle confusion": one toggle in DarkSubs ("Enable machine
  translation"), one in our addon ("Translate via AI even when
  DarkSubs auto_translate is OFF"). If the second wasn't on, the
  hook never fired for users who deliberately keep DarkSubs's
  auto_translate off, and they wondered why they got Google
  output sometimes.

- Two changes that together fix the default:
  * settings.xml ships `force_ai_when_auto_translate_off=true`
    by default for new installs.
  * dark_subs_integration auto-flips the toggle ON for existing
    users (one-shot, marker-gated as _force_ai_autoenable_done).
    Users who explicitly turn the toggle OFF later are respected:
    the migration marker stays set, we never re-flip.

- DarkSubs settings.xml is also rebranded to make clear the whole
  "machine translation" section runs via Gemini AI when a key is
  connected -- prior versions only relabelled the toggle, leaving
  the section heading "תרגום מכונה" and the source dropdown "מקור
  תרגום: Google Translate" looking like Google was still in
  charge. Three label rewrites now applied (each independent,
  idempotent, surgical):
  * Section heading -> "תרגום מכונה — דרך Gemini AI (POV IL)
    כשמפתח מחובר"
  * Toggle -> "(Gemini AI)" suffix (unchanged from v0.2.x)
  * Source dropdown -> "מקור fallback (לא בשימוש כש-Gemini AI
    מחובר)"
  The dropdown VALUES (Google Translate | Bing Web | Yandex) are
  left untouched -- those describe the true fallback path that
  runs when no AI key is configured, so they remain honest.

v0.2.38
- Four targeted improvements to Hebrew gender (זכר/נקבה) accuracy
  in AI translation. Today the gender form is right ~90% of the
  time; the remaining 10% mostly traces to four specific failure
  modes that these changes address. No behavioural change for any
  non-Hebrew code path.

- (a) Cross-chunk context carry-over: we translate in parallel
  chunks (default 250 entries each). Until now, each chunk was
  translated INDEPENDENTLY: the AI saw the cast block but no
  recent conversational thread, so a character finishing a line
  at the end of chunk N would sometimes get a different gender
  the moment they spoke at the start of chunk N+1. We now feed
  the last 5 SOURCE-text lines of chunk N-1 into chunk N's
  prompt as a "PREVIOUS DIALOGUE CONTEXT" section -- AI sees the
  thread, picks up the gender consistently. The previous chunk's
  source is precomputed up-front (deterministic per index) so
  parallel dispatch still works -- no inter-chunk dependency.
  Tunable via the `prev_context_lines` setting (default 5;
  0 disables the feature).

- (b) Speaker-prefix hints: SRT entries that start with an
  ALL-CAPS speaker tag like "MABEL:" or "DR. SMITH:" used to be
  dropped on instruction ("do NOT include speaker prefixes in
  the Hebrew"). The prompt now tells the AI to MATCH the prefix
  name against the cast block, use that person's gender for
  first-person forms in the line, then drop only the prefix
  from the output. Speaker prefixes are common in SDH /
  Telegram SRTs and were previously thrown away even though
  they're the strongest gender signal we ever get.

- (c) Reply heuristic: in two-character dialogues, short
  follow-up lines ("Right.", "No way.", "You sure?", "I told
  you.") are almost always REPLIES from the OTHER speaker.
  New explicit prompt rule: "speakers alternate unless context
  says otherwise; apply alternation before falling back to a
  gender guess." Catches the most common single-line gender
  flip in dialogue.

- (d) Cast list widened from top-12 to top-25 (`tmdb_helper`
  default). Minor characters (waiters, partners, one-scene
  parents) whose gender mattered for the line they spoke were
  often outside the top-12 cut and defaulted to the AI's
  best guess. Existing cached cast lookups with fewer than
  20 entries are treated as cache misses and re-fetched on
  next translation, so users with prior watches get the
  expanded list automatically without manual cache clear.

v0.2.37
- User report: Android phone says "address not found" when opening
  the pair URL, even though the phone is on the same WiFi as Kodi
  and the user typed the URL with the port. Multiple Android devices
  fail the same way; the user's iPhone on the same network works.
  Not an HTTPS-cleartext issue (we'd see a different error) -- the
  phone genuinely can't reach the IP we displayed.

- Root cause: `get_lan_ip()` only ever returned ONE IP -- whichever
  the default route happened to bind to. On any device with multiple
  network interfaces (Android TV with WiFi + Ethernet, laptop with
  WiFi + VPN, PC with Docker bridge active) that single IP can be
  on a subnet the user's phone is NOT on. Phone tries to reach it,
  fails silently, user blames the addon.

- `gemini_pair` now enumerates ALL plausible LAN IPv4 addresses:
  * The 8.8.8.8-probe trick (default-route interface)
  * `ip -4 addr` / `ifconfig` via subprocess (works on Android,
    Linux, BSD -- catches non-default-route interfaces)
  * Per-private-range probes (sometimes routing tables bind
    different ranges to different NICs)
  * Hostname lookup as final fallback
  All results are filtered through `_is_private_lan_ip()` (RFC 1918
  + 100.64/10 CGNAT, excluding 127.x and 169.254.x) so VPN
  tunnel IPs etc. don't pollute the list.

- The pair dialog now renders every detected LAN URL. With two or
  more interfaces, the text says "מטלפון אחר ב-WiFi (נסה כל אחת עד
  שאחת תיפתח):" and lists them as bullets so the user can try each.

- Better Gemini error surfacing: when Google returns 400/403,
  `gemini.test_key()` now parses the JSON `error.message` (e.g.
  "API key not valid. Please pass a valid API key.") and includes
  it in the user-facing dialog. Previously the user only saw
  "Key rejected by Gemini (400)" with no clue WHY -- typo'd vs
  revoked vs quota vs project-not-enabled all looked identical.

- Pair dialog text now includes explicit troubleshooting for the
  two known phone-side failure modes:
  * Chrome on Android refusing HTTP: pointer to "Always use secure
    connections" toggle + Firefox/Brave alternatives.
  * iPhone 400 from Gemini after paste: compare the fingerprint
    against AI Studio's key, regenerate if it matches.

- Pair confirmation fingerprint widened from first-4/last-4 to
  first-8/last-8 chars (still hides the middle 23 of a 39-char
  Gemini key for shoulder-surf safety). Easier eyeball-compare
  against the AI Studio source.

v0.2.36
- Two regressions surfaced on the latest version: (a) iPhone users
  STILL got Google API 400 even with v0.2.34's autocorrect=off +
  NFKC + ASCII allow-list; (b) users on non-FENtastic skins (Arctic
  Zephyr was the reported case) still saw the UUID gibberish in
  the subtitle-dialog header even though the percentages were fine.

- Fix (a): iOS Smart Punctuation replaces ASCII hyphen-minus
  (U+002D) with em-dash (U+2014) for `--` and en-dash (U+2013) for
  a lone `-`. NFKC does NOT collapse these back to ASCII -- they're
  distinct Unicode codepoints. v0.2.34's allow-list regex stripped
  them entirely, so an iPhone-pasted `AIza...XYZ-ABC` arrived at
  our server as `AIza...XYZABC`, two characters short, and Google
  rejected the truncated string with 400.
  `_sanitize_key()` now coerces every dash-shaped Unicode codepoint
  back to ASCII `-` BEFORE the allow-list runs. Covers 11 variants:
  em-dash, en-dash, minus sign, unicode hyphen, non-breaking
  hyphen, figure dash, horizontal bar, hyphen bullet, full-width
  hyphen-minus, and the small-form variants. Verified against an
  end-to-end PairServer POST with em-dash corruption.

- Fix (b): the old `fentastic_dialog_subtitles_patcher` was
  hard-coded to FENtastic's XML structure. Users on Arctic Zephyr,
  Estuary, Aeon Nox etc. got `'no_file'` from it -- the dialog
  header kept reading `$INFO[Player.Filename]` (the UUID for
  TorBox URLs). The new `skin_dialog_subtitles_patcher`
  auto-detects the active skin via `xbmc.getSkinDir()`, locates
  `DialogSubtitles.xml` in the skin's `xml/`, `720p/`, or `1080i/`
  directory, and uses a regex-based match (case-insensitive on
  the `.Filename` vs `.FileName` capitalisation that varies
  across skins) to swap the label out.
  Self-migrates from the v1 FENtastic-only inject so users
  upgrading don't end up with stale duplicated controls. Verified
  end-to-end against FENtastic, Arctic Zephyr Mod, and Estuary
  XMLs -- all three parse as valid XML after patching, with the
  v2 marker and dual-visibility-gated label pair in place.

v0.2.35
- Make the port number in the Gemini pair-from-phone URL visually
  unmissable so users can manually type the right URL even when
  their phone's QR scanner truncates the displayed result.
- Background: I verified end-to-end that our QR API call already
  encodes the full URL including the port (decoded the actual
  generated QR with pyzbar) -- so the QR itself is correct. But
  Android OEM camera apps (Samsung Bixby Vision, Xiaomi MIUI
  scanner, etc.) sometimes display the URL truncated at the colon,
  so users see "http://10.0.0.5" and type that into their browser,
  missing the ":8765" suffix that we actually need them to use.
- The text below the QR now:
  * Renders the `:port` portion in bright yellow (#ffd166), the
    same accent colour as the page title -- visually impossible to
    miss.
  * Increases the body font from font13 to font14 and widens the
    text region so it doesn't wrap awkwardly.
  * The "or open one of the URLs in a browser" header is now bold
    red (#bf7f7f) and explicitly says "חובה כולל החלק הצהוב — הפורט"
    ("must include the yellow part — the port") so users who type
    the URL manually after a truncated scan know exactly which
    piece they're missing.

v0.2.34
- Fix the Gemini pair form returning a 400 from Google's API on
  every iPhone Safari paste. Symptom: pair-from-phone flow works
  perfectly on Android, Mac, Windows -- but iOS users always see
  "ה-key נדחה ע"י Gemini" right after the page accepts their key.
  The form HTML had autocomplete/autocapitalize/spellcheck off but
  was missing the iOS-only `autocorrect="off"` attribute. Without
  it, Safari quietly mutates the pasted string -- smart quotes
  around it, non-breaking spaces inserted mid-key, zero-width
  marks from text direction inference -- and Google's API
  rejects the corrupted result.
- Three layers of fix:
  * gemini_pair.py form: adds `autocorrect="off"` and
    `inputmode="verbatim"` to the API-key input. These together
    disable Safari's pasted-text rewrites and the iOS keyboard's
    word-suggestion bar.
  * gemini_pair.py server: new `_sanitize_key()` runs NFKC unicode
    normalisation then keeps only the ASCII alphanumeric +
    dash/underscore charset Gemini keys actually use. Strips out
    NBSP, ZWSP, BOM, RTL/LTR marks, smart quotes, full-width
    characters -- every byte iOS has been observed to insert.
    Sanitised against 11 known iOS-corruption patterns
    end-to-end.
  * The "key sent" confirmation page now echoes a fingerprint
    (first 4 chars … last 4 chars (length)) so the iPhone user can
    eyeball-compare against the key they copied from AI Studio.
    If sanitisation dropped the wrong thing or Safari mangled the
    inner characters somehow, the fingerprint mismatch makes it
    obvious -- no need to wait for the Gemini API call to fail.

v0.2.33
- A CoreELEC user reported: passes the diagnostic but AI still
  doesn't translate his manual subtitle picks. Reason: he keeps
  DarkSubs's auto_translate setting OFF (so DarkSubs doesn't auto-
  translate with Google) and expected AI to take over instead.
  But the hook lives inside machine_translate_subs, which DarkSubs
  only calls when auto_translate=true. With auto_translate=false
  the hook never fires -- English subs go straight to the screen.

- Two complementary changes resolve this without breaking users
  who deliberately want subs in the source language (e.g. language
  learners watching English-with-English-subs):

  * Hook bumped to v3. When AI is configured (api_key set), the
    hook NO LONGER falls back to Google on AI timeout/crash/empty
    output. Instead, on any AI failure it copies the original
    English subtitle to the output path and short-circuits -- the
    user sees the English subs on screen instead of being silently
    "rescued" by Google Translate. Google fallback is preserved
    ONLY when no AI key is configured (so non-AI users behave
    identically to upstream DarkSubs).

  * New patcher (darksubs_download_sub_patcher) widens the elif in
    download_sub so it ALSO fires when the user has explicitly
    opted in via a new toggle. The toggle lives in our addon
    settings:
        "Translate via AI even when DarkSubs auto_translate is OFF"
        (id: force_ai_when_auto_translate_off, default: false)
    With this on, picking a non-Hebrew subtitle while
    auto_translate=false routes through machine_translate_subs (and
    thus through our hook). With it off (default), behaviour is
    identical to upstream DarkSubs for users who deliberately keep
    auto_translate off.

- Bug fix: the existing hook patcher silently rewrote DarkSubs's
  engine.py from CRLF to LF on every Kodi startup. Text-mode read
  with universal newlines + text-mode write with default newline=
  was doing the conversion. Hook patcher now uses BINARY read/write
  end-to-end, preserves original line endings, and detects the EOL
  style of the source file when writing the injected hook body.

- Diagnostic checklist now has 10 rows. New rows:
  * "פאצ' download_sub פעיל" -- whether the elif rewrite is on disk.
  * "הוק AI יפעל על כתוביות לא-עבריות" -- the TRUTHFUL "does this
    setup actually translate anything?" check. Considers
    auto_translate + force_ai toggle + download_sub marker to give
    a concrete yes/no plus an explanation of which lever to flip
    if the user wants different behaviour.

v0.2.32
- 0.2.31's diagnostic surfaced a real failure mode: a CoreELEC user
  reported all 8 checks green BUT the hook still doesn't fire --
  picking an English subtitle in DarkSubs with auto_translate ON
  still routes through Google Translate, never reaches our AI.
  Root cause boils down to one of:

  * Python bytecode cache (.pyc): DarkSubs declares
    <reuselanguageinvoker>true</...> so its interpreter caches the
    OLD engine.py bytecode -- even though we wrote a new engine.py
    to disk, the running process keeps executing pre-patch code.
  * Silent fall-through inside the hook (key check returns empty
    inside DarkSubs's process, RunScript times out, hook crashes).

  Both look identical from the user's side: AI subs settings show
  the key is set, the file on disk has the marker, but Google
  Translate text appears anyway.

- Three fixes in 0.2.32:

  1. Patcher now invalidates engine's __pycache__ on every successful
     write. Wipes engine.cpython-*.pyc so Python is forced to
     re-compile from the new .py the next time DarkSubs imports it.
     Without this, reuselanguageinvoker + an existing stale .pyc
     means the patch lives on disk but never reaches RAM.

  2. Hook v2 (HOOK_VERSION = 2) is loud. Every fire writes:
       * Window(10000).Property('ai_subs.hook_last_fire')  = timestamp
       * Window(10000).Property('ai_subs.hook_last_key_len') = key len
       * Window(10000).Property('ai_subs.hook_last_outcome') =
         ok | timeout | no_key | crash:<msg>
     Plus xbmc.log lines at each branch and a visible Kodi
     Notification toast whenever the hook fires but falls back
     (timeout / no key / crash) -- so the user SEES why instead of
     just getting Google Translate output silently. Marker bumped
     to v2; v1 → v2 migration handled by the existing OLD_MARKERS
     mechanism (the patcher strips v1 block before injecting v2).

  3. Diagnostic checklist gained a 9th row: "הוק AI ירה בפועל
     מ-DarkSubs". Reads the heartbeat Window property and reports
     when the hook last fired and what the outcome was. If row 4
     (marker in engine.py) is ✓ but row 9 (heartbeat) is ✗, that's
     the smoking gun for the .pyc caching issue -- the patch is on
     disk but the hook never executed in DarkSubs's process.

v0.2.31
- Add visible diagnostics for the DarkSubs hook integration. CoreELEC
  reports surfaced that the hook can silently fail to inject (engine.py
  not writable, signature mismatch from an upstream DarkSubs update,
  etc.) and the user has no signal -- they see Google Translate output
  and assume "AI subs not working" with no idea why. Two new entry
  points fix that:

  * Automatic startup toast: surface_status_if_problem() runs once per
    Kodi launch from service.py. If the integration has an actionable
    problem (engine missing/unreadable, signature changed, read-only
    filesystem, no API key, auto_translate off), pops a Hebrew toast
    explaining the specific failure class. Only once per failure-class
    + nag-version, so it doesn't spam on every boot.

  * Manual "Test DarkSubs integration" button: new entry in the
    addon's settings (under the Gemini API category, next to "Test
    connection"). Triggers RunScript -> default.py action
    `darksubs_status` -> a textviewer dialog with an 8-point
    checklist (DarkSubs installed, engine.py reachable/readable,
    hook marker present, signature matches, file writable, API key
    set, auto_translate on). Each line is green-✓ or red-✗ with a
    one-line explanation, so when a user reports "AI subs not
    working" we get an exact failure mode instead of guessing.

- Why this matters more than any silent fix: the previous releases
  added several patchers without any way for the user to verify the
  end-to-end chain. When ONE link silently broke (read-only fs on
  CoreELEC, an upstream signature change in DarkSubs, etc.), the
  whole feature silently regressed. From 0.2.31 forward, any such
  break is immediately visible.

v0.2.30
- After v0.2.29 the dialog header still showed the UUID for TorBox
  playbacks even though the property writes were in place. Two
  root causes discovered:

  1. POV's addon.xml declares <reuselanguageinvoker>true</...> which
     means Kodi keeps the Python interpreter in memory across plugin
     invocations. Our pov_source_name_patcher writes the patched
     sources.py to disk, but POV's in-memory copy of _process() is
     the OLD pre-patch version until the user does a FULL Kodi
     restart -- quick_update + addon reload isn't enough. So on the
     first playbacks after upgrade, subs.player_filename was never
     getting written.

  2. The subtitle dialog HEADER is rendered by Kodi's
     DialogSubtitles.xml in the skin via `$INFO[Player.Filename]`,
     which Kodi computes directly from the player URL. No amount of
     Window property writes affects it -- the skin XML has to point
     at our property instead.

- Two complementary fixes:

  * fentastic_dialog_subtitles_patcher: rewrites FENtastic's
    DialogSubtitles.xml so the header label first checks
    Window(10000).Property(subs.player_filename) and only falls
    back to Player.Filename when our property is empty. Two stacked
    `<control type="label">` controls with mutually-exclusive
    `<visible>` conditions. Self-healing -- re-applies on every
    Kodi startup so a FENtastic update can't permanently revert it.

  * subs_filename_publisher: an xbmc.Player subclass living in
    our addon's service process that publishes subs.player_filename
    on every onAVStarted. Reads pov_picked_source_name first (set
    by POV's inline patch when it does apply), falls back to
    synthesising a release-style filename from VideoPlayer info-
    labels. Because our service runs in its own Python process,
    it's orthogonal to POV's reuselanguageinvoker behaviour --
    the property gets set on EVERY playback, no Kodi restart
    required.

- Net effect: TorBox playbacks now show the real release name (or
  synthesised Title.SxxExx.QUALITYp.mkv when the picked-source
  property hasn't been set yet) as the dialog header from the
  first playback after upgrade, with NO Kodi restart needed. Match
  percentages also improve since the Tagline branch of engine.py
  now has a real value to work with.

v0.2.29
- Fix the DarkSubs subtitle-dialog HEADER showing the UUID/hash for
  TorBox playbacks. v0.2.28 raised the match percentages off 0% by
  patching get_playing_filename(), but the dialog title is rendered
  from a separate code path in DarkSubs (sub_window.py uses
  `video_data['Tagline'] or video_data['file_original_path']`) and
  Tagline is read from `xbmcgui.Window(10000).getProperty(
  "subs.player_filename")`. v0.2.28 wasn't writing that property,
  so the Tagline branch was empty and DarkSubs displayed only the
  URL basename.
- v2 of pov_source_name_patcher now also writes
  `subs.player_filename` (the property DarkSubs natively reads into
  Tagline_From_Fen, intended for FEN-family scrapers to expose the
  picked release name). Setting it gives DarkSubs:
    * the real release name in the dialog HEADER (no more UUID)
    * the same name fed to the Tagline percentage branch, which
      engine.py:197 takes the max of with the file_original_path
      branch -- so matches climb from 30-50% to 70-95% routinely
- v2 of darksubs_filename_fallback_patcher clears
  `subs.player_filename` (plus pov_picked_source_name/url) when the
  URL guard fails. Prevents a stale property from a previous POV
  play leaking into a later non-POV play (e.g. the next time the
  user plays a video from a different addon).
- Migration: both patchers detect the v0.2.28 injection state on
  disk, revert it back to the original, then apply the v2 form.
  Idempotent across reboots; safe to re-apply.

v0.2.28
- Fix DarkSubs subtitle-match percentages and dialog title for all
  debrid services. Two complementary patchers:

  * pov_source_name_patcher: patches POV's sources.py so that the
    moment POV picks a source from the source-select dialog (the
    one with cached/uncached/quality flags), it stashes the picked
    item's release name and unrestricted URL in a Window(10000)
    property. Window properties cross the addon-dispatch boundary
    so DarkSubs (separate addon) can read them.

  * darksubs_filename_fallback_patcher: patches DarkSubs's
    get_playing_filename() to prefer the release name stashed by
    POV. A URL-equality guard (stored URL == currently-playing URL)
    prevents stale values from a previous POV play leaking into a
    later non-POV play. Falls back to synthesising a release-style
    filename from VideoPlayer / ListItem info-labels for the rare
    case where no POV property is available AND the URL basename
    looks like an opaque hash (TorBox CDN without our POV patch).

- Why this matters: for TorBox sources, the CDN returns URLs like
  `https://store-N.torbox.app/<uuid>?token=...` -- no filename in
  the path. Without the fix, DarkSubs sees only the UUID:
  * The dialog title shows the UUID -- the user can't visually
    compare it to subtitle release names to pick one manually
  * Token overlap with subtitle release names is 0%, so every
    subtitle shows 0% and ranking is useless
  With the fix, both the dialog title AND the percentage matching
  use the real release name (e.g.
  `War.Machine.2026.1080p.NF.WEB-DL.DDP5.1.Atmos.H.264-RYNDR.mkv`)
  that POV picked from the source list.

- Real Debrid / AllDebrid / Premiumize also benefit: even though
  their URLs do include the release filename, it may not exactly
  match the source POV picked (the CDN sometimes returns a
  different variant). Using POV's picked name gives consistent
  matching across all services. Expected match: 70-95%, depending
  on how closely the release name matches the subtitle authoring.

- Self-healing: both patchers re-apply on every Kodi startup. If
  POV or DarkSubs upstream restructures the patched function in a
  way our pattern doesn't match, we skip with a log warning -- the
  affected playback path goes back to its pre-patch behaviour but
  nothing else breaks.

v0.2.27
- Gemini "Pair from phone" flow now shows an actual scannable QR
  image instead of printing the qrserver.com URL as text. The
  previous DialogProgress-based UI only had a text body, so the
  QR URL was useless to anyone who wasn't going to manually
  type a 80-char qrserver.com URL into a browser.
- Replaced the DialogProgress with a custom WindowDialog
  (_PairWindow in default.py) that programmatically lays out:
    1. Title bar at the top
    2. Real 380x380 QR image (fetched from qrserver.com on
       first display, Kodi caches the PNG)
    3. The two URLs as text below the QR for fallback (the
       user can type them manually if their phone camera
       doesn't pick up the QR)
    4. A live countdown + Back-to-cancel hint
  Same poll loop semantics as before: 5 minute deadline, polled
  every 500ms, closes on key arrival or user cancel.
- Tiny `dark_bg.png` bundled in resources/lib/icons/ as the
  semi-opaque overlay background. 78 bytes.
- The QR encodes ps.url_lan when available (accessible both
  from other phones on the same WiFi AND from the local
  device's own browser since the pair server binds 0.0.0.0)
  or falls back to ps.url_local when LAN detection failed.

v0.2.26
- Fix a systemic bug in POV's device-flow auth (Trakt, Real Debrid,
  TorBox, Premiumize, AllDebrid) where the auth dialog would stay
  stuck on screen with the same code/URL even after the user
  authorized on the website.

  Root cause: POV's RepeatTimer.run() in myservices.py polls the
  service's auth endpoint every N seconds. The polling call is
  NOT wrapped in try/except. Any exception from the user-function
  (network timeout, SSLError, malformed JSON, KeyError on
  unexpected response structure) propagates up to threading.Timer
  which doesn't catch it -- the polling thread dies silently and
  the auth detection logic stops running entirely. The dialog
  stays up because nothing was telling it the auth completed.

  This isn't a per-service bug. Any device-flow service POV
  integrates with hits the same fragility -- single transient
  failure on poll #1 = the rest of the user's auth flow is dead.

  Fix is one try/except wrap. Successive polls keep retrying;
  eventually one returns 200 with the token and the auth gets
  captured normally.

  Delivered through a new patcher:
    addons/service.subtitles.kodipovilai/resources/lib/
      pov_repeat_timer_patcher.py
  Wired into service.py's startup hook right after
  _maybe_patch_pov_services() (both modify the same file). Marker
  AI_SUBS_REPEAT_TIMER_PATCH gates idempotency. Defensive: if
  upstream POV restructures RepeatTimer in a way our pattern
  can't match, the patcher skips with a log and POV continues
  working as upstream (auth dialogs go back to being fragile but
  nothing breaks).

  Sandbox tested end-to-end against the current shipped
  myservices.py: first run reports 'patched' and wraps the body
  correctly, re-run reports 'unchanged', py_compile of the
  patched file passes.

v0.2.25
- Smooth out the DarkSubs <-> AI integration so users don't have
  to know that DarkSubs's "Enable machine translation" toggle is
  the on/off switch for the AI hook. Three changes, all inside
  dark_subs_integration.py and run from
  service.py's _maybe_patch_darksubs() startup hook:

  (1) Auto-enable DarkSubs's auto_translate on first run.
      When the user has a Gemini key but the toggle is currently
      off, the patcher flips it to true exactly once and remembers
      having done so (marker `_darksubs_autoenable_done` in our
      addon's settings). After that we respect any subsequent
      explicit user choice -- if they later turn it off, we
      don't fight them. Skipped entirely when no Gemini key is
      set (we don't want to wake up Google Translate fallback
      for users who haven't opted in to AI).

  (2) Relabel the toggle so the AI hook is visible in DarkSubs's
      own settings UI. Patches resources/settings.xml: the
      shipped baseline
          label="הפעל תרגום מכונה"
      becomes
          label="הפעל תרגום מכונה (Gemini AI)"
      Surgical: only replaces the exact baseline string, so any
      user/upstream edit is left alone. Idempotent via a marker
      substring in the new label. Self-healing across DarkSubs
      updates (if upstream rewrites settings.xml, we re-apply
      on the next Kodi startup).

  (3) One-time toast on the auto-enable. Shows "תרגום AI הופעל
      ב-DarkSubs" so the user knows what changed without
      having to dig into DarkSubs's settings.

  None of this changes the actual translation flow -- the
  existing engine.py hook from v0.2.x still intercepts the
  call before DarkSubs would fall through to Google Translate.
  These changes just make the integration self-configuring
  instead of requiring the user to know to flip a toggle.

    "הסרטים שלי (POV)"   action=favorites_movies
