v0.2.21
- Two follow-ups to the home-tile migration after user feedback:

  (a) The TMDB home tiles created by v0.2.18+ pointed at the
      original Trakt-branded icons (the My_Shows.png / My_Movies.png
      from the Twilight build, both with a red TRAKT badge in the
      upper-right of the folder graphic). Now ships two TMDB-branded
      variants -- My_Shows_TMDB.png and My_Movies_TMDB.png -- inside
      the addon at resources/lib/media_assets/build_icons/Twilight/
      (Shows|Movies)/. A new build_icons_patcher copies any
      bundled icons that are missing from the live
      media/build_icons/ tree on every Kodi startup.
      Defensive: never overwrites pre-existing icons, so a user
      who placed a custom icon at the same path keeps it.

  (b) The favourites_xml_patcher previously REPLACED the
      Trakt-collection home tiles with TMDB-favourites tiles,
      leaving users with both services connected unable to reach
      their Trakt collection from the home screen. Now it RESTORES
      a Trakt tile right after the matching TMDB tile when the
      user has Trakt configured (POV setting trakt_user non-empty).
      Detection reads POV's settings.xml directly because POV
      isn't importable from inside our addon's process. Users
      without Trakt see only TMDB tiles -- clean home screen.

  Same patcher pass also updates any existing TMDB tile whose
  thumb still points at the old Trakt-branded icon, swapping it
  to the new My_(Shows|Movies)_TMDB.png path so the visible
  branding matches the tile name.

  All three responsibilities (migrate Trakt->TMDB, fix thumb,
  restore Trakt) happen in one read-modify-write of
  userdata/favourites.xml. Sandbox tested with two scenarios:
    * trakt_connected=True  -> 4 tiles (TMDB+Trakt for both
      mediatypes), thumbs correct, custom user tile preserved,
      idempotent on re-run
    * trakt_connected=False -> 2 tiles (only TMDB for both),
      idempotent on re-run

  service.py wires the new build_icons_patcher BEFORE
  favourites_xml_patcher so the TMDB-branded icons exist on
  disk by the time the XML starts pointing at them.

v0.2.20
- Personal-area widget header on the Movies / TV Shows pages now
  carries a visible recommendation to connect both TMDB and Trakt:
      איזור אישי   · מומלץ לחבר TMDB + Trakt
  (main label yellow bold, recommendation gray italic.)
  Rationale: TMDB handles Favorites / Watchlist / custom lists
  cleanly with no rate limits and a working mobile app; Trakt is
  the only service with a /sync/playback API that powers cross-
  device Continue Watching / Next Episode -- TMDB doesn't expose
  that. Combining the two gives the user the best of both.
- fentastic_widget_patcher upgraded to recognise BOTH legacy
  headers and migrate them to the new one:
    State A: "...איזור אישי (חובה להתחבר לTrakt)..." (very
             original baseline)
    State B: "...איזור אישי..." (what v0.2.18 wrote)
  Either matches the regex, both rewrite to State C with the
  recommendation. Re-run is unchanged (idempotent). Custom user
  headers don't match the pattern and are left alone.

v0.2.19
- Three patchers from v0.2.18 didn't take effect for at least one
  user even though the addon was delivered via quickfix and the
  service.py runs them on every Kodi startup. Visible improvement
  was the personal-area widget (which our navigator.db patcher
  worked for), but the context menu, the widget header, and the
  home tiles still showed the old layout.
- Two suspected root causes addressed:

  (a) Exact-string matching was too strict. The
      favourites_xml_patcher and fentastic_widget_patcher used
      byte-exact substring match against the lines they expected
      to find. Kodi sometimes rewrites favourites.xml after the
      user reorders tiles via the GUI, normalizing whitespace or
      attribute order in ways that broke the match. Same risk for
      the widget XML across skin updates. Both patchers now use
      regex matching that requires the SAME tile identity (visible
      name + action + mode) but tolerates whitespace, attribute
      order, quote style, and similar incidental variations.

  (b) The pov_menus_patcher detection marker was specific to
      movies.py and tvshows.py and didn't appear in episodes.py,
      so episodes.py was being needlessly re-copied on every
      Kodi startup (correct behaviour but wasteful logs). Marker
      changed to a substring that all three PR #98 versions share
      and no earlier baseline had:
        kodi_utils.get_setting('trakt_user'
      Also clarified the rewrite path: detection by marker
      presence/absence rather than byte-compare of two files.

- Every patcher now logs explicit INFO/WARNING entries to the
  Kodi log so the next "patcher didn't work" report can be
  diagnosed from the log file alone. Look for
      kodipovilai: <patcher>: <event>
  in xbmc.log -- entries cover not just successes but also
  no_match, no_file, write_failed, etc.

- Sandbox tested end-to-end:
    * First run from pre-PR-#97 baseline: all 4 patchers report
      'patched'/'fixed'
    * Idempotent re-run: all 4 patchers report 'unchanged' (no
      writes, no log spam)
    * Tolerance test: a favourites.xml with double-space between
      attributes, different thumb path, and different name= URL
      param still gets correctly migrated; a custom unrelated
      <favourite> in the same file is left untouched

v0.2.18
- Self-heal four POV/skin/userdata customizations on existing
  installs so users get the recent UX improvements without a
  full build reinstall (which would wipe their connected service
  tokens). The wizard's quickfix mechanism delivers this addon
  but doesn't touch POV/skin/userdata files; the patchers below
  bridge that gap.

  All patchers are surgical and idempotent -- they verify the
  shipped baseline byte-for-byte before rewriting, so any user
  customization aborts the patch cleanly without overwriting.

  - pov_menus_patcher (new): force-syncs POV's three context-menu
    builders (movies.py, tvshows.py, episodes.py) to the canonical
    PR #98 versions bundled in resources/lib/pov_overrides/menus/.
    Whole-file copy when bytes differ (vs the marker-inject pattern
    used by pov_services_patcher.py, because PR #98 replaces an
    existing block rather than appending one). Drops the matching
    __pycache__ entry so the next plugin invocation picks up the
    new code without waiting for Kodi to recompile.
  - pov_navigator_patcher (extended): added
    maybe_fix_personal_area_lists() which rewrites the two
    "FENtastic - <type> - איזור אישי" rows in POV's navigator.db
    to insert a TMDB Favorites entry before the existing Trakt
    Collection entry. Only rewrites rows that match the shipped
    baseline exactly. Doesn't touch the existing favourites
    typo fix.
  - fentastic_widget_patcher (new): drops the "(must connect
    to Trakt)" subtitle from the personal-area widget header in
    skin.fentastic's two widget XMLs.
  - favourites_xml_patcher (new): migrates the two Trakt
    collection home-screen tiles in userdata/favourites.xml to
    TMDB Favorites equivalents. Surgical -- if the line doesn't
    match the shipped baseline (user renamed/edited the tile),
    leaves it alone.

  service.py wires all four patchers into the startup sequence
  alongside the existing pov_services / pov_navigator /
  fentastic_notification / darksubs patchers.

v0.2.17
- Fix: clicking "Replace key" in the Gemini menu no longer wipes
  the existing key before the new one is confirmed. v0.2.16 and
  earlier called set_setting('api_key', '') the moment the user
  picked Replace, so any cancel mid-flow (closing the QR dialog,
  dismissing the on-screen keyboard, tapping outside) destroyed
  the working key with nothing to replace it. Now we leave the
  current key in place; the new one overwrites atomically when
  validation succeeds, and the original survives any cancel.
- Defensive: kodi_utils.set_setting() now reads the value back
  after writing and returns False if Kodi didn't actually
  persist the change. _test_save_or_retry uses this to surface
  a "Kodi didn't save the key" dialog instead of a false
  "Connection succeeded" dialog when the underlying
  addon().setSetting() call silently fails (reported on certain
  Kodi 21 / Android combinations). Caller-side change only --
  the set_setting signature stays backward compatible because
  the previous implementation implicitly returned None.

v0.2.16
- Add "Pair with QR (scan from another device)" button inside the
  addon's settings, next to the existing "Get a free Gemini API
  key" button. Triggers the same connect_gemini flow that the POV
  "Connect Services" menu uses, so users who install only the
  addon zip (without our build) have a working onboarding path
  that doesn't depend on the Kodi settings UI persisting a typed
  string -- some users reported the manual api_key field not
  saving on certain Kodi/Android combinations; QR pairing writes
  the key directly via setSetting after validation, bypassing
  whatever Kodi UI state was preventing the save.

v0.2.15
- Fix the home-screen Favorites tile freezing Kodi. POV ships
  userdata/addon_data/plugin.video.pov/navigator.db with the
  RootList row's Favorites entry pointing at
      mode=navigator.favourites    (UK spelling, with 'u')
  but plugin.video.pov's navigator.py defines the method as
  `def favorites(self):` (US spelling, no 'u'). POV's router
  uses getattr(cls, mode, None) and silently returns None when
  the method is missing -- endOfDirectory() never runs, Kodi
  hits its 5-second script-didn't-finish timeout, the plugin is
  killed, and the user is bounced back to the previous screen.
  Manifests as: click Favorites, Kodi freezes for ~a minute,
  ends up on the home screen.
- Two-pronged fix:
  1. New module resources/lib/pov_navigator_patcher.py opens the
     navigator.db on every Kodi startup and rewrites the bad
     'navigator.favourites' token to 'navigator.favorites' in
     the RootList row. Idempotent (re-run is a no-op),
     defensive (any error path leaves the DB alone), narrow
     (only the one substring is touched). Wired into
     service.py alongside the existing patchers.
  2. The DB shipped in the FENtastic build zip is pre-corrected,
     so future fresh installs don't carry the bug.
- The long-press "Favorites Manager" context-menu and POV's
  own dialogs.favorites_choice were never affected -- they
  call the underlying favorites_cache.Favorites.add() directly
  rather than going through the broken URL. Only the home-
  screen tile was hitting the typo.

v0.2.14
- TMDB out of the box. The addon now ships with a public TMDB v3
  key copied from jurialmunkey's script.module.tmdbhelper (an
  open-source addon that publishes the same key to tens of
  thousands of installs). With it, gender-aware translation
  works for every user the moment they connect Gemini -- no
  more "next step: connect TMDB" tutorial.
- Resolution order in tmdb_helper._get_tmdb_key():
    1. The user's own key, if tmdbhelper has one set in its
       settings (candidate fields: api_key, tmdb_apikey,
       tmdb_api_key). Personal keys still win, both for users
       who already connected and for users who connect later --
       we re-read on every lookup.
    2. The bundled fallback. Only used when no personal key
       is configured.
  We NEVER write the bundled key into tmdbhelper's settings,
  so tmdbhelper itself is untouched and behaves exactly as
  before.
- New helper tmdb_helper.using_bundled_key() exposes the state
  so the settings UI can render an accurate status line.
- The "🛈 TMDB" notice button (and Gemini-connect success
  dialog) was rewritten to reflect the new reality: TMDB is
  no longer a required setup step, just an optional override
  for power users who want to use their own quota.

v0.2.13
- Daily Gemini quota display. Free-tier gemini-3.1-flash-lite is
  capped at ~500 requests/day and resets at UTC midnight. We now
  surface today's usage in two places:
    A) appended to the end-of-translation toast --
       "AI: תרגום הסתיים בהצלחה (5 chunks · 47/500 ביום)"
    B) a new "📊 ניצול היום" entry in the POV My Services ->
       Gemini chooser menu (shown only when a key is already set).
       Opens a Dialog().ok() with model, count, percentage,
       remaining, and the reset boundary.
- Scope is intentionally narrow: ONLY gemini-3.1-flash-lite is
  counted. Other models (3.1 flash, 2.5 variants) silently
  no-op through note_request() so the displayed number stays
  meaningful. Per-device count (not synced across machines).
- Storage: two new hidden settings, _usage_count and
  _usage_date_utc. The counter is reset automatically the first
  time note_request runs after the UTC date changes.
- Hook point is inside gemini.generate() just before the success
  return -- catches every counted request regardless of caller
  (parallel translation, DarkSubs hook, future entry points).
  Wrapped in try/except at every call site so a bug in the
  quota module can never break translation.
- New runscript action `show_gemini_usage` for opening the
  status panel from anywhere (Wizard shortcut, remote, etc.).
- No new dependencies; stdlib-only datetime + the existing
  kodi_utils get/set_setting shims.

v0.2.12
- "Pair from another device" Gemini onboarding. New action
  `connect_gemini` in default.py spins up a tiny localhost HTTP
  server on a free port (8765-8769 preferred, OS-allocated
  fallback), binds 0.0.0.0 so both LAN AND localhost work, and
  shows the user a QR code + clickable URLs in a Kodi
  DialogProgress. The user scans on their phone (or types
  http://localhost:<port> if Kodi is on the phone with cellular
  only), pastes the key into a small RTL Hebrew web form, and
  hits submit. The addon polls the server's shared state for the
  submitted key, then runs the same gemini.test_key validation
  the typed-input flow uses -- if Gemini rejects the key, we
  show the specific reason and DO NOT save.
- Two onboarding paths now offered when the user clicks Gemini
  in POV's My Services with no existing key:
    📱 Pair from another device  (new)
    ⌨️ Type the key here          (the existing flow)
- For users WITH an existing key the menu stays: Test / Replace
  / Remove. Replace re-runs the chooser (Pair or Type), so
  switching keys via QR is just two clicks.
- All the Gemini UX lives in default.py now; the POV
  myservices.py patcher just shells out via
  RunScript(...action=connect_gemini) so future UX iteration
  doesn't require a patcher bump. INJECT_VERSION 5 -> 6 to roll
  the simpler injected forwarder into existing installs.
- New module resources/lib/gemini_pair.py owns the HTTP server,
  LAN-IP detection, and the form HTML. Stdlib-only (http.server
  + threading + socket), no new dependencies.
- Pair server end-to-end-tested in sandbox: bind ok, GET form
  serves RTL Hebrew + key field, POST empty key returns the
  "field empty" page without saving, POST with key returns the
  "sent to Kodi" page and stashes the key into the polling
  dict, 404 for bogus paths, shutdown frees the port cleanly.

v0.2.11
- Gemini auth flow in POV's "My Services" menu rewritten:
  * First-time setup now shows a QR code for
    aistudio.google.com/apikey, same pattern as Trakt /
    Real-Debrid / Premiumize. User scans with phone, opens
    browser, creates key, comes back to Kodi.
  * The entered key is VALIDATED against Gemini's /models
    endpoint INLINE before saving. If Gemini rejects the key
    (400/403), reports quota (429), is in a temporary outage
    (5xx), or doesn't respond (timeout) -- we DO NOT write the
    key to settings. Instead we show the specific reason and
    offer to retry. Previously the addon silently saved any
    string the user typed, leaving them "connected" with a
    broken key.
  * For users with a key already set, clicking Gemini now opens
    a chooser: 🔍 Test connection / 🔄 Replace key / ❌ Remove.
    "Test connection" makes the test button accessible from
    the same place the user authenticates -- no need to dig
    into the addon's own settings page.
  * Validation is implemented inline (direct requests call) so
    we don't have to wrestle with sys.path conflicts importing
    our resources.lib.gemini from POV's namespace.
  POV myservices INJECT_VERSION 4 -> 5 so existing patched
  installs get the new dialog flow on the next Kodi startup.

v0.2.10
- HOTFIX: revert the v0.2.9 FENtastic DialogNotification patch.
  User report: "משהו נדפק שם. עכשיו יש הודעה ריקה והבחירה של
  הכתוביות מאוד באגית, הייתי מחזיר לאיך שהיה". The wraplabel
  swap evidently broke more than it fixed -- empty notifications
  + buggy subtitle picker. service.py now calls
  fentastic_patcher.ensure_unpatched() on every startup, so any
  install that got v0.2.9 on disk gets the upstream FENtastic
  file restored automatically. The original LTR-scroll-for-
  Hebrew quirk comes back, but the user is OK with that --
  better than a broken picker.
  Note: the patcher module is kept (with the unpatch routine
  driving it now), in case a better skin-side fix is found later.

v0.2.9
- FENtastic skin: self-healing patch of DialogNotification.xml
  that swaps the message control from <fadelabel id="402">
  to <wraplabel id="402">. The fadelabel widget scrolls long
  text in a BiDi-deaf direction that reads backwards for Hebrew
  users; wraplabel wraps to multiple lines and has no scroll
  direction at all. Title (id 401) stays as fadelabel since
  the title is always "Kodi POV IL" (short, no scroll).
  - Idempotent + self-healing on every Kodi startup
  - Sanity-checks the patched XML for well-formedness before
    writing
  - If FENtastic refactors away from the canonical id=402
    fadelabel shape, the patcher bails without touching the
    file ('unmatched' status logged to kodi.log)
  - ensure_unpatched() available for users who want to revert
  - Sandbox-tested: patched + reverted XML both parse cleanly,
    idempotent on both directions
  - Only patches when FENtastic skin is installed. Users on
    Estuary / other skins are unaffected.

v0.2.8
- Default flipped to 'reverse'. User confirmed the wrong-side
  punctuation reproduces on every Hebrew subtitle for every user
  -- not just specific lines. That means Kodi's subtitle renderer
  (across all observed setups: Windows, Android, FENtastic skin)
  is NOT BiDi-reordering Hebrew lines, so the v0.2.0-v0.2.7
  "auto" direction (move punct to logical end) was producing
  visually-wrong output everywhere. The 'reverse' mode (move
  punct to logical start) introduced in v0.2.7 produces the
  correct visual placement. Made it the default; renamed 'auto'
  to 'legacy' and kept it as an option for the rare setup where
  BiDi reorder DOES happen. CACHE_RTL_FIX_VERSION bumped 2 -> 3
  so the migration re-runs over already-cached translations and
  applies the new direction on next Kodi startup. Backwards-
  compat: users who manually picked 'auto' in v0.2.7 settings
  are silently mapped to 'legacy'.

v0.2.7
- User-selectable RTL punctuation fix direction. The previous
  versions assumed Kodi does proper BiDi reorder on subtitle
  lines (logical-end Hebrew punct -> visual-left), so we moved
  the misplaced punct from line start to line end. For some
  Kodi setups -- specific skins, certain Android configurations,
  versions that render source order without RTL reorder -- the
  opposite is true: a logical-START punct ends up at visual-end
  of the reading flow. The user observed this consistently
  across all their subtitles even after the v0.2.5/v0.2.6 fixes.
  New setting `rtl_punct_mode` (Translation -> Behavior) with
  three choices: 'auto' (default, move to end), 'reverse' (move
  to start, for non-BiDi renderers), 'off' (no processing).
  Migration + cache-hit reapply respect the chosen mode.

v0.2.6
- RTL punctuation fix now runs on EVERY cache hit, not just on
  first translation. The v0.2.5 migration (re-running the fix
  over the cached-translation pool) only fires once on Kodi
  startup; users who didn't restart -- or whose cache was
  written between two migration versions -- could still see
  files with the old, broken layout. resolve() now re-applies
  fix_rtl_punctuation in-place on every cache hit (idempotent
  -- if the file is already clean, no write happens), and
  _handle_translate_file applies it once more at the final
  delivery to DarkSubs as belt-and-suspenders. This means the
  user no longer needs to restart Kodi for the fix to roll out
  -- the next time they play a previously-translated subtitle,
  it gets cleaned up on the spot.

v0.2.5
- RTL punctuation fix now handles HTML-wrapped lines. Gemini
  often emits italicised speech as "<i>.לעזאזל</i>" -- the
  previous regex couldn't see past the opening tag and left the
  period at the start. The screenshot the user kept hitting was
  exactly this case. Also covers:
    - period BEFORE the tag: ".<i>לעזאזל</i>" -> "<i>לעזאזל.</i>"
    - dialogue dash + punct + tag: "- <i>.שלום</i>" -> "- <i>שלום.</i>"
    - bold and other tag types (<b>, <font>, ...) -- any tag of
      the shape <name...> at line start.
  Ellipsis still respected (left at start as a continuation
  marker). Existing tests + 16-case unit suite all green.
- CACHE_RTL_FIX_VERSION 1 -> 2 so the migration re-runs over the
  whole cached-translation pool on next startup, applying the
  updated regex to already-saved files that were affected.

v0.2.4
- Toast notifications now scroll right-to-left as Hebrew should.
  Kodi's notification widget infers paragraph direction from the
  first strong character in the message body. Most of our
  notifications start with "AI: ..." (Latin 'A' = strong LTR),
  so the widget set paragraph direction to LTR and scrolled
  left-to-right when the text overflowed. Prepending an invisible
  RIGHT-TO-LEFT MARK (U+200F) in kodi_utils.notify() flips the
  inference to RTL. Single point of fix; applies to every notify
  call site in the addon automatically.

v0.2.3
- Display-name fix: the bundled subtitle addon's ID is
  service.subtitles.All_Subs but its visible name in the UI is
  "DarkSubs". User-facing strings now say "DarkSubs" everywhere
  so users aren't confused by an addon name they don't recognise
  in the menu. Internal Python references still use the addon ID
  (that's what xbmcaddon.Addon() and the patcher path resolution
  need).
- POV myservices INJECT_VERSION 3 -> 4 to roll the renamed
  dialog text into existing patched installs.

v0.2.2
- Wyzie is now clearly labelled as OPTIONAL across the UI. The
  setting category, field label, and help text all say so. When
  the user clicks "Claim a free Wyzie key" or opens the Wyzie
  row in POV's My Services, the dialog first checks whether
  All_Subs (DarkSubs) is installed -- if it is, the dialog tells
  the user up front that Wyzie is redundant for them (clicking a
  non-Hebrew subtitle in All_Subs already triggers AI translation
  via the hook). Users without All_Subs see the original flow.
- POV myservices INJECT_VERSION bumped 2 -> 3 to re-roll the
  Wyzie auth dialog into existing patched installs.

v0.2.1
- RTL punctuation: strip invisible BiDi / direction-control / BOM
  characters (U+200E/F, U+202A-E, U+2066-9, U+FEFF) from line
  starts and ends BEFORE the leading-punct regex runs. Gemini and
  similar LLMs occasionally insert U+200F (RLM) before a Hebrew
  line, which used to hide the trailing period from our matcher
  -- the user observed this on a freshly-translated subtitle where
  the period stayed on the wrong side despite the post-processor.
  Also: tightened the post-processor. Now also handles
  "punct at both start AND end of line" -> drop the leading
  duplicate (was previously leaving it untouched). Ellipsis at
  start of line ("..." -- a legitimate continuation marker) is
  explicitly excluded so we don't shift it to the end. 12-case
  unit suite added.
- One-shot cache repair: on next Kodi startup the service walks
  cache/translated/ and re-applies fix_rtl_punctuation() to every
  cached SRT. Marker-gated (_rtl_fix_done = '1'), so it runs once
  per profile and doesn't re-run after that. Means existing
  translations with periods/question-marks in the wrong place get
  fixed without the user having to clear cache manually.

v0.2.0
- Force the icon update through. v0.1.9 shipped the new icons
  and the corrected patcher code, but every existing install
  already had the v0.1.8 injection block in POV's myservices.py
  marked as v1, so the patcher saw 'already_patched' and bailed
  before overwriting the icon names. Bumped INJECT_VERSION to v2
  and added v1 to OLD_MARKERS, so on next startup the patcher
  strips the v1 block (with the placeholder icon references) and
  re-injects v2 (with the gemini.png / wyzie.png references).

v0.1.9
- Custom icons for the Gemini AI and Wyzie entries in POV's
  My Services menu (instead of the reused tmdb.png / mdblist.png
  placeholders that v0.1.8 shipped). 512x512 RGBA, generated to
  visually distinct: Gemini is a white four-point sparkle on a
  blue->purple gradient, Wyzie is a stylised "W" with three
  subtitle bars on a teal gradient. The pov_services_patcher now
  also copies these icons into POV's media folder whenever it
  runs (idempotent SHA1 comparison, no copy if already up to
  date).

v0.1.8
- POV "My Services" patcher. The user was clicking on
  plugin.video.pov's own Connect Services menu (entry id 32455 =
  "My Services"), not the wizard's login_menu -- different menu,
  same kind of hardcoded service list. v0.1.7 patched only the
  wizard, so the rows still didn't appear where the user expected
  them. This release adds a parallel patcher for
  plugin.video.pov/resources/lib/modules/myservices.py that
  defines Gemini and Wyzie auth classes and wraps the authorize()
  function to include them in the My Services dialog. Same
  pattern: idempotent, marker-gated, runs every Kodi startup.

v0.1.7
- Self-healing wizard patcher: the wizard's AUTOUPDATE is
  hardcoded 'No', so existing installs never pull the upstream
  wizard updates. We patch the installed wizard's loginit.py on
  disk to add the Gemini AI + Wyzie Connect Services entries.
  Idempotent + re-injects on every Kodi startup. Now users get
  the entries WITHOUT having to manually reinstall the wizard.
- Wyzie timeout resilience: tightened per-request timeout from
  15s to 8s, added one retry on connection-level failures (with
  2s delay), and short-circuit the rest of a multi-language
  search if Wyzie is clearly offline (no response on first call).
  Prevents the user staring at a 45+ second wait for "no subs
  found" when Wyzie's Cloudflare frontend is having a bad day.
- Test connection + empty-result diagnostics now distinguish
  timeout-vs-other-failure clearly and offer a workaround in
  Hebrew ("click an English sub from All_Subs - the AI hook
  will translate it").

v0.1.6
- RTL punctuation fix. Strengthened the prompt with explicit
  examples ("מה שלומך?" right, "?מה שלומך" wrong) AND added a
  defensive post-processor that automatically moves misplaced
  punctuation from the start of a Hebrew line to the end.
  Verified with 5 unit cases.
- Wyzie language-alias fallback. OpenSubtitles (which Wyzie wraps)
  serves Hebrew under three different codes: 'he', 'heb', and the
  legacy 'iw'. The search now tries all three for any Hebrew
  request and unions the results, so titles indexed under the
  legacy code finally surface. For other languages, no behaviour
  change.
- Detailed empty-result diagnostics. Instead of a generic "Wyzie
  לא מצא כתוביות" the user now sees the actual reason: 0 results
  across all three Hebrew codes (= title not in index), HTTP 401/
  403 (= key rejected), 429 (= daily quota), 5xx (= service down),
  or "no response" (= timeout/network).

v0.1.5
- Test connection button for Wyzie (mirrors the one for Gemini).
  Sanity-checks the key against the live Wyzie API and reports a
  clear Hebrew explanation if quota's exhausted, key's rejected,
  or the service is down.
- New "TMDB" group in settings with an info button explaining
  that gender-aware translation needs TMDB API key wired up via
  the wizard's "Connect Services" screen. The button checks the
  current status (TMDB Helper installed + key set) and tells the
  user what to do next.
- This addon now shows up in the wizard's "Connect Services"
  menu as two entries: "Gemini AI" and "Wyzie". Save / restore /
  display key flow matches the other services there.

v0.1.4
- CRITICAL FIX: undo the auto_translate=false state that v0.1.1
  left behind. Without this, the v0.1.3 hook never fires (DarkSubs
  only calls machine_translate_subs when auto_translate=true) and
  users who'd been through the v0.1.1 takeover ended up getting
  plain English subs on every DarkSubs pick -- no AI AND no Google.
  The restore is gated on the takeover marker, so users who had
  manually turned auto_translate off themselves (no marker) are
  untouched.

v0.1.3
- DarkSubs handshake replaced with proper routing: when the user
  has a Gemini key, DarkSubs's machine-translate now calls into
  OUR AI translator instead of Google/Bing/Yandex. No key set ->
  exactly the same Google flow as upstream (zero regression).
- Self-healing: a service.py-side patcher injects the hook into
  DarkSubs's engine.py on every Kodi startup. If a DarkSubs update
  wipes the hook, it comes back on next launch automatically. If
  DarkSubs ever renames the function, the patcher silently skips
  rather than breaking anything.
- Cache buttons (Clear cache / Clear temp) and the API / Wyzie
  action buttons now have explicit <control type="button"
  format="action"> blocks. Kodi 21 v1 settings schema needs that
  for action-type settings to actually render.

v0.1.2
- (rolled into v0.1.3 below)

v0.1.1
- One-shot DarkSubs handshake: when the user sets a Gemini key,
  flip DarkSubs's auto_translate off so its built-in Google/Bing
  Hebrew rows stop competing with (and outranking) ours. Gated
  so we never re-stomp on a user who deliberately toggled it
  back on.
- Fix: Clear-cache / Clear-temp buttons were hidden because an
  empty `label=""` on a sibling visible=false setting bailed Kodi
  21 out of rendering the rest of the group. Now label="0".
- Prompt: dedicated GENDER AGREEMENT section with concrete
  Hebrew examples for 1st/2nd/3rd person, and an explicit
  "double-check before finalising" instruction. Should reduce
  the occasional male/female mix-ups.

v0.1.0 (initial)
- AI-translated Hebrew subtitles via Gemini 3.1 Flash Lite (free tier)
- Sources from OpenSubtitles (anonymous, 200/day) + local SRT files
- Cast-aware gender forms via TMDB (script.module.tmdbhelper)
- Caches translations under userdata/addon_data with TTL + size cap
- Skips translation when real Hebrew subs already exist
- Settings UI for API key, model, source language priority, cache
